Consider the expansion of  $\sigma_{d,\tau}$  in terms of tensor product of Gegenbauer polynomials. We have

$$
\sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right), \tag {76}
$$

where

$$
\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \Big [ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \Big (\sqrt {d _ {1}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \sqrt {d _ {Q}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)} \Big) \Big ],
$$

where the expectation is taken over  $\overline{\pmb{x}} = (\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(1)},\dots ,\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(Q)})\sim \mu_{\pmb{d}}$

Lemma 9. Let  $\sigma$  be an activation function that satisfies Assumptions 2.(a) and 2.(b). Consider  $N\leq o_d(d^\gamma)$  and  $\mathcal{Q} = \mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{RF}}(\gamma)$  as defined in Theorem 6.(a). Then there exists  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  and  $d_0$  and a constant  $C > 0$  such that for  $d\geq d_0$  and  $\pmb {\tau}\in [1 - \varepsilon_0,1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$

$$
\max _ {\boldsymbol {k} \not \in \mathcal {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}) ^ {2} \leq C d ^ {- \gamma}.
$$

Proof of Lemma 9. Notice that by Assumption 2.(b) we can apply Lemma 19 to any  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}^c$  such that  $|\pmb{k}| = k_1 + \ldots + k_Q \leq L$ . In particular, there exists  $C > 0$ ,  $\varepsilon_0' > 0$  and  $d_0'$  such that for any  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}^c$  with  $|\pmb{k}| \leq L$ ,  $d \geq d_0'$  and  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_0', 1 + \varepsilon_0']^Q$ ,

$$
\left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} d ^ {\left(\xi - \eta_ {q} - \kappa_ {q}\right) k _ {q}}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}\right) ^ {2} \leq C <   \infty ,
$$

Furthermore, using that  $B(\pmb{d}, \pmb{k}) = \Theta(d_1^{k_1} d_2^{k_2} \dots d_Q^{k_Q})$ , there exists  $C' > 0$  such that for  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}^c$  with  $|\pmb{k}| \leq L$ ,

$$
\lambda_ {k} ^ {d} \left(\sigma_ {d, \tau}\right) ^ {2} \leq C ^ {\prime} \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} d ^ {\left(\eta_ {q} + \kappa_ {q} - \xi\right) k _ {q}} d _ {q} ^ {- k _ {q}} = C ^ {\prime} \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} d ^ {\left(\kappa_ {q} - \xi\right) k _ {q}} \leq C ^ {\prime} d ^ {- \gamma}, \tag {77}
$$

where we used in the last inequality  $\pmb{k} \notin \mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{RF}}(\gamma)$  implies  $(\xi - \kappa_1)k_1 + \ldots + (\xi - \kappa_Q)k_Q \geq \gamma$  by definition.

Furthermore, from Assumption 2 and Lemma 17.(b), there exists  $\varepsilon_0'' > 0$ ,  $d_0''$  and  $C < \infty$ , such that

$$
\sup  _ {d \geq d _ {0} ^ {\prime \prime}} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon_ {0} ^ {\prime \prime}, 1 + \varepsilon_ {0} ^ {\prime \prime} ] ^ {Q}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\{\langle \boldsymbol {w} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) ^ {2} \right] <   C.
$$

From the Gegenbauer decomposition (76), this implies that for any  $\pmb{k} \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$ ,  $d \leq d_{0}^{\prime \prime}$  and  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_{0}^{\prime \prime}, 1 + \varepsilon_{0}^{\prime \prime}]^{Q}$ ,

$$
B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}\right) ^ {2} \leq C.
$$

In particular, for  $|\pmb{k}| = k_1 + \ldots +k_Q > L = \max_{q\in [Q]}\lceil \gamma /\eta_q\rceil$  , we have

$$
\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {d} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}\right) ^ {2} \leq \frac {C}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})} \leq C ^ {\prime} \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} d ^ {- \eta_ {q} k _ {q}} \leq C ^ {\prime} \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} d ^ {- \gamma k _ {q} / L} \leq C ^ {\prime} d ^ {- \gamma}. \tag {78}
$$

Combining Eqs (77) and (78) yields the result.

![](images/d08df7db9bf3922f270bc439ee84aa4d4804c337f8bf75b61500c14e04ed6e86.jpg)

# E.2 Proof of Theorem 6.(a): Outline

Let  $\mathcal{Q} \equiv \mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{RF}}(\gamma)$  as defined in Theorem 6.(a) and  $\Theta = \sqrt{D}\mathbf{W}$  such that  $\pmb{\theta}_i = \sqrt{D}\pmb{w}_i \sim_{iid}$ $\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{D-1}(\sqrt{D}))$ .

Define the random vectors  $\mathbf{V} = (V_{1},\dots ,V_{N})^{\top}$ $V_{\mathcal{Q}} = (V_{1,\mathcal{Q}},\ldots ,V_{N,\mathcal{Q}})^{\top}$ $V_{\mathcal{Q}^c} = (V_{1,\mathcal{Q}^c},\dots ,V_{N,\mathcal{Q}^c})^\top$  with

$$
V _ {i, \mathcal {Q}} \equiv \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left[ \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \right] (\boldsymbol {x}) \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \right], \tag {79}
$$

$$
V _ {i, \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \equiv \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left[ \mathbb {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \right] (\boldsymbol {x}) \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \right], \tag {80}
$$

$$
V _ {i} \equiv \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \right] = V _ {i, Q} + V _ {i, Q ^ {c}}. \tag {81}
$$

Define the random matrix  $\pmb{U} = (U_{ij})_{i,j\in [N]}$ , with

$$
U _ {i j} = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \sigma (\langle \boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} \rangle / R) \sigma (\langle \boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} \rangle / R) ]. \tag {82}
$$

In what follows, we write  $R_{\mathrm{RF}}(f_d) = R_{\mathrm{RF}}(f_d, \mathbf{W}) = R_{\mathrm{RF}}(f_d, \Theta / \sqrt{D})$  for the random features risk, omitting the dependence on the weights  $\mathbf{W} = \Theta / \sqrt{D}$ . By the definition and a simple calculation, we have

$$
R _ {\mathrm {R F}} (f _ {d}) = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {a} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {N}} \left\{\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) ^ {2} ] - 2 \langle \boldsymbol {a}, \boldsymbol {V} \rangle + \langle \boldsymbol {a}, \boldsymbol {U} \boldsymbol {a} \rangle \right\} = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) ^ {2} ] - \boldsymbol {V} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V},
$$

$$
R _ {\mathrm {R F}} (\mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d}) = \min _ {\pmb {a} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {N}} \left\{\mathbb {E} _ {\pmb {x}} [ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} (\pmb {x}) ^ {2} ] - 2 \langle \pmb {a}, \pmb {V} _ {\leq \ell} \rangle + \langle \pmb {a}, \pmb {U} \pmb {a} \rangle \right\} = \mathbb {E} _ {\pmb {x}} [ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} (\pmb {x}) ^ {2} ] - \pmb {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} ^ {\mathsf {T}} \pmb {U} ^ {- 1} \pmb {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}}.
$$

By orthogonality, we have

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) ^ {2} \right] = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left[ \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \right] (\boldsymbol {x}) ^ {2} \right] + \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left[ \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \right] (\boldsymbol {x}) ^ {2} \right],
$$

which gives

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| R _ {\mathrm {R F}} \left(f _ {d}\right) - R _ {\mathrm {R F}} \left(\mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d}\right) - \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left[ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \right] (\boldsymbol {x}) ^ {2} \right] \right| \\ = \left| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} - \boldsymbol {V} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} \right| = \left| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} - \left(\boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} + \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}}\right) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \left(\boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} + \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}}\right) \right| \tag {83} \\ = \left| 2 \boldsymbol {V} ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} - \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \right| \leq 2 \| \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \| _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {V} \| _ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq 2 \| \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \| _ {2} \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} + \| \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \| _ {2} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality used the fact that

$$
0 \leq R _ {\mathrm {R F}} (f _ {d}) = \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} - \boldsymbol {V} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V},
$$

so that

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {V} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} = \boldsymbol {V} ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} \leq \left\| f _ {d} \right\| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

The Theorem follows from the following two claims

$$
\left\| \mathbf {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \right\| _ {2} / \| \mathbb {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1), \tag {84}
$$

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = O _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1), \tag {85}
$$

This is achieved by the Proposition 1 and 2 stated below.

Proposition 1 (Expected norm of  $V$ ). Let  $\sigma$  be an activation function satisfying Assumptions 2.(a) and 2.(b) for a fixed  $\gamma >0$ . Denote  $\mathcal{Q} = \mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{RF}}(\gamma)$ . Let  $\varepsilon >0$  and define  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{Q}^c,\varepsilon}$  by

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}, \varepsilon} \equiv \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\varepsilon}} [ \langle \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}, 0} f _ {d}, \sigma (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \cdot \rangle / R) \rangle_ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} ],
$$

where we recall that  $\mathbb{E}_{\pmb{\theta}_{\varepsilon}} = \mathbb{E}_{\pmb{\tau}_{\varepsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\overline{\pmb{\theta}}}$  the expectation with respect to  $\pmb{\tau}$  restricted to  $[1 - \varepsilon, 1 + \varepsilon]^{Q}$  and  $\overline{\pmb{\theta}} \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathrm{PS}^d)$ .

Then there exists a constant  $C > 0$  and  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  (depending only on the constants of Assumptions 2.(a) and 2.(b)) such that for  $d$  sufficiently large,

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}, \varepsilon_ {0}} \leq C d ^ {- \gamma} \cdot \| \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

Proposition 2 (Lower bound on the kernel matrix). Assume  $N = o_d(d^\gamma)$  for a fixed integer  $\gamma >0$ . Let  $(\pmb{\theta}_i)_{i\in [N]}\sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{D - 1}(\sqrt{D}))$  independently, and  $\sigma$  be an activation function satisfying Assumption 2.(a). Let  $\pmb{U}\in \mathbb{R}^{N\times N}$  be the kernel matrix defined by Eq. (82). Then there exists a constant  $\varepsilon >0$  that depends on the activation function  $\sigma$ , such that

$$
\lambda_ {\min } (\boldsymbol {U}) \geq \varepsilon ,
$$

with high probability as  $d \to \infty$ .

The proofs of these two propositions are provided in the next sections.

Proposition 1 shows that there exists  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  such that

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \right] = N \mathcal {E} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}, \varepsilon_ {0}} \leq C N d ^ {- \gamma} \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

Hence, by Markov's inequality, we get for any  $\varepsilon > 0$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \| _ {2} \geq \varepsilon \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}}\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\left\{\| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \| _ {2} \geq \varepsilon \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} \right\} \cap \mathcal {P} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {P} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {c}\right) \\ \leq \frac {N \mathcal {E} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c} , \varepsilon_ {0}}}{\varepsilon^ {2} \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}} + o _ {d} (1) \\ \leq C ^ {\prime} N d ^ {- \gamma} + o _ {d} (1), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used Lemma 8. By assumption, we have  $N = o_d(d^\gamma)$ , hence Eq. (84) is verified. Furthermore Eq. (85) follows simply from Proposition 2. This proves the theorem.

# E.3 Proof of Proposition 1

We will denote:

$$
\bar {f} _ {d} (\bar {\boldsymbol {x}}) = f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}),
$$

such that  $\overline{f}$  is a function on the normalized product of spheres  $\mathbf{PS}^d$  (Note that we defined  $\mathsf{P}_k f_d(\pmb{x}) \equiv \mathsf{P}_k \overline{f}_d(\overline{\pmb{x}})$  the unambiguous polynomial approximation of  $f_d$  with polynomial of degree  $\pmb{k}$ ). We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} V _ {i, \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left[ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \right] (\boldsymbol {x}) \sigma \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \langle \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} \rangle / R\right) \right] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \left[ \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \bar {f} _ {d} \right] (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {i} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We recall the expansion of  $\sigma_{d,\tau}$  in terms of tensor product of Gegenbauer polynomials

$$
\sigma (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta},\boldsymbol {x}\rangle /R) = \sum_{\boldsymbol {k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol {d},\tau})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\left(\{\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(q)}\rangle \}_{q\in [Q]}\right),
$$

$$
\lambda_ {\pmb {k}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau}}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\pmb {x}}}} \Big [ \sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau}} \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) Q _ {\pmb {k}} ^ {\pmb {d}} \Big (\sqrt {d _ {1}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \sqrt {d _ {Q}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)} \Big) \Big ].
$$

For any  $\pmb{k} \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$ , the spherical harmonics expansion of  $P_{\pmb{k}} \overline{f}_d$  gives

$$
P _ {\pmb {k}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} (\overline {{\pmb {x}}}) = \sum_ {\pmb {s} \in [ B (\pmb {d}, \pmb {k}) ]} \lambda_ {\pmb {k}, \pmb {s}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\overline {{f}} _ {d}) Y _ {\pmb {k}, \pmb {s}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\overline {{\pmb {x}}}).
$$

Using Eq. (37) to get the following property

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \right] = \frac {1}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime})} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ]} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ Y _ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \right] \tag {86} \\ = \frac {1}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \delta_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \left[ \mathrm {P} _ {\boldsymbol {k}} \bar {f} _ {d} \right] (\bar {\boldsymbol {x}}) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} \left(\left\{\langle \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right] \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime} \geq \mathbf {0}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}) \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ]} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{f}} _ {d}) \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right] \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ]} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{f}} _ {d}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}) Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}). \\ \end{array}
$$

Let  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  be a constant as specified in Lemma 9. We consider

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {E} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}, \varepsilon_ {0}} = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ [ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} ] (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right] ^ {2} \right] \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \tau_ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \left[ \mathsf {P} _ {\boldsymbol {k}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} \right] (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right] \right. \\ \times \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}} \left[ \left[ \mathsf {P} _ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}} \bar {f} _ {d} \right] (\bar {\boldsymbol {y}}) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\left\{\left. \langle \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {y}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right] \right] \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}) \right] \\ \times \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ]} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}) ]} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\bar {f} _ {d}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\bar {f} _ {d}) \mathbb {E} _ {\bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}}} \left[ Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}}) Y _ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}}) \right] \tag {87} \\ = \sum_ {\pmb {k} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \mathbb {E} _ {\pmb {\tau} _ {\hat {\varepsilon} _ {0}}} \left[ \lambda_ {\pmb {k}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau}}) ^ {2} \right] \sum_ {\pmb {s} \in [ B (\pmb {d}, \pmb {k}) ]} \lambda_ {\pmb {k}, \pmb {s}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\overline {{f}} _ {d}) ^ {2} \\ \leq \left[ \max  _ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}\right) ^ {2} \right] \right] \cdot \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ]} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\overline {{f}} _ {d}\right) ^ {2} \\ = \left[ \max _ {\pmb {k} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \mathbb {E} _ {\pmb {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \lambda_ {\pmb {k}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau}}) ^ {2} \right] \right] \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

From Lemma 9, there exists a constant  $C > 0$  such that for  $d$  sufficiently large, we have for any  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}^c$ ,

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon_ {0}, 1 + \varepsilon_ {0} ] ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}\right) ^ {2} \leq C d ^ {- \gamma}. \tag {88}
$$

Combining Eq. (87) and Eq. (88) yields

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}, \varepsilon_ {0}} \leq C d ^ {- \gamma} \cdot \| \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \bar {f} _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}}.
$$

# E.4 Proof of Proposition 2

# Step 1. Construction of the activation functions  $\hat{\sigma}$ ,  $\bar{\sigma}$ .

Without loss of generality, we will assume that  $q_{\xi} = 1$ . From Assumption 2.(b),  $\sigma$  is not a degree  $\lfloor \gamma / \eta_1 \rfloor$ -polynomial. This is equivalent to having  $m \geq \lfloor \gamma / \eta_1 \rfloor + 1$  such that  $\mu_m(\sigma) \neq 0$ . Let us denote

$$
m = \inf  \left\{k \geq \lfloor \gamma / \eta_ {1} \rfloor + 1 \mid \mu_ {m} (\sigma) \neq 0 \right\}.
$$

Recall the expansion of  $\sigma_{d,\tau}$  in terms of product of Gegenbauer polynomials

$$
\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right),
$$

where

$$
\lambda_ {k} ^ {d} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} \left(\bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) Q _ {k} ^ {d} \left(\sqrt {d _ {1}} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \sqrt {d _ {Q}} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) \right].
$$

Denoting  $\pmb{m} = (m,0,\dots,0) \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$  and using the Gegenbauer coefficients of  $\sigma_{\pmb{d},\pmb{\tau}}$ , we define an activation function  $\bar{\sigma}_{\pmb{d},\pmb{\tau}}$  which is a degree  $m$  polynomial in  $\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(1)}$  and do not depend on  $\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(q)}$  for  $q \geq 2$ .

$$
\begin{array}{l} \bar {\sigma} _ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} \left( \right.\left\{ \right.\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \left. \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \left. \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\left. \right) = \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {m}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {m}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {m}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \\ = \lambda_ {m} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}) B (d _ {1}, m) Q _ {m} ^ {(d _ {1})} \left(\sqrt {d _ {1}} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(1)}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

and an activation function

$$
\hat {\sigma} _ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left( \right.\left\{ \right.\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \left. \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \left. \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\left. \right) = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \neq \boldsymbol {m} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right).
$$

Step 2. The kernel functions  $u_{d}$ ,  $\hat{u}_{d}$  and  $\bar{u}_{d}$ .

Let  $u_{d}$ ,  $\hat{u}_{d}$  and  $\bar{u}_d$  be defined by

$$
\begin{array}{l} u _ {\boldsymbol {d}} ^ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {2}} \left( \right.\left\{ \right.\left.\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\left. \right) \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \sigma (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \sigma (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) ] \tag {89} \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geqslant 0} ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {1}}\right) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {2}}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \hat {u} _ {\boldsymbol {d}} ^ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {2}} \left( \right.\left\{ \right.\left.\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\left. \right) \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \hat {\sigma} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \hat {\sigma} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) ] \tag {90} \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \neq \boldsymbol {m} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {1}}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {2}}) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

and

$$
\begin{array}{l} \bar {u} _ {\boldsymbol {d}} ^ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {2}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \bar {\sigma} (\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R) \bar {\sigma} (\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R) ] \tag {91} \\ = \lambda_ {m} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {1}}\right) \lambda_ {m} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {2}}\right) B \left(d _ {1}, m\right) Q _ {m} ^ {\left(d _ {1}\right)} \left(\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2} ^ {(1)} \right\rangle\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

We immediately have  $u_{\pmb{d}}^{\tau_1,\tau_2} = \hat{u}_{\pmb{d}}^{\tau_1,\tau_2} + \bar{u}_{\pmb{d}}^{\tau_1,\tau_2}$ . Note that all three correspond to positive semi-definite kernels.

Step 3. Analyzing the kernel matrix.

Let  $\pmb{U},\hat{\pmb{U}},\bar{\pmb{U}}\in \mathbb{R}^{N\times N}$  with

$$
\boldsymbol {U} _ {i j} = u _ {\boldsymbol {d}} ^ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {j}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right),
$$

$$
\hat {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i j} = \hat {u} _ {\boldsymbol {d}} ^ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {j}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right),
$$

$$
\bar {U} _ {i j} = \bar {u} _ {\boldsymbol {d}} ^ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {j}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right).
$$

Since  $\hat{\pmb{U}} = \pmb {U} - \bar{\pmb{U}}\succeq 0$  , we immediately have  $\pmb {U}\succeq \bar{\pmb{U}}$  . In the following, we will lower bound  $\bar{U}$

By the decomposition of  $\bar{U}$  in terms of Gegenbauer polynomials (91), we have

$$
\bar {\boldsymbol {U}} = B (d _ {1}, m) \operatorname {d i a g} \left(\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {m}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}})\right) \cdot \boldsymbol {W} _ {m} \cdot \operatorname {d i a g} \left(\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {m}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}})\right),
$$

where  $\mathbf{W}_m\in \mathbb{R}^{N\times N}$  with  $W_{m,ij} = Q_m^{(d_1)}(\langle \overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i^{(1)},\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_j^{(1)}\rangle)$ . From Proposition 6 (recalling that by definition of  $m > \gamma /\eta_1$ , i.e.  $\gamma < m\eta_{1}$ , we have  $N < d^{\eta_1m - \delta} = d_1^{m - \delta '}$  for some  $\delta >0$ ), we have

$$
\| \mathbf {W} _ {m} - \mathbf {I} _ {N} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1).
$$

Hence we get

$$
\left\| \overline {{\boldsymbol {U}}} - B (d _ {1}, m) \operatorname {d i a g} \left(\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {m}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {i}}\right) ^ {2}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \max  _ {i \in [ N ]} \left\{B (d _ {1}, m) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {m}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {i}}\right) ^ {2} \right\} \cdot o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1). \tag {92}
$$

From Assumption 2.(a) and Lemma 20 applied to coefficient  $m$ , as well as the assumption that  $\mu_{m}(\sigma) \neq 0$ , there exists  $\varepsilon_{0} > 0$  and  $C, c > 0$  such that for  $d$  large enough,

$$
\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon_ {0}, 1 + \varepsilon_ {0} ] ^ {Q}} B \left(d _ {1}, m\right) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {m}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}\right) ^ {2} \leq C <   \infty , \tag {93}
$$

$$
\inf  _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon_ {0}, 1 + \varepsilon_ {0} ] ^ {Q}} B \left(d _ {1}, m\right) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {m}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}\right) ^ {2} \geq c > 0. \tag {93}
$$

We restrict ourselves to the event  $\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon_0}$  defined in Eq. (74), which happens with high probability (Lemma 8). Hence from Eqs. (92) and (93), we deduce that with high probability

$$
\overline {{\boldsymbol {U}}} = B (d _ {1}, m) \mathrm {d i a g} \left(\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {m}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}}\right) ^ {2}\right) + o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1) \succeq \frac {c}{2} \mathbf {I} _ {N}.
$$

We conclude that with high probability

$$
\boldsymbol {U} = \bar {\boldsymbol {U}} + \hat {\boldsymbol {U}} \succeq \overline {{\boldsymbol {U}}} \succeq \frac {c}{2} \mathbf {I} _ {N}.
$$

# F Proof of Theorem 6.(b): upper bound for RF model

# F.1 Preliminaries

Lemma 10. Let  $\sigma$  be an activation function that satisfies Assumptions 2.(a) and 2.(b). Let  $\| \pmb{w}^{(q)}\|_2 = 1$  be unit vectors of  $\mathbb{R}^{d_q}$ , for  $q = 1,\dots ,Q$ . Fix  $\gamma >0$  and denote  $\mathcal{Q} = \overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathrm{RF}}(\gamma)$ . Then there exists  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  and  $d_0$  and constants  $C,c > 0$  such that for  $d\geq d_0$  and  $\pmb {\tau}\in [1 - \varepsilon_0,1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\{\langle \boldsymbol {w} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq C <   \infty , \tag {94}
$$

$$
\min  _ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q}} \lambda_ {k, 0} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {d, \tau}\right) ^ {2} \geq c d ^ {- \gamma} > 0. \tag {95}
$$

Proof of Lemma 10. The first inequality comes simply from Assumption 2.(a) and Lemma 17.(b). For the second inequality, notice that by Assumption 2.(c) we can apply Lemma 19 to any  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}$ . Hence (using that  $\mu_k(\sigma)^2 > 0$  and we can choose  $\delta$  sufficiently small), we deduce that there exists  $c > 0$ ,  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  and  $d_0$  such that for any  $d \geq d_0$ ,  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$  and  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}$ ,

$$
\left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} d ^ {(\xi - \eta_ {q} - \kappa_ {q}) k _ {q}}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}) ^ {2} \geq c > 0.
$$

Furthermore, using that  $B(\pmb{d}, \pmb{k}) = \Theta(d_1^{k_1} d_2^{k_2} \dots d_Q^{k_Q})$ , there exists  $c' > 0$  such that for any  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}$

$$
\lambda_ {\pmb {k}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau}}) ^ {2} \geq c ^ {\prime} \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} d ^ {(\eta_ {q} + \kappa_ {q} - \xi) k _ {q}} d _ {q} ^ {k _ {q}} = c ^ {\prime} \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} d ^ {(\kappa_ {q} - \xi) k _ {q}} \geq c d ^ {- \gamma},
$$

where we used in the last inequality  $\pmb{k} \in \overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathrm{RF}}(\gamma)$  implies  $(\xi - \kappa_1)k_1 + \ldots + (\xi - \kappa_Q)k_Q \leq \gamma$  by definition.

# F.2 Properties of the limiting kernel

Similarly to the proof of [GMMM19b, Theorem 1.(b)], we construct a limiting kernel which is used as a proxy to upper bound the RF risk.

We recall the definition of  $\mathrm{PS}^{\pmb{d}} = \prod_{q\in [Q]}\mathbb{S}^{d_q - 1}(\sqrt{d_q})$  and  $\mu_{\pmb{d}} = \mathrm{Unif}(\mathrm{PS}^{\pmb{d}})$ . Let us denote  $\mathcal{L} = L^2 (\mathrm{PS}^{\pmb{d}},\mu_{\pmb{d}})$ . Fix  $\pmb {\tau}\in \mathbb{R}_{>0}^{Q}$  and recall the definition for a given  $\pmb {\theta} = (\overline{\pmb{\theta}},\pmb {\tau})$  of  $\sigma_{\pmb {d},\pmb{\tau}}(\{\langle \overline{\pmb{\theta}}^{(q)},\cdot \rangle /\sqrt{d_q}\})\in \mathcal{L}$ ,

$$
\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\pmb {\theta}}} _ {q}, \overline {{\pmb {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) = \sigma \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \tau^ {(q)} (r _ {q} / R) \langle \overline {{\pmb {\theta}}} _ {q}, \overline {{\pmb {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle\right).
$$

Define the operator  $\mathbb{T}_{\tau}:\mathcal{L}\to \mathcal{L}$ , such that for any  $g\in \mathcal{L}$

$$
\mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} g (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) g (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \right].
$$

It is easy to check that the adjoint operator  $\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{*}:\mathcal{L}\to \mathcal{L}$  verifies  $\mathbb{T}^* = \mathbb{T}$  with variables  $\overline{\pmb{x}}$  and  $\overline{\theta}$  exchanged.

We define the operator  $\mathbb{K}_{\tau,\tau'}: \mathcal{L} \to \mathcal{L}$  as  $\mathbb{K}_{\tau,\tau'} \equiv \mathbb{T}_{\tau} \mathbb{T}_{\tau'}^*$ . For  $g \in \mathcal{L}$ , we can write

$$
\mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {2}} g (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}} \left[ K _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {2}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) g (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) \right],
$$

where

$$
K _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {2}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {1}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {2}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right].
$$

We recall the decomposition of  $\sigma_{d,\tau}$  in terms of tensor product of Gegenbauer polynomials

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\{\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right), \\ \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} \left(\{\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\sqrt {d _ {q}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

Recall that  $\{Y_{\pmb{k},\pmb{s}}^{\pmb{d}}\}_{\pmb{k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q},\pmb{s}\in [B(\pmb {d},\pmb {s})]}$  forms an orthonormal basis of  $\mathcal{L}$ . From Eq. (86), we have for any  $\pmb{k}\geq 0$  and  $\pmb {s}\in [B(\pmb {d},\pmb {k})]$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\tau}}Y_{\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{s}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}) = \sum_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{\tau}})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime})\mathbb{E}_{\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}}\left[Q_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\left(\{\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(q)}\rangle \}_{q\in [Q]}\right)Y_{\boldsymbol {k},\boldsymbol{s}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\overline{\boldsymbol{x}})\right] \\ = \lambda_ {k} ^ {d} \left(\sigma_ {d, \tau}\right) Y _ {k, s} ^ {d} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {k ^ {\prime}}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \right] = \frac {\delta_ {\boldsymbol {k} , \boldsymbol {k ^ {\prime}}}}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}).
$$

The same equation holds for  $\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{*}$ . Therefore, we directly deduce that

$$
\mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) = \left(\mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}} ^ {*}\right) Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) = \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}\right) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}}\right) Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}).
$$

We deduce that  $\{Y_{\pmb{k},\pmb{s}}^{\pmb{d}}\}_{\pmb{k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q},\pmb{s}\in [B(\pmb {d},\pmb {s})]}$  is an orthonormal basis that diagonalizes the operator  $\mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau},\pmb{\tau}'}$

Let  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  be defined as in Lemma 10. We will consider  $\pmb{\tau},\pmb{\tau}' \in [1 - \varepsilon_0,1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$  and restrict ourselves to the subspace  $V_{\mathcal{Q}}^d$ . From the choice of  $\varepsilon_0$  and for  $d$  large enough, the eigenvalues  $\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{d}(\sigma_{\pmb{d},\pmb{\tau}})\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{d}(\sigma_{\pmb{d},\pmb{\tau}'})\neq 0$  for any  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}$ . Hence, the operator  $\mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau},\pmb{\tau}'}|_{V_{\mathcal{Q}}^d}$  is invertible.

# F.3 Proof of Theorem 6.(b)

Without loss of generality, let us assume that  $\{f_d\}$  are polynomials contained in  $V_{\mathcal{Q}}^{d}$ , i.e.  $\overline{f}_d = \mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{Q}}\overline{f}_d$ . Consider

$$
\hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x}; \boldsymbol {\Theta}, \boldsymbol {a}) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} a _ {i} \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right).
$$

Define  $\alpha_{\pmb{\tau}}(\overline{\pmb{\theta}})\equiv \mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau},\pmb{\tau}}^{-1}\mathbb{T}_{\pmb{\tau}}\overline{f}_d(\overline{\pmb{\theta}})$  and choose  $a_i^* = N^{-1}\alpha_{\pmb{\tau}_i}(\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i)$ , where we denoted  $\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i = (\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i^{(q)})_{q\in [Q]}$  with  $\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i^{(q)} = \pmb {\theta}_i^{(q)} / \tau_i^{(q)}\in \mathbb{S}^{d_q - 1}(\sqrt{d_q})$  and  $\tau_i^{(q)} = \| \pmb {\theta}_i^{(q)}\| _2 / \sqrt{d_q}$  independent of  $\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i^{(q)}$ .

Let  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  be defined as in Lemma 10 and consider the expectation over  $\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon_0}$  of the RF risk (in particular,  $\pmb{a}^{*} = (a_{1}^{*},\dots,a_{N}^{*})$  are well defined):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ R _ {\mathrm {R F}} (f _ {d}, \boldsymbol {\Theta}) \right] = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \inf  _ {\boldsymbol {a} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {N}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ (f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x}; \boldsymbol {\Theta}, \boldsymbol {a})) ^ {2} \right] \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x}; \boldsymbol {\Theta}, \boldsymbol {a} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {\Theta}))\right) ^ {2} \right] \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We can expand the squared loss at  $\pmb{a}^{*}$  as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x}; \boldsymbol {\Theta}, \boldsymbol {a} ^ {*})\right) ^ {2} \right] = \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} - 2 \sum_ {\substack {i = 1 \\ N}} ^ {N} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ a _ {i} ^ {*} \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] \tag{96} \\ + \sum_ {i, j = 1} ^ {N} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ a _ {i} ^ {*} a _ {j} ^ {*} \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

The second term of the expansion (96) around  $\pmb{a}^{*}$  verifies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ a _ {i} ^ {*} \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] \right] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \left[ \alpha_ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \overline {{f}} _ {d} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \right] \right] \right] \tag {97} \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\in_ {0}}} \left[ \langle \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} \bar {f} _ {d}, \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} \bar {f} _ {d} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}} \right] \\ = \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used that for each  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$ , we have  $\mathbb{T}_{\pmb{\tau}}^{*}\mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau},\pmb{\tau}}^{-1}\mathbb{T}_{\pmb{\tau}}|_{V_Q^d} = \mathbf{I}|_{V_Q^d}$ .

Let us consider the third term in the expansion (96) around  $\pmb{a}^{*}$ : the non-diagonal term verifies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \sum_ {i \neq j} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ a _ {i} ^ {*} a _ {j} ^ {*} \sigma (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \sigma (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \right] \right] \\ = \left(1 - N ^ {- 1}\right) \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {2}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}} \left[ \alpha_ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}) \alpha_ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) \right. \\ \times E _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right] \\ = \left(1 - N ^ {- 1}\right) \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {2}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}} \left[ \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}} \bar {f} _ {d} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}) \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} ^ {- 1} T _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} \bar {f} _ {d} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) \right] \\ = \left(1 - N ^ {- 1}\right) \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {2}} \left[ \langle \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}} \overline {{f}} _ {d}, \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

For  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}$  and  $\pmb{s} \in [B(\pmb{d},\pmb{k})]$  and  $\pmb{\tau}^{1}, \pmb{\tau}^{2} \in [1 - \varepsilon_{0}, 1 + \varepsilon_{0}]^{Q}$ , we have (for  $d$  large enough)

$$
\mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {1}} ^ {*} \mathbb {K} _ {\tau^ {1}, \tau^ {1}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {K} _ {\tau^ {1}, \tau^ {2}} \mathbb {K} _ {\tau^ {2}, \tau^ {2}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {2}} Y _ {k, s} ^ {d} = \left(\mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {1}} ^ {*} \mathbb {K} _ {\tau^ {1}, \tau^ {1}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {1}}\right) \cdot \left(\mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {2}} ^ {*} \mathbb {K} _ {\tau^ {2}, \tau^ {2}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {2}}\right) \cdot Y _ {k, s} ^ {d} = Y _ {k, s} ^ {d}.
$$

Hence for any  $\pmb{\tau}^1, \pmb{\tau}^2 \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$ ,  $\mathbb{T}_{\pmb{\tau}^1}^* \mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau}^1, \pmb{\tau}^1}^{-1} \mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau}^1, \pmb{\tau}^2} \mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau}^2, \pmb{\tau}^2}^{-1} \mathbb{T}_{\pmb{\tau}^2}|_{V_Q^d} = \mathbf{I}|_{V_Q^d}$ . Hence

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \sum_ {i \neq j} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ a _ {i} ^ {*} a _ {j} ^ {*} \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \right] \right] = (1 - N ^ {- 1}) \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \tag {98}
$$

The diagonal term verifies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ N ]} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(a _ {i} ^ {*}\right) ^ {2} \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) ^ {2} \right] \right] \\ = N ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \left[ \alpha_ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) ^ {2} K _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \right] \\ \leq N ^ {- 1} \left[ \max  _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon_ {0}, 1 + \varepsilon_ {0} ] ^ {Q}} K _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \right] \cdot \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ \| \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We have by definition of  $\mathbb{K}_{\tau,\tau}$

$$
\sup _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon_ {0}, 1 + \varepsilon_ {0} ] ^ {Q}} K _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) = \sup _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon_ {0}, 1 + \varepsilon_ {0} ] ^ {Q}} \| \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} \leq C,
$$

for  $d$  large enough (using Lemma 10). Furthermore

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q}} \frac {1}{\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {\tau}}) ^ {2}} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ]} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{f}} _ {d}) ^ {2} \\ \leq \left[ \max _ {\pmb {k} \in \mathcal {Q}} \frac {1}{\lambda_ {\pmb {k}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d} , \pmb {\tau}}) ^ {2}} \right] \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

From Lemma 10, we get

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \left\| \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} \bar {f} _ {d} \right\| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} \right] \leq C d ^ {\gamma} \cdot \left\| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \right\| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

Hence,

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ N ]} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(a _ {i} ^ {*}\right) ^ {2} \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) ^ {2} \right] \right] \leq C \frac {d ^ {\gamma}}{N} \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \tag {99}
$$

Combining Eq. (97), Eq. (98) and Eq. (99), we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ R _ {\mathrm {R F}} (f _ {d}, \boldsymbol {\Theta}) \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x}; \boldsymbol {\Theta}, \boldsymbol {a} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {\Theta}))\right) ^ {2} \right] \right] \\ = \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} - 2 \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} + (1 - N ^ {- 1}) \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} + N ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \left[ (\alpha_ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}})) ^ {2} K _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \right] \\ \leq C \frac {d ^ {\gamma}}{N} \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

By Markov's inequality, we get for any  $\varepsilon > 0$  and  $d$  large enough,

$$
\mathbb {P} (R _ {\mathrm {R F}} (f _ {d}, \boldsymbol {\Theta}) > \varepsilon \cdot \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}) \leq \mathbb {P} (\{R _ {\mathrm {R F}} (f _ {d}, \boldsymbol {\Theta}) > \varepsilon \cdot \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} \} \cap \mathcal {P} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}) + \mathbb {P} (\mathcal {P} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {c}) \leq C ^ {\prime} \frac {d ^ {\gamma}}{N} + \mathbb {P} (\mathcal {P} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {c}).
$$

The assumption  $N = \omega_d(d^\gamma)$  and Lemma 8 conclude the proof.

# G Proof of Theorem 7.(a): lower bound for NT model

# G.1 Preliminaries

We consider the activation function  $\sigma : \mathbb{R} \to \mathbb{R}$  with weak derivative  $\sigma'$ . Consider  $\sigma_{d,\tau}' : \mathrm{ps}^d \to \mathbb{R}$  defined as follows

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) = \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \tau^ {(q)} \cdot \left(r _ {q} / R\right) \cdot \left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}}\right) \tag {100} \\ \equiv \sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime} \left(\{\langle \pmb {\overline {{\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \pmb {\overline {{x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

Consider the expansion of  $\sigma_{d,\tau}^{\prime}$  in terms of product of Gegenbauer polynomials. We have

$$
\sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right), \tag {101}
$$

where

$$
\lambda_ {k} ^ {d} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\pmb {x}}}} \Big [ \sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime} \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) Q _ {k} ^ {d} \Big (\sqrt {d _ {1}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \sqrt {d _ {Q}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)} \Big) \Big ],
$$

where the expectation is taken over  $\overline{\pmb{x}} = (\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(1)},\dots ,\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(Q)})\sim \mu_{\pmb{d}}$

Lemma 11. Let  $\sigma$  be an activation function that satisfies Assumptions 3.(a) and 3.(b). Define for  $\pmb{k} \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$  and  $\pmb{\tau} \in \mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$ ,

$$
A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} = r _ {q} ^ {2} \cdot \left[ t _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q} - 1} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q -}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k} _ {q -}) + s _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q} + 1} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}) \right], \tag {102}
$$

with  $\pmb{k}_{q + } = (k_{1},\dots ,k_{q} + 1,\dots ,k_{Q})$  and  $\pmb{k}_{q - } = (k_{1},\dots ,k_{q} - 1,\dots ,k_{Q})$ , and

$$
s _ {d, k} = \frac {k}{2 k + d - 2}, t _ {d, k} = \frac {k + d - 2}{2 k + d - 2},
$$

with the convention  $t_{d, -1} = 0$ . Then there exists constants  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  and  $C > 0$  such that for  $d$  large enough, we have for any  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$  and  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{NT}}(\gamma)^c$ ,

$$
\frac {A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} , \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)}}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})} \leq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} C d ^ {\xi} d ^ {- \gamma - (\xi - \min  _ {q \in S (\boldsymbol {k})} \kappa_ {q})} & \text {i f k _ {q} > 0}, \\ C d ^ {\eta_ {q} + 2 \kappa_ {q} - \xi} d ^ {- \gamma - (\xi - \min  _ {q \in S (\boldsymbol {k})} \kappa_ {q})} & \text {i f k _ {q} = 0}, \end{array} \right.
$$

where we recall  $S(\pmb{k}) \subset [Q]$  is the subset of indices corresponding to the non zero integers  $k_q > 0$ .

Proof of Lemma 11. Let us fix an integer  $M$  such that  $\mathcal{Q} \subset [M]^Q$ . We will denote  $\mathcal{Q} \equiv \mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{NT}}(\gamma)$  for simplicity. Following the same proof as in Lemma 9, there exists  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$ ,  $d_0$  and  $C > 0$  such that for any  $d \geq d_0$  and  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$ , we have for any  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}^c \cap [M]^Q$ ,

$$
\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} \leq C d ^ {- \gamma - (\xi - \min _ {q \in S (\boldsymbol {k})} \kappa_ {q})},
$$

$$
\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q -}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \leq C d ^ {\xi - \kappa_ {q} - \gamma - \left(\xi - \min _ {q \in S (\boldsymbol {k})} \kappa_ {q}\right)},
$$

$$
\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} \leq C d ^ {\kappa_ {q} - \xi - \gamma - \left(\xi - \min  _ {q \in S (\boldsymbol {k})} \kappa_ {q}\right)},
$$

while for  $\pmb{k} \notin [M]^Q$ , we get

$$
\max  \{\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}, \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q -}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2}, \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} \} \leq C d ^ {- (M - 1) \min  _ {q \in [ Q ]} \eta_ {q}}.
$$

Injecting this bound in the formula (102) of  $A_{\pmb{\tau}, \pmb{k}}^{(q)}$ , we get for  $d \geq d_0$ ,  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$  and any  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}^c \cap [M]^Q$ : if  $k_q > 0$ ,

$$
\frac {A _ {\pmb {\tau} , \pmb {k}} ^ {(q)}}{B (\pmb {d} , \pmb {k})} \le C ^ {\prime} d ^ {\eta_ {q} + \kappa_ {q}} d ^ {\xi - \kappa_ {q} - \gamma - (\xi - \min _ {q \in S (\pmb {k})} \kappa_ {q})} = C ^ {\prime} d ^ {\xi} d ^ {- \gamma - (\xi - \min _ {q \in S (\pmb {k})} \kappa_ {q})},
$$

while for  $k_{q} = 0$

$$
\frac {A _ {\pmb {\tau} , \pmb {k}} ^ {(q)}}{B (\pmb {d} , \pmb {k})} \le C ^ {\prime} d ^ {\eta_ {q} + \kappa_ {q}} d ^ {- \eta_ {q}} d ^ {\kappa_ {q} + \eta_ {q} - \xi - \gamma - (\xi - \min _ {q \in S (\pmb {k})} \kappa_ {q})} = C ^ {\prime} d ^ {\eta_ {q} + 2 \kappa_ {q} - \xi} d ^ {- \gamma - (\xi - \min _ {q \in S (\pmb {k})} \kappa_ {q})},
$$

where we used that for  $k_q \in [M]$ , there exists a constant  $c > 0$  such that  $s_{d_q, k_q} \leq cd^{-\eta_q}$  and  $t_{d_q, k_q} \leq c$ . Similarly, we get for  $\pmb{k} \notin [M]^Q$

$$
\frac {A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} , \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)}}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})} \leq C ^ {\prime \prime} d ^ {\kappa_ {q} + \eta_ {q} - (M - 1) \min  _ {q \in [ Q ]} \eta_ {q}},
$$

where we used that  $s_{d_q,k}, t_{d_q,k} \leq 1$  for any  $k \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$ . Taking  $M$  sufficiently large yields the result.

![](images/c4ad68c42b2651427c19742abc5ae8e68b5f110f6e1aac683c281e20827c958b.jpg)

# G.2 Proof of Theorem 7.(a): Outline

The structure of the proof for the NT model is the same as for the RF case, however some parts of the proof requires more work.

We define the random vector  $\mathbf{V} = (\mathbf{V}_1, \ldots, \mathbf{V}_N)^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{Nd}$ , where, for each  $j \leq N$ ,  $\mathbf{V}_j \in \mathbb{R}^D$ , and analogously  $\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{Q}} = (\mathbf{V}_{1,\mathcal{Q}}, \ldots, \mathbf{V}_{N,\mathcal{Q}})^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{ND}$ ,  $\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{Q}^c} = (\mathbf{V}_{1,\mathcal{Q}^c}, \ldots, \mathbf{V}_{N,\mathcal{Q}^c})^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{ND}$ , as follows

$$
\boldsymbol {V} _ {i, Q} = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left[ \mathrm {P} _ {Q} f _ {d} \right] (\boldsymbol {x}) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \boldsymbol {x} \right],
$$

$$
\boldsymbol {V} _ {i, \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left[ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \right] (\boldsymbol {x}) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \boldsymbol {x} \right],
$$

$$
\boldsymbol {V} _ {i} = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \boldsymbol {x} \right] = \boldsymbol {V} _ {i, \mathcal {Q}} + \boldsymbol {V} _ {i, \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}}.
$$

We define the random matrix  $\mathbf{U} = (\mathbf{U}_{ij})_{i,j\in [N]}\in \mathbb{R}^{ND\times ND}$ , where for each  $i,j\leq N$ ,  $\mathbf{U}_{ij}\in \mathbb{R}^{D\times D}$ , is given by

$$
\boldsymbol {U} _ {i j} = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} \right\rangle / R\right) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} \right\rangle / R\right) \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \right]. \tag {103}
$$

Proceeding as for the RF model, we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| R _ {\mathrm {N T}} \left(f _ {d}\right) - R _ {\mathrm {N T}} \left(\mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d}\right) - \left\| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \right\| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} \right| \\ = \left| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} - \boldsymbol {V} ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} \right| = \left| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} - (\boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} + \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}}) ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} (\boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q}} + \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}}) \right| \\ = \left| 2 \boldsymbol {V} ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} - \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \right| \\ \leq 2 \| \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \| _ {2} \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} + \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We claim that we have

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1 / 2} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} = \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\left\| \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \right\| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}\right). \tag {104}
$$

To show this result, we will need the following two propositions.

Proposition 3 (Expected norm of  $\mathbf{V}$ ). Let  $\sigma$  be a weakly differentiable activation function with weak derivative  $\sigma'$  and  $\mathcal{Q} \subset \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^Q$ . Let  $\varepsilon > 0$  and define  $\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{Q}^c, \varepsilon}^{(q)}$  by

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}, \varepsilon} ^ {(q)} \equiv \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\varepsilon}} \left[ \langle \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ [ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} ] (\boldsymbol {x}) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} ], \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ [ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} ] (\boldsymbol {x}) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} ] \rangle \right],
$$

where the expectation is taken with respect to  $\pmb{x} = (\pmb{x}^{(1)},\dots,\pmb{x}^{(Q)})\sim \mu_{\pmb{d}}^{\kappa}$ . Then,

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}, \varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {(q)} \leq \left[ \max  _ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} ] \right] \cdot \| \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

Proposition 4 (Lower bound on the kernel matrix). Let  $N = o_d(d^\gamma)$  for some  $\gamma >0$ , and  $(\pmb{\theta}_i)_{i\in [N]}\sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{D - 1}(\sqrt{D}))$  independently. Let  $\sigma$  be an activation that satisfies Assumptions 3.(a) and 3.(b). Let  $\pmb {U}\in \mathbb{R}^{ND\times ND}$  be the kernel matrix with  $i,j$  block  $\pmb{U}_{ij}\in \mathbb{R}^{D\times D}$  defined by Eq. (103). Then there exists two matrices  $\pmb{D}$  and  $\pmb{\Delta}$  such that

$$
\boldsymbol {U} \succeq \boldsymbol {D} + \boldsymbol {\Delta},
$$

with  $\pmb{D} = \mathrm{diag}(D_{ii})$  block diagonal. Furthermore,  $\pmb{D}$  and  $\pmb{\Delta}$  verifies the following properties:

(a)  $\| \pmb{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d^{-\max_{q\in [Q]}\kappa_q})$  
(b) For each  $i \in [N]$ , we can decompose the matrix  $D_{ii}$  into block matrix form  $(\pmb{D}_{ii}^{qq'})_{q,q' \in [Q]} \in \mathbb{R}^{DN \times DN}$  with  $\pmb{D}_{ii}^{qq'} \in \mathbb{R}^{d_qN \times d_{q'}N}$  such that

- For any  $q \in [Q]$ , there exists constants  $c_{q}, C_{q} > 0$  such that we have with high probability

$$
0 <   c _ {q} \frac {r _ {q} ^ {2}}{d _ {q}} = c _ {q} d ^ {\kappa_ {q}} \leq \min  _ {i \in [ N ]} \lambda_ {\min } \left(\boldsymbol {D} _ {i i} ^ {q q}\right) \leq \max  _ {i \in [ N ]} \lambda_ {\max } \left(\boldsymbol {D} _ {i i} ^ {q q}\right) \leq C _ {q} \frac {r _ {q} ^ {2}}{d _ {q}} = C _ {q} d ^ {\kappa_ {q}} <   \infty , \tag {105}
$$

as  $d\to \infty$

- For any  $q \neq q' \in [Q]$ , we have

$$
\max  _ {i \in [ N ]} \sigma_ {\max } \left(\boldsymbol {D} _ {i i} ^ {q q ^ {\prime}}\right) = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(r _ {q} r _ {q ^ {\prime}} / \sqrt {d _ {q} d _ {q ^ {\prime}}}\right). \tag {106}
$$

The proofs of these two propositions are provided in the next sections.

From Proposition 4, we can upper bound Eq. (104) as follows

$$
\boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {U} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \preceq \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {D} + \boldsymbol {\Delta}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} = \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {D} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} - \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {D} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\Delta} (\boldsymbol {D} + \boldsymbol {\Delta}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}}. \tag {107}
$$

Let us fix  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  as prescribed in Lemma 11. We decompose the vector  $V_{i,\mathcal{Q}^c} = (V_{i,\mathcal{Q}^c}^{(q)})_{q\in [Q]}$  where

$$
\boldsymbol {V} _ {i, \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {(q)} = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left[ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \right] (\boldsymbol {x}) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \right].
$$

We denote  $V_{\mathcal{Q}^c}^{(q)} = (V_{1,\mathcal{Q}^c}^{(q)},\ldots ,V_{N,\mathcal{Q}^c}^{(q)})\in \mathbb{R}^{d_qN}$ . From Proposition 3, we have

$$
\frac {d _ {q}}{r _ {q} ^ {2}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ \| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {(q)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} ] \leq \left[ \max _ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} N d ^ {- \kappa_ {q}} B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} ] \right] \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

Hence, using the upper bounds on  $A_{\pmb{\tau}, \pmb{k}}^{(q)}$  in Lemma 11, we get for  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}^c$  with  $k_q > 0$ :

$$
N d ^ {- \kappa_ {q}} B (\pmb {d}, \pmb {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} _ {\tau_ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ A _ {\pmb {\tau}, \pmb {k}} ^ {(q)} ] \le C N d ^ {- \kappa_ {q}} d ^ {- \gamma + \min _ {q \in S (\pmb {k})} \kappa_ {q}} = o _ {d} (1),
$$

where we used that  $N = o_d(d^\gamma)$  and  $\kappa_q \geq \min_{q \in S(\pmb{k})} \kappa_q$  (we have  $k_q > 0$  and therefore  $q \in S(\pmb{k})$  by definition). Similarly for  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}^c$  with  $k_q = 0$ :

$$
N d ^ {- \kappa_ {q}} B (\pmb {d}, \pmb {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} _ {\pmb {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ A _ {\pmb {\tau}, \pmb {k}} ^ {(q)} ] \le C N d ^ {\eta_ {q} + \kappa_ {q} - \xi} d ^ {- \gamma - (\xi - \min _ {q \in S (\pmb {k})} \kappa_ {q})} = o _ {d} (1),
$$

where we used that by definition of  $\xi$  we have  $\eta_q + \kappa_q \leq \xi$  and  $\min_{q \in S(\pmb{k})} \kappa_q \leq \xi$ . We deduce that

$$
\frac {d _ {q}}{r _ {q} ^ {2}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ \| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {(q)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} ] = o _ {d} (1) \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2},
$$

and therefore by Markov's inequality that

$$
\frac {d _ {q}}{r _ {q} ^ {2}} \| \mathbf {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {(q)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1) \cdot \| \mathbb {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \tag {108}
$$

Notice that the properties (105) and (106) imply that there exists  $c > 0$  such that with high probability  $\lambda_{\min}(\pmb{D}) \geq \min_{i \in [N]} \lambda_{\min}(\pmb{D}_{ii}) \geq c$ . In particular, we deduce that  $\| (\pmb{D} + \pmb{\Delta})^{-1} \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq c^{-1} / 2$  with high probability. Combining these bounds and Eq. (108) and recalling that  $\|\pmb{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d^{-\max_{q \in [Q]} \kappa_q})$  show that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| V _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {\top} D ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\Delta} (D + \boldsymbol {\Delta}) ^ {- 1} V _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \right| \leq \| D ^ {- 1} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| (D + \boldsymbol {\Delta}) ^ {- 1} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \| \boldsymbol {\Delta} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| V _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {(q)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \tag {109} \\ = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1) \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We are now left to show  $\mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{Q}^c}^\top \mathbf{D}^{-1} \mathbf{V}_{\mathcal{Q}^c} = o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1)$ . For each  $i \in [N]$ , denote  $\pmb{B}_{ii} = \pmb{D}_{ii}^{-1}$  and notice that we can apply Lemma 24 to  $\pmb{B}_{ii}$  and get

$$
\max _ {i \in [ N ]} \| \boldsymbol {B} _ {i i} ^ {q q} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = O _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\frac {d _ {q}}{r _ {q} ^ {2}}\right), \quad \max _ {i \in [ N ]} \| \boldsymbol {B} _ {i i} ^ {q q ^ {\prime}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\frac {\sqrt {d _ {q} d _ {q ^ {\prime}}}}{r _ {q} r _ {q ^ {\prime}}}\right).
$$

Therefore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {D} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} = \sum_ {i \in [ N ]} \sum_ {q, q ^ {\prime} \in [ Q ]} (\boldsymbol {V} _ {i, \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {B} _ {i i} ^ {q q ^ {\prime}} \boldsymbol {V} _ {i, \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \\ \leq \sum_ {q, q ^ {\prime} \in [ Q ]} O _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1) \cdot \left(\frac {d _ {q}}{r _ {q} ^ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {(q)} \| _ {2} ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \left(\frac {d _ {q ^ {\prime}}}{r _ {q ^ {\prime}} ^ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \| _ {2} ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2}. \tag {110} \\ \end{array}
$$

Using Eq. (108) in Eq. (110), we get

$$
\boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} ^ {\mathrm {T}} \boldsymbol {D} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {V} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1) \cdot \| \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \tag {111}
$$

Combining Eq. (109) and Eq. (111) yields Eq. (104). This proves the theorem.

# G.3 Proof of Proposition 3

Proof of Proposition 3. Let us consider  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  as prescribed in Lemma 11. We have for  $q\in [Q]$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {E} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}, \varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {(q)} = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\varepsilon}} \left[ \langle \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ [ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} ] (\boldsymbol {x}) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} ], \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {y}} [ [ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} ] (\boldsymbol {y}) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \boldsymbol {y} \rangle / R) \boldsymbol {y} ^ {(q)} ] \rangle \right] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}} \left[ \left[ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} \right] (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \left[ \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \overline {{f}} \right] (\overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) H _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {(q)} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \right] \right], \\ \end{array}
$$

where we denoted  $H_{\tau}^{(q)}$  the kernel given by

$$
\begin{array}{l} H _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {(q)} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right] \langle \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {y} ^ {(q)} \rangle , \\ \end{array}
$$

Then we have

$$
H _ {\tau} ^ {(q)} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} A _ {\tau , \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right), \tag {112}
$$

where  $A_{\tau ,k}^{(q)}$  is given in Lemma 12. Hence we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {E} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}, \varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {(q)} = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}} \left[ P _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \bar {f} _ {d} (\bar {\boldsymbol {x}}) P _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \bar {f} _ {d} (\bar {\boldsymbol {y}}) H _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {(q)} (\bar {\boldsymbol {x}}, \bar {\boldsymbol {y}}) \right] \right] \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {> 0} ^ {Q}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} ] \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {\boldsymbol {q} \in [ Q ]}\right) [ P _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} ] (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) [ P _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} ] (\overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We have

$$
