\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \left[ P _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \bar {f} _ {d} \right] (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \left[ P _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \bar {f} _ {d} \right] (\overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \right] \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {l}, \boldsymbol {l} ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {l}) ]} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {l} ^ {\prime}) ]} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {l}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {f}}} _ {\boldsymbol {d}}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {l} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {f}}} _ {\boldsymbol {d}}) \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \right\} _ {\boldsymbol {q} \in [ Q ]}\right) Y _ {\boldsymbol {l}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) Y _ {\boldsymbol {l} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \right] \\ = \delta_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ]} \frac {\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} , \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{f}} _ {d}) ^ {2}}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used in the third line

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) Y _ {\boldsymbol {l}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) Y _ {\boldsymbol {l} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \right] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) Y _ {\boldsymbol {l}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \right] Y _ {\boldsymbol {l} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \right] \\ = \frac {\delta_ {\boldsymbol {k} , \boldsymbol {l}}}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}} \left[ Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) Y _ {\boldsymbol {l} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \right] \\ = \frac {\delta_ {\boldsymbol {k} , l} \delta_ {\boldsymbol {k} , l ^ {\prime}} \delta_ {\boldsymbol {s} , \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}}}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We conclude that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {E} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}, \varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {(q)} = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} ] \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ]} \frac {\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} , \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{f}} _ {d}) ^ {2}}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})} \delta_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \frac {\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} , \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} ]}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})} \| P _ {\boldsymbol {k}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} \\ \leq \left[ \max  _ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} ] \right] \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Lemma 12. Let  $\sigma$  be a weakly differentiable activation function with weak derivative  $\sigma'$ . For a fixed  $\tau \in \mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$ , define the kernels for  $q \in [Q]$

$$
\begin{array}{l} H _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {(q)} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \\ = \frac {r _ {q} ^ {2}}{d _ {q}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right] \langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle . \\ \end{array}
$$

Then, we have the following decomposition in terms of product of Gegenbauer polynomials,

$$
H _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {(q)} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {> 0} ^ {2}} A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right),
$$

where

$$
A _ {\pmb {\tau}, \pmb {k}} ^ {(q)} = r _ {q} ^ {2} \cdot [ t _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q} - 1} \lambda_ {\pmb {k} _ {q -}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau}} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} B (\pmb {d}, \pmb {k} _ {q -}) + s _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q} + 1} \lambda_ {\pmb {k} _ {q +}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau}} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} B (\pmb {d}, \pmb {k} _ {q +}) ],
$$

with  $\pmb{k}_{q + } = (k_{1},\dots ,k_{q} + 1,\dots ,k_{Q})$  and  $\pmb{k}_{q - } = (k_{1},\dots ,k_{q} - 1,\dots ,k_{Q})$ , and

$$
s _ {d, k} = \frac {k}{2 k + d - 2}, \qquad t _ {d, k} = \frac {k + d - 2}{2 k + d - 2},
$$

with the convention  $t_{d, - 1} = 0$

Proof of Lemma 12. Recall the decomposition of  $\sigma'$  in terms of tensor product of Gegenbauer polynomials,

$$
\sigma^{\prime}(\langle \boldsymbol {\theta},\boldsymbol {x}\rangle /R) = \sum_{\boldsymbol {k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{d}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{\tau}}^{\prime})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{d}\left(\{\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(q)}\rangle \}_{q\in [Q]}\right),
$$

$$
\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime} \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sqrt {d _ {1}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \sqrt {d _ {q}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) \right],
$$

Injecting this decomposition into the definition of  $H_{\tau}^{(q)}$  yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} H _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {(q)} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \\ = \frac {r _ {q} ^ {2}}{d _ {q}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right] \langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \\ = \frac{r_{q}^{2}}{d_{q}}\sum_{\boldsymbol {k},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol{d},\tau}^{\prime})\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol{d},\tau}^{\prime})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime})\times \\ \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right] \langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle . \\ \end{array}
$$

Recalling Eq. (36), we have

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right] = \delta_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}} \frac {Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} , \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right)}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})}.
$$

Hence,

$$
\begin{array}{l} H _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {(q)} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \\ = \frac{r^{2}}{d_{q}}\sum_{\boldsymbol {k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol {d},\tau}^{\prime})^{2}B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\left(\{\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}^{(q)}\rangle \}_{q\in [Q]}\right)\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}^{(q)}\rangle \\ = r _ {q} ^ {2} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) \left[ Q _ {k _ {q}} ^ {(d _ {q})} (\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle) \langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / d _ {q} \right] \prod_ {q ^ {\prime} \neq q} Q _ {k _ {q ^ {\prime}}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \rangle). \\ \end{array}
$$

By the recurrence relationship for Gegenbauer polynomials (23), we have

$$
\frac {t}{d _ {q}} Q _ {k _ {q}} ^ {(d _ {q})} (t) = s _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q}} Q _ {k _ {q} - 1} ^ {(d _ {q})} (t) + t _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q}} Q _ {k _ {q} + 1} ^ {(d _ {q})} (t),
$$

where (we use the convention  $t_{d_q, -1} = 0$ )

$$
s _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q}} = \frac {k _ {q}}{2 k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 2}, \qquad t _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q}} = \frac {k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 2}{2 k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 2}.
$$

Hence we get,

$$
\begin{array}{l} H _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {(q)} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}}) \\ = r _ {q} ^ {2} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2} B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) \left[ Q _ {k _ {q}} ^ {(d _ {q})} \left(\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle\right) \left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / d _ {q} \right] \prod_ {q ^ {\prime} \neq q} Q _ {k _ {q ^ {\prime}}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \right\rangle\right) \\ = r_{q}^{2}\sum_{\boldsymbol {k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{\tau}}^{\prime})^{2}B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})\Big[s_{d_{q},k_{q}}Q_{k_{q} - 1}^{(d_{q})}(\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}^{(q)}\rangle) + t_{d_{q},k_{q}}Q_{k_{q} + 1}^{(d_{q})}(\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{y}}^{(q)}\rangle)\Big] \\ \times \prod_ {q ^ {\prime} \neq q} Q _ {k _ {q ^ {\prime}}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \rangle) \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {2}} A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we get by matching the coefficients,

$$
A _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} = r _ {q} ^ {2} \cdot \left[ t _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q} - 1} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q -}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k} _ {q -}) + s _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q} + 1} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime}) ^ {2} B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}) \right],
$$

with  $\pmb{k}_{q+} = (k_1, \dots, k_q + 1, \dots, k_Q)$  and  $\pmb{k}_{q-} = (k_1, \dots, k_q - 1, \dots, k_Q)$ .

# G.4 Proof of Proposition 4

# G.4.1 Preliminaries

Lemma 13. Let  $\psi : \mathbb{R}^Q \to \mathbb{R}$  be a function such that  $\psi(\{\langle e_q, \cdot \rangle\}_{q \in [Q]}) \in L^2(\mathrm{PS}^d, \mu_d)$ . We will consider for integers  $\pmb{i} = (i_1, \dots, i_Q) \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^Q$ , the associated function  $\psi^{(i)}$  given by:

$$
\psi^ {(i)} \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) = \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}\right) ^ {i _ {1}} \dots \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) ^ {i _ {Q}} \psi \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right).
$$

Assume that  $\psi^{(i)}(\{\langle \pmb{e}_q,\cdot \rangle \}_{q\in [Q]})\in L^2 (\mathrm{PS}^d,\mu_d)$ . Let  $\{\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{\pmb{d}}(\psi)\}_{\pmb{k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}$  be the coefficients of the expansion of  $\psi$  in terms of the product of Gegenbauer polynomials

$$
\psi \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\psi) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sqrt {d _ {1}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \sqrt {d _ {Q}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right),
$$

$$
\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\psi) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \psi \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sqrt {d _ {1}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \sqrt {d _ {Q}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) \right].
$$

Then we can write

$$
\psi^ {(i)} \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \ldots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {i}} (\psi) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sqrt {d _ {1}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \ldots , \sqrt {d _ {Q}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right),
$$

where the coefficients  $\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{\pmb{d},\pmb{i}}(\psi)$  are given recursively: denoting  $\pmb{i}_{q+} = (i_1, \dots, i_q + 1, \dots, i_Q)$ , if  $k_q = 0$ ,

$$
\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {i} _ {q +}} (\psi) = \sqrt {d _ {q}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {i}} (\psi),
$$

and for  $k_{q} > 0$

$$
\lambda_ {\pmb {k}} ^ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {i} _ {q +}} (\psi) = \sqrt {d _ {q}} \frac {k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 2}{2 k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 2} \lambda_ {\pmb {k} _ {q +}} ^ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {i}} (\psi) + \sqrt {d _ {q}} \frac {k _ {q}}{2 k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 2} \lambda_ {\pmb {k} _ {q -}} ^ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {i}} (\psi),
$$

where we recall the notations  $\pmb{k}_{q+} = (k_1, \dots, k_q + 1, \dots, k_Q)$  and  $\pmb{k}_{q-} = (k_1, \dots, k_q - 1, \dots, k_Q)$ .

Proof of Lemma 13. We recall the following two formulas for  $k \geq 1$  (see Section B.2):

$$
\frac {x}{d} Q _ {k} ^ {(d)} (x) = \frac {k}{2 k + d - 2} Q _ {k - 1} ^ {(d)} (x) + \frac {k + d - 2}{2 k + d - 2} Q _ {k + 1} ^ {(d)} (x),
$$

$$
B (d, k) = \frac {2 k + d - 2}{k} \binom {k + d - 3} {k - 1}.
$$

Furthermore, we have  $Q_0^{(d)}(x) = 1$ ,  $Q_1^{(d)}(x) = x / d$  and therefore therefore  $xQ_0^{(d)}(x) = dQ_1^{(d)}(x)$ . Similarly to the proof of [GMMM19b, Lemma 6], we insert these expressions in the expansion of the function  $\psi$ . Matching the coefficients of the expansion yields the result.

Let  $\pmb{u}:\mathbb{S}^{D - 1}(\sqrt{D})\times \mathbb{S}^{D - 1}(\sqrt{D})\to \mathbb{R}^{D\times D}$  be a matrix-valued function defined by

$$
\boldsymbol {u} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \right].
$$

We can write this function as a  $Q$  by  $Q$  block matrix function  $\pmb{u} = (\pmb{u}^{(qq')})_{q,q' \in [Q]}$ , where  $\pmb{u}^{(qq')}: \mathbb{S}^{D-1}(\sqrt{D}) \times \mathbb{S}^{D-1}(\sqrt{D}) \to \mathbb{R}^{d_q \times d_{q'}}$  are given by

$$
\boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \right].
$$

We have the following lemma which is a generalization of [GMMM19b, Lemma 7], that shows essentially the same decomposition of the matrix  $\pmb{u}(\pmb{\theta}_1,\pmb{\theta}_2)$  as by integration by part if we had  $\pmb{x}\sim \mathsf{N}(0,\mathbf{I})$ .

Lemma 14. For  $q \in [Q]$ , there exists functions  $u_{1}^{(qq)}, u_{2}^{(qq)}, u_{3,1}^{(qq)}, u_{3,2}^{(qq)} : \mathbb{S}^{D-1}(\sqrt{D}) \times \mathbb{S}^{D-1}(\sqrt{D}) \to \mathbb{R}$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) = u _ {1} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \mathbf {I} _ {d _ {q}} + u _ {2} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) [ \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\top} ] \\ + u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\top} + u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\top}. \\ \end{array}
$$

For  $q,q^{\prime}\in [Q]$  , there exists functions  $u_{2,1}^{(qq^{\prime})},u_{2,2}^{(qq^{\prime})},u_{3,1}^{(qq^{\prime})},u_{3,2}^{(qq^{\prime})}:\mathbb{S}^{D - 1}(\sqrt{D})\times \mathbb{S}^{D - 1}(\sqrt{D})\to \mathbb{R}$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \pmb {u} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1}, \pmb {\theta} _ {2}) = u _ {2, 1} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1}, \pmb {\theta} _ {2}) \pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} (\pmb {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\top} + u _ {2, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1}, \pmb {\theta} _ {2}) \pmb {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\top} \\ + u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime}}) ^ {\mathsf {T}} + u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime}}) ^ {\mathsf {T}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof of Lemma 14. Denote  $\gamma^{(q)} = \langle \overline{\pmb{\theta}}_1^{(q)},\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_2^{(q)}\rangle /d_q$ . Let us rotate each sphere  $q\in [Q]$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} = \left(\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \sqrt {d _ {q}}, 0, \dots , 0\right), \\ \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} = \left(\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \sqrt {d _ {q}} \gamma^ {(q)}, \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \sqrt {d _ {q}} \sqrt {1 - (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2}}, 0, \dots , 0\right). \tag {113} \\ \end{array}
$$

Step 1:  $\pmb{u}^{(qq)}$ .

Let us start with  $\pmb{u}^{(qq)}$ . For clarity, we will denote (in the rotated basis (113))

$$
\alpha_ {1} = \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R = \sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \sqrt {d _ {q}} / R \cdot \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)},
$$

$$
\alpha_ {2} = \langle \pmb {\theta} _ {2}, \pmb {x} \rangle / R = \sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left[ \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \sqrt {d _ {q}} \gamma^ {(q)} / R \cdot \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)} + \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \sqrt {d _ {q}} \sqrt {1 - (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2}} / R \cdot \overline {{x}} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \right].
$$

Then it is easy to show that we can rewrite

$$
\boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {1}) \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {2}) \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\mathsf {T}} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {u} _ {1: 2, 1: 2} ^ {(q q)} & \boldsymbol {0} \\ \boldsymbol {0} & \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {1}) \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {2}) (x _ {3} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} ] \boldsymbol {\mathbf {I}} _ {d _ {q} - 2} \end{array} \right],
$$

with

$$
\boldsymbol {u} _ {1: 2, 1: 2} ^ {(q q)} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {1}) \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {2}) (x _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} ] & \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {1}) \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {2}) x _ {1} ^ {(q)} x _ {2} ^ {(q)} ] \\ \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {1}) \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {2}) x _ {2} ^ {(q)} x _ {1} ^ {(q)} ] & \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {1}) \sigma^ {\prime} (\alpha_ {2}) (x _ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} ] \end{array} \right].
$$

Case (a):  $\theta_1^{(q)}\neq \theta_2^{(q)}$

Given any functions  $u_{1}^{(qq)}, u_{2}^{(qq)}, u_{3,1}^{(qq)}, u_{3,2}^{(qq)} : \mathbb{S}^{D-1}(\sqrt{D}) \times \mathbb{S}^{D-1}(\sqrt{D}) \to \mathbb{R}$ , we define

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}\right) = u _ {1} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}\right) \mathbf {I} _ {d _ {1}} + u _ {2} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}\right) \left[ \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)}\right) ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) ^ {\top} \right] \\ + u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\mathsf {T}} + u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\mathsf {T}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

In the rotated basis (113), we have

$$
\tilde {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \tilde {\boldsymbol {u}} _ {1: 2, 1: 2} ^ {(q q)} & \boldsymbol {0} \\ \boldsymbol {0} & u _ {1} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \mathbf {I} _ {d _ {q} - 2} \end{array} \right],
$$

where (we dropped the dependency on  $(\pmb{\theta}_1, \pmb{\theta}_2)$  for clarity)

$$
\boldsymbol {u} _ {1 1} ^ {(q q)} = u _ {1} ^ {(q q)} + 2 \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} d _ {q} \gamma^ {(q)} u _ {2} ^ {(q q)} + (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q)} + (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2} u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q)},
$$

$$
\pmb {u} _ {1 2} ^ {(q q)} = \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} d _ {q} \sqrt {1 - (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2}} u _ {2} ^ {(q q)} + (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} \gamma^ {(q)} \sqrt {1 - (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2}} u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q)},
$$

$$
\pmb {u} _ {2 2} ^ {(q q)} = u _ {1} ^ {(q q)} + (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} (1 - (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2}) u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q)}.
$$

We see that  $\pmb{u}^{(qq)}$  and  $\tilde{\pmb{u}}^{(qq)}$  will be equal if and only if we have the following equalities:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {T r} \left(\boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}\right)\right) = \operatorname {T r} \left(\tilde {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}\right)\right) \\ = d _ {q} u _ {1} ^ {(q q)} + 2 \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} d _ {q} \gamma^ {(q)} u _ {2} ^ {(q q)} + (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q)} + (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \rangle = \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \tilde {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \rangle \\ = \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} d _ {q} \gamma^ {(q)} u _ {1} ^ {(q q)} + (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} ^ {2} (1 + (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2}) u _ {2} ^ {(q q)} \\ + (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} d _ {q} ^ {2} \gamma^ {(q)} u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q)} + \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} d _ {q} ^ {2} \gamma^ {(q)} u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} \rangle = \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \tilde {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} \rangle \\ = (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} u _ {1} ^ {(q q)} + 2 (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} d _ {q} ^ {2} \gamma^ {(q)} u _ {2} ^ {(q q)} \\ + \left(\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) ^ {4} d _ {q} ^ {2} u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q)} + \left(\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) ^ {2} \left(\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}\right) ^ {2} d _ {q} ^ {2} \left(\gamma^ {(q)}\right) ^ {2} u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \rangle = \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)}, \tilde {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \rangle \\ = (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} u _ {1} ^ {(q q)} + 2 \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} d _ {q} ^ {2} \gamma^ {(q)} u _ {2} ^ {(q q)} \\ + (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} ^ {2} (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2} u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q)} + (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {4} d _ {q} ^ {2} u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence  $\tilde{\pmb{u}}^{(qq)} = \pmb{u}^{(qq)}$  if and only if

$$
\left[ \begin{array}{l} u _ {1} ^ {(q q)} \\ u _ {2} ^ {(q q)} \\ u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q)} \\ u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q)} \end{array} \right] = d _ {q} ^ {- 1} \left(\boldsymbol {M} ^ {(q q)}\right) ^ {- 1} \times \left[ \begin{array}{l} \operatorname {T r} \left(\boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}\right)\right) \\ \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}\right) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle \\ \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}\right) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle \\ \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}\right) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle \end{array} \right], \tag {114}
$$

where

$$
\pmb {M} ^ {(q q)} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c c c} 1 & 2 \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \gamma^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} & (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \\ \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \gamma^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} (1 + (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2}) & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} d _ {q} \gamma^ {(q)} & \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} d _ {q} \gamma^ {(q)} \\ (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} & 2 (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} d _ {q} \gamma^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {4} d _ {q} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \\ (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} & 2 \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} d _ {q} \gamma^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2} & (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {4} d _ {q} \end{array} \right]
$$

is invertible almost surely (for  $\tau_1^{(q)},\tau_2^{(q)}\neq 0$  and  $\gamma^{(q)}\neq 1$ ).

Case (b):  $\pmb{\theta}_{1}^{(q)} = \pmb{\theta}_{2}^{(q)}$ .

Similarly, for some fixed  $\alpha$  and  $\beta$ , we define

$$
\tilde {\pmb {u}} ^ {(q q)} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1}, \pmb {\theta} _ {1}) = \alpha \mathbf {I} _ {d _ {q}} + \beta \pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\top}.
$$

Then  $\pmb{u}^{(qq)}(\pmb{\theta}_1, \pmb{\theta}_1)$  and  $\tilde{\pmb{u}}^{(qq)}(\pmb{\theta}_1, \pmb{\theta}_1)$  are equal if and only if

$$
\left[ \begin{array}{c} \alpha \\ \beta \end{array} \right] = d _ {q} ^ {- 1} (M _ {\parallel} ^ {(q q)}) ^ {- 1} \times \left[ \begin{array}{c} \operatorname {T r} (\boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1})) \\ \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} \rangle \end{array} \right],
$$

where

$$
\boldsymbol {M} _ {\parallel} ^ {(q q)} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} 1 & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \\ (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {4} d _ {q} \end{array} \right].
$$

Step 2:  $\pmb{u}^{(qq^{\prime})}$  for  $q\neq q^{\prime}$

Similarly to the two previous steps, we define for any functions  $u_{2,1}^{(qq')}, u_{2,2}^{(qq')}, u_{3,1}^{(qq')}, u_{3,2}^{(qq')}: \mathbb{S}^{D-1}(\sqrt{D}) \times \mathbb{S}^{D-1}(\sqrt{D}) \to \mathbb{R}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \tilde {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) = u _ {2, 1} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\mathsf {T}} + u _ {2, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\mathsf {T}} \\ + u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1}, \pmb {\theta} _ {2}) \pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime}}) ^ {\mathsf {T}} + u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1}, \pmb {\theta} _ {2}) \pmb {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} (\pmb {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime}}) ^ {\mathsf {T}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

We can rewrite  $\tilde{\pmb{u}}^{(qq^{\prime})}$  as

$$
\tilde {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \tilde {\boldsymbol {u}} _ {1: 2, 1: 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} & \boldsymbol {0} \\ \boldsymbol {0} & \boldsymbol {0} \end{array} \right],
$$

where

$$
\tilde {\boldsymbol {u}} _ {1 1} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} = u _ {2, 1} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})} + u _ {2, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q)} + u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} + u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q)} \gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})},
$$

$$
\tilde {\pmb {u}} _ {1 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} = u _ {2, 1} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \sqrt {1 - (\gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {2}} + u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q)} \sqrt {1 - (\gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {2}},
$$

$$
\tilde {\pmb {u}} _ {2 1} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} = u _ {2, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \sqrt {1 - (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2}} + u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \sqrt {1 - (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2}} \gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})},
$$

$$
\tilde {\pmb {u}} _ {2 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} = u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \sqrt {1 - (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2}} \sqrt {1 - (\gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {2}}.
$$

Case (a):  $\pmb{\theta}_{1}^{(q)}\neq \pmb{\theta}_{2}^{(q)}$

We have equality  $\tilde{\pmb{u}}^{(qq^{\prime})} = \pmb{u}^{(qq^{\prime})}$  if and only if

$$
\left[ \begin{array}{l} u _ {2, 1} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \\ u _ {2, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \\ u _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \\ u _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \end{array} \right] = (d _ {q} d _ {q ^ {\prime}}) ^ {- 1} (\pmb {M} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {- 1} \times \left[ \begin{array}{l} \langle \pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \pmb {u} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1}, \pmb {\theta} _ {2}) \pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \rangle \\ \langle \pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \pmb {u} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1}, \pmb {\theta} _ {2}) \pmb {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \rangle \\ \langle \pmb {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)}, \pmb {u} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1}, \pmb {\theta} _ {2}) \pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \rangle \\ \langle \pmb {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)}, \pmb {u} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1}, \pmb {\theta} _ {2}) \pmb {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \rangle \end{array} \right],
$$

where  $M^{(qq^{\prime})}$  is given by

$$
\left[ \begin{array}{c c c c} (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})} & \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {2} \gamma^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {2} & \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q)} \gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \\ (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {2} & \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q)} \gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})} & \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {2} \gamma^ {(q)} \\ \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q)} \gamma^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {2} & \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {2} \gamma^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \\ \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {2} \gamma^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})} & \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \gamma^ {(q)} \gamma^ {(q ^ {\prime})} & (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {2} \end{array} \right],
$$

which is invertible almost surely (for  $\tau_1^{(q)},\tau_2^{(q)}\neq 0$  and  $\gamma^{(q)}\neq 1$ ).

Case (b):  $\pmb{\theta}_{1}^{(q)} = \pmb{\theta}_{2}^{(q)}$ .

It is straightforward to check that

$$
\boldsymbol {u} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}) = \beta \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime}}) ^ {\mathsf {T}},
$$

where

$$
\beta = (d _ {q} d _ {q ^ {\prime}}) ^ {- 1} (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {- 2} \Big \langle \pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \pmb {u} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\pmb {\theta} _ {1}, \pmb {\theta} _ {1}) \pmb {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \Big \rangle .
$$

![](images/bc8e43573263064abb1c3b61e57dd8d735d1946893925dda6eb2bf958ce9ff28.jpg)

# G.4.2 Proof of Proposition 4

# Step 1. Construction of the activation function  $\hat{\sigma}$ .

Recall the definition of  $\sigma_{d,\tau}$  in Eq. (100) and its expansion in terms of tensor product of Gegenbauer polynomials:

$$
\sigma^{\prime}(\langle \boldsymbol {\theta},\boldsymbol {x}\rangle /R) = \sum_{\boldsymbol {k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{d}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{\tau}}^{\prime})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{d}\left(\{\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(q)}\rangle \}_{q\in [Q]}\right),
$$

$$
\lambda_ {k} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime} \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \ldots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) Q _ {k} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sqrt {d _ {1}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \ldots , \sqrt {d _ {Q}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) \right].
$$

We recall the definition of  $q_{\xi} = \arg \max_{q\in [Q]}\{\eta_q + \kappa_q\}$ . Let  $l_{2} > l_{1}\geq 2L + 5$  be two indices that satisfy the conditions of Assumption 3.(b) and we define  $l_{1} = (0,\dots ,0,l_{1},0,\dots ,0)$  ( $l_{1}$  at position  $q_{\xi}$ ) and  $l_{2} = (0,\ldots ,0,l_{2},0,\ldots ,0)$  ( $l_{2}$  at position  $q_{\xi}$ ). Using the Gegenbauer coefficients of  $\sigma^{\prime}$ , we define a new activation function  $\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}$  by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \hat {\sigma} ^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q} \backslash \left\{l _ {1}, l _ {2} \right\}} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) (115) \\ + \sum_ {t = 1, 2} \left(1 - \delta_ {t}\right) \lambda_ {l _ {t}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}\right) B \left(d _ {q _ {\xi}}, l _ {t}\right) Q _ {l _ {t}} ^ {\left(d _ {q _ {\xi}}\right)} \left(\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {\left(q _ {\xi}\right)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {\left(q _ {\xi}\right)} \right\rangle\right), (116) \\ \end{array}
$$

for some  $\delta_1, \delta_2$  that we will fix later (with  $|\delta_t| \leq 1$ ).

# Step 2. The functions  $u, \hat{u}$  and  $\bar{u}$ .

Let  $\pmb{u}$  and  $\hat{\pmb{u}}$  be the matrix-valued functions associated respectively to  $\sigma^{\prime}$  and  $\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}$

$$
\boldsymbol {u} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}\right) = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \right], \tag {117}
$$

$$
\hat {\boldsymbol {u}} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}\right) = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \hat {\sigma} ^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \hat {\sigma} ^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \right]. \tag {118}
$$

From Lemma 14, there exists functions  $u_{1}^{ab}, u_{2,1}^{ab}, u_{2,2}^{ab}, u_{3,1}^{ab}, u_{3,2}^{ab}$  and  $\hat{u}_{1}^{ab}, \hat{u}_{2,1}^{ab}, \hat{u}_{2,2}^{ab}, \hat{u}_{3,1}^{ab}, \hat{u}_{3,2}^{ab}$  (for  $a, b \in [Q]$ ), which decompose  $\pmb{u}$  and  $\hat{\pmb{u}}$  along  $\pmb{\theta}_{1}$  and  $\pmb{\theta}_{2}$  vectors. We define  $\bar{\pmb{u}} = \pmb{u} - \hat{\pmb{u}}$ . Then we have the same decomposition for  $\bar{u}_{k,j}^{ab} = u_{k,j}^{ab} - \hat{u}_{k,j}^{ab}$  for  $a, b \in [Q], k = 1, 2, 3, j = 1, 2$ .

# Step 3. Construction of the kernel matrices.

Let  $\pmb{U},\hat{\pmb{U}},\bar{\pmb{U}}\in \mathbb{R}^{ND\times ND}$  with  $i,j$  -th block (for  $i,j\in [N]$ ) given by

$$
\boldsymbol {U} _ {i j} = \boldsymbol {u} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right), \tag {119}
$$

$$
\hat {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i j} = \hat {\boldsymbol {u}} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right), \tag {120}
$$

$$
\bar {U} _ {i j} = \bar {\boldsymbol {u}} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) = \boldsymbol {u} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) - \hat {\boldsymbol {u}} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right). \tag {121}
$$

Note that we have  $\mathbf{U} = \hat{\mathbf{U}} + \bar{\mathbf{U}}$ . By Eq. (120) and (118), it is easy to see that  $\hat{\mathbf{U}} \succeq 0$ . Then we have  $\mathbf{U} \succeq \bar{\mathbf{U}}$ . In the following, we would like to lower bound matrix  $\bar{\mathbf{U}}$ .

We decompose  $\bar{U}$  as

$$
\bar {U} = D + \Delta ,
$$

where  $\pmb{D} \in \mathbb{R}^{DN \times DN}$  is a block-diagonal matrix, with

$$
\boldsymbol {D} = \operatorname {d i a g} \left(\bar {U} _ {1 1}, \dots , \bar {U} _ {N N}\right), \tag {122}
$$

and  $\pmb{\Delta} \in \mathbb{R}^{DN \times DN}$  is formed by blocks  $\pmb{\Delta}_{ij} \in \mathbb{R}^{D \times D}$  for  $i, j \in [n]$ , defined by

$$
\boldsymbol {\Delta} _ {i j} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} 0, & i = j, \\ \bar {U} _ {i j}, & i \neq j. \end{array} \right. \tag {123}
$$

In the rest of the proof, we will prove that  $\| \pmb{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d^{-\max_q\in [Q]}\kappa_q)$  and the block matrix  $D$  verifies the properties (105) and (106).

Step 4. Prove that  $\| \pmb{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d^{-\max_{q\in [Q]}\kappa_q})$

We will prove in fact that  $\| \pmb{\Delta}\| _F^2 = o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d^{-2\max_q\in [Q]}\kappa_q)$ . For the rest of the proof, we fix  $\varepsilon_0\in (0,1)$  and we restrict ourselves without loss of generality to the set  $\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon_0}$ .

Let us start with  $\overline{\pmb{u}}^{(qq)}$  for  $q\in [Q]$ . Denoting  $\gamma_{ij}^{(q)} = \langle \overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i^{(q)},\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_j^{(q)}\rangle /d_q < 1$ , we get, from Eq. (114),

$$
\left[ \begin{array}{l} \bar {u} _ {1} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) \\ \bar {u} _ {2} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) \\ \bar {u} _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) \\ \bar {u} _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) \end{array} \right] = \left[ \begin{array}{c} u _ {1} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) - \hat {u} _ {1} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) \\ u _ {2} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) - \hat {u} _ {2} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) \\ u _ {3, 1} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) - \hat {u} _ {3, 1} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) \\ u _ {3, 2} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) - \hat {u} _ {3, 2} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}\right) \end{array} \right] = d _ {q} ^ {- 1} \left(M _ {i j} ^ {(q q)}\right) ^ {- 1} \times \left[ \begin{array}{c} \operatorname {T r} (\bar {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2})) \\ \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \rangle \\ \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1} ^ {(q)} \rangle \\ \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {u}} ^ {(q q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2}) \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \rangle \end{array} \right], \tag {124}
$$

where  $M_{ij}^{(qq)}$  is given by

$$
\left[ \begin{array}{c c c c} 1 & 2 \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \gamma_ {i j} ^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} & (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \\ \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} \gamma_ {i j} ^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} (1 + (\gamma_ {i j} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2}) & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} d _ {q} \gamma_ {i j} ^ {(q)} & \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} d _ {q} \gamma_ {i j} ^ {(q)} \\ (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} & 2 (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} \tau_ {2} ^ {(q)} d _ {q} \gamma^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {4} d _ {q} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} (\gamma_ {i j} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \\ (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} & \tau_ {1} ^ {(q)} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {3} d _ {q} \gamma_ {i j} ^ {(q)} & (\tau_ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} d _ {q} (\gamma^ {(q)}) ^ {2} & (\tau_ {2} ^ {(q)}) ^ {4} d _ {q} \end{array} \right].
$$

Using the notations of Lemma 13, we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {T r} \left(\boldsymbol {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)}\right) = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \right] \\ = r_{q}^{2}\sum_{\boldsymbol {k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}}^{\prime})\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{j}}^{\prime})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\left(\{\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}^{(q)}\rangle \}_{q\in [Q]}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \rangle = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {z} ^ {(q)} \rangle \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle ] \\ = r_{q}^{2}\tau_{i}^{(q)}\tau_{j}^{(q)}\sum_{\boldsymbol {k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{1}_{q}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol{d},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}}^{\prime})\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{1}_{q}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol{d},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{j}}^{\prime})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\left(\{\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}^{(q)}\rangle \}_{q\in [Q]}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} \rangle = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R\right) \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle^ {2} \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R\right) \right] \\ = r_{q}^{2}(\tau_{i}^{(q)})^{2}\sum_{\boldsymbol {k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{2}_{q}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol{d},\tau_{i}}^{\prime})\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol{d},\tau_{j}}^{\prime})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\left(\{\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}^{(q)}\rangle \}_{q\in [Q]}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \rangle = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle^ {2} \right] \\ = r_{q}^{2}(\tau_{j}^{(q)})^{2}\sum_{\boldsymbol {k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol{d},\tau_{i}}^{\prime})\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{ \boldsymbol{d},\boldsymbol{2}_{q}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol{d},\tau_{j}}^{\prime})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\left(\{\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{i}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{j}^{(q)}\rangle \}_{q\in [Q]}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we denoted  $\mathbf{1}_q = (0, \dots, 0, 1, 0, \dots, 0)$  (namely the  $q$ 'th coordinate vector in  $\mathbb{R}^Q$ ) and  $\mathbf{2}_q = (0, \dots, 0, 2, 0, \dots, 0) = 2\mathbf{1}_q$ .

We get similar expressions for  $\hat{U}_{ij}$  with  $\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{\pmb{d}}(\sigma_{\pmb{d},\tau}^{\prime})$  replaced by  $\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{\pmb{d}}(\hat{\sigma}_{\pmb{d},\tau}^{\prime})$ . Because we defined  $\sigma^{\prime}$  and  $\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}$  by only modifying the  $l_{1}$ -th and  $l_{2}$ -th coefficients, we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {T r} \left(\bar {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)}\right) = \operatorname {T r} \left(U _ {i j} ^ {(q q)} - \hat {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)}\right) \\ = r _ {q} ^ {2} \sum_ {t = 1, 2} \delta_ {t} (2 - \delta_ {t}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {l} _ {t}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {i}} ^ {\prime}\right) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {l} _ {t}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {j}} ^ {\prime}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {l} _ {t}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {l} _ {t}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{d _ {q} \gamma_ {i j} ^ {(q)} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right). \tag {126} \\ \end{array}
$$

Recalling that  $\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{d,\mathbf{1}_{q}}$  only depend on  $\lambda_{\pmb{k} - \mathbf{1}_q}^{\pmb{d}}$  and  $\lambda_{\pmb{k} + \mathbf{1}_q}^{\pmb{d}}$ , and  $\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{d,2_q}$  on  $\lambda_{\pmb{k} - \pmb{2}_q}^{\pmb{d}}$ ,  $\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{\pmb{d}}$  and  $\lambda_{\pmb{k} + \pmb{2}_q}^{\pmb{d}}$ , (Lemma 13), we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \rangle \\ = r_{q}^{2}\tau_{i}^{(q)}\tau_{j}^{(q)}\sum_{t = \{1,2\} ,\boldsymbol {k}\in \{\boldsymbol{l}_{t}\pm \mathbf{1}_{q}\}}\delta_{t}(2 - \delta_{t})\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d},\mathbf{1}_{q}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol{d},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}}^{\prime})\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d},\mathbf{1}_{q}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol{d},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{j}}^{\prime})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\left(\left\{d_{q}\gamma_{ij}^{(q)}\right\}_{q\in [Q]}\right), \\ \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} \rangle \\ = r _ {q} ^ {2} \left(\tau_ {i} ^ {(q)}\right) ^ {2} \sum_ {t \in \{1, 2 \}, k \in \left\{\boldsymbol {l} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {l} _ {t} \pm \mathbf {2} _ {q} \right\}} \delta_ {t} (2 - \delta_ {t}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}, \mathbf {2} _ {q}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {i}} ^ {\prime}\right) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau_ {j}} ^ {\prime}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{d _ {q} \gamma_ {i j} ^ {(q)} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right), \tag {127} \\ \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \rangle \\ = r_{q}^{2}(\tau_{j}^{(q)})^{2}\sum_{t\in \{1,2\} ,\boldsymbol {k}\in \{\boldsymbol{l}_{t},\boldsymbol{l}_{t}\pm \mathbf{2}_{q}\}}\delta_{t}(2 - \delta_{t})\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{d},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{i}}) \lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d},\mathbf{2}_{q}}(\sigma^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{d},\boldsymbol{\tau}_{j}})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\left(\left\{d_{q}\gamma_{ij}^{(q)}\right\}_{q\in [Q]}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used the convention  $\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{d}(\sigma_{\pmb{d},\tau}^{\prime}) = 0$  if one of the coordinates verifies  $k_{q} < 0$ .

From Lemma 13, Lemma 19 and Lemma 20, we get for  $t = 1,2$  and  $q \neq q_{\xi}$ :

$$
\lim _ {(d, \pmb {\tau} _ {i}, \pmb {\tau} _ {j}) \to (+ \infty , \mathbf {1}, \mathbf {1})} \lambda_ {\pmb {l} _ {t}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau} _ {i}} ^ {\prime}) \lambda_ {\pmb {l} _ {t}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau} _ {j}} ^ {\prime}) B (\pmb {d}, \pmb {l} _ {t}) = \frac {\mu_ {l _ {t}} (\sigma^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{l _ {t} !},
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {(d, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {j}) \rightarrow (+ \infty , \mathbf {1}, \mathbf {1})} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {l} _ {t} + \mathbf {1} _ {q}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}, \mathbf {1} _ {q}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}} ^ {\prime}\right) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {l} _ {t} + \mathbf {1} _ {q}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}, \mathbf {1} _ {q}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {j}} ^ {\prime}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {l} _ {t} + \mathbf {1} _ {q}) = \frac {\mu_ {l _ {t}} \left(\sigma^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}}{l _ {t} !}, \tag {128} \\ \lim _ {(d, \pmb {\tau} _ {i}, \pmb {\tau} _ {j}) \to (+ \infty , \mathbf {1}, \mathbf {1})} \lambda_ {\pmb {l} _ {t}} ^ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {2} _ {q}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau} _ {i}} ^ {\prime}) \lambda_ {\pmb {l} _ {t}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau} _ {j}} ^ {\prime}) B (\pmb {d}, \pmb {l} _ {t}) = \frac {\mu_ {l _ {t}} (\sigma^ {\prime}) ^ {2}}{l _ {t} !}, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\lim _ {(d, \pmb {\tau} _ {i}, \pmb {\tau} _ {j}) \to (+ \infty , \mathbf {1}, \mathbf {1})} \lambda_ {l _ {t} + \mathbf {2} _ {q}} ^ {\pmb {d}, \mathbf {2} _ {q}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau} _ {i}} ^ {\prime}) \lambda_ {l _ {t} + \mathbf {2} _ {q}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau} _ {j}} ^ {\prime}) B (\pmb {d}, l _ {t} + \mathbf {2} _ {q}) = 0,
$$

while for  $q = q_{\xi}$  and  $u \in \{-1, 1\}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {(d, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {j}) \rightarrow (+ \infty , \mathbf {1}, \mathbf {1})} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {l} _ {t} + u \mathbf {1} _ {q _ {\xi}}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}, \mathbf {1} _ {q _ {\xi}}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}} ^ {\prime}\right) [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {l} _ {t} + u \mathbf {1} _ {q _ {\xi}}) (l _ {t} + u)! ] ^ {1 / 2} \tag {129} \\ = \mu_ {l _ {t} + u + 1} \left(\sigma^ {\prime}\right) + \left(l _ {t} + u\right) \mu_ {l _ {t} + u - 1} \left(\sigma^ {\prime}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

and for  $v\in \{-2,0,2\}$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lim  _ {(d, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {j}) \rightarrow (+ \infty , \mathbf {1}, \mathbf {1})} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {l} _ {t} + v \mathbf {1} _ {q _ {\xi}}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}, \mathbf {2} _ {q _ {\xi}}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {i}} ^ {\prime}\right) [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {l} _ {t} + v \mathbf {1} _ {q _ {\xi}}) (l _ {t} + v)! ] ^ {1 / 2} \tag {130} \\ = \mu_ {l _ {t} + v + 2} \left(\sigma^ {\prime}\right) + (2 l _ {t} + 2 v + 1) \mu_ {l _ {t} + v} \left(\sigma^ {\prime}\right) + (l _ {t} + v) \left(l _ {t} + v - 1\right) \mu_ {l _ {t} + v - 2} \left(\sigma^ {\prime}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

From Lemma (26), we recall that the coefficients of the  $k$ -th Gegenbauer polynomial  $Q_k^{(d)}(x) = \sum_{s=0}^k p_{k,s}^{(d)} x^s$  satisfy

$$
p _ {k, s} ^ {(d)} = O _ {d} \left(d ^ {- k / 2 - s / 2}\right). \tag {131}
$$

Furthermore, Lemma 27 shows that  $\max_{i\neq j}|\langle \overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i^{(q)},\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_j^{(q)}\rangle | = O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{d_q\log d_q})$  . We deduce that

$$
\left. \max  _ {i \neq j} \left| Q _ {k _ {q}} ^ {(d _ {q})} \left(\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle\right) \right| = \tilde {O} _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(d _ {q} ^ {- k _ {q} / 2}\right) \right. \tag {132}
$$

Plugging the estimates (128) and (132) into Eqs. (126) and (127), we obtain that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \right. \max  _ {i \neq j} \left\{\left| \operatorname {T r} \left(\bar {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)}\right)\right|, \left| \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \bar {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \rangle \right|, \left| \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \bar {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} \rangle , \left| \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)}, \bar {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \rangle \right|\right.\right\} \tag {133} \\ = \tilde {O} _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(d ^ {2 \xi} d ^ {- \eta_ {q} l _ {1} / 2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

From Eq. (125), using the fact that  $\max_{i\neq j}|\gamma_{ij}^{(q)}| = O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{(\log d_q) / d_q})$  and Cramer's rule for matrix inversion, it is easy to see that

$$
\max  _ {i \neq j} \max  _ {l, k \in [ 4 ]} | ((\boldsymbol {M} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)}) ^ {- 1}) _ {l k} | = O _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1). \tag {134}
$$

We deduce from (133), (124) and (134) that for  $a \in [3], b \in [2]$ ,

$$
\max  _ {i \neq j} \left\{\left| \bar {u} _ {a, b} ^ {(q q)} \left(\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)}\right) \right| \right\} = \tilde {O} _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(d ^ {2 \xi} d ^ {- \eta_ {q} l _ {1} / 2}\right). \tag {135}
$$

As a result, combining Eq. (135) with Eq. (121) in the expression of  $\overline{u}^{(qq)}$  given in Lemma 14, we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {i \neq j} \| \bar {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q)} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \\ = \max _ {i \neq j} \| \bar {u} _ {1} ^ {(q q)} \mathbf {I} _ {d _ {q}} + \bar {u} _ {2} ^ {(q q)} [ \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\top} ] + \bar {u} _ {3, 1} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\top} + \bar {u} _ {3, 2} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\top} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \\ \leq \tilde {O} _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(d ^ {6 \xi} d ^ {- \eta_ {q} l _ {1}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

A similar computation shows that

$$
\max  _ {i \neq j} \| \bar {U} _ {i j} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \leq \tilde {O} _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (d ^ {6 \xi} d ^ {- \eta_ {q} l _ {1}}).
$$

By the expression of  $\triangle$  given by (123), we conclude that

$$
\| \boldsymbol {\Delta} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \leq \| \boldsymbol {\Delta} \| _ {F} ^ {2} = \sum_ {q, q ^ {\prime} \in [ Q ]} \sum_ {i, j = 1, i \neq j} ^ {N} \| \bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i j} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \| _ {F} ^ {2} = \tilde {\mathcal {O}} _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (N ^ {2} d ^ {6 \xi - \eta_ {q} l _ {1}}).
$$

By assumption,  $N = o_{d}(d^{\gamma})$ . Hence, since by assumption  $\eta_q l_1 \geq 2\gamma + 7\xi$ , we deduce that  $\|\pmb{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d^{-\xi}) = o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d^{-\max_{q \in [Q]} \kappa_q})$ .

Step 5. Checking the properties of matrix  $D$ .

By Lemma 14, we can express  $\bar{U}_{ii}$  as a block matrix with

$$
\bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i i} ^ {(q q)} = \alpha^ {(q)} \mathbf {I} _ {d _ {q}} + \beta^ {(q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\mathsf {T}}, \quad \bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i i} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} = \beta^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\mathsf {T}},
$$

with coefficients given by

$$
\left[ \begin{array}{c} \alpha^ {(q)} \\ \beta^ {(q)} \end{array} \right] = \left[ d _ {q} \left(d _ {q} - 1\right) \left(\tau_ {i} ^ {(q)}\right) ^ {4} \right] ^ {- 1} \left[ \begin{array}{c c} d _ {q} \left(\tau_ {i} ^ {(q)}\right) ^ {4} & - \left(\tau_ {i} ^ {(q)}\right) ^ {2} \\ - \left(\tau_ {i} ^ {(q)}\right) ^ {2} & 1 \end{array} \right] \times \left[ \begin{array}{c} \operatorname {T r} \left(\bar {U} _ {i i} ^ {(q q)}\right) \\ \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \bar {U} _ {i i} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle \end{array} \right], \tag {136}
$$

$$
\beta^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} = (d _ {q} d _ {q ^ {\prime}}) ^ {- 1} (\tau_ {i} ^ {(q)} \tau_ {i} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) ^ {- 2} \langle \pmb {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\pmb {U}} _ {i i} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} (\pmb {\theta} _ {i}, \pmb {\theta} _ {i}) \pmb {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \rangle .
$$

Let us first focus on the  $q = q_{\xi}$  sphere. Using Eqs. (126) and (127) with the expressions (129) and (130), we get the following convergence in probability (using that  $\{\tau_i^{(q)}\}_{i\in [N]}$  concentrates on 1),

$$
\sup  _ {i \in [ N ]} \left| r _ {q _ {\xi}} ^ {- 2} \operatorname {T r} \left(\bar {U} _ {i i} ^ {(q _ {\xi} q _ {\xi})}\right) - F _ {1} (\boldsymbol {\delta}) \right| \xrightarrow {\mathbb {P}} 0,
$$

$$
\sup  _ {i \in [ N ]} \left| r _ {q _ {\xi}} ^ {- 2} \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q _ {\xi})}, \bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i i} ^ {(q _ {\xi} q _ {\xi})} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q _ {\xi})} \right\rangle - F _ {2} (\boldsymbol {\delta}) \right| \xrightarrow [ ]{\mathbb {P}} 0, \tag {137}
$$

where we denoted  $\delta = (\delta_{1},\delta_{2})$  (where  $\delta_1,\delta_2$  first appears in the definition of  $\hat{\sigma}$  in Eq. (115), and till now  $\delta_1,\delta_2$  are still not determined) and, similarly to the proof of [GMMM19b, Proposition 5] and letting  $\mu_k\equiv \mu_k(\sigma ')$ , we have

$$
F _ {1} (\delta) = \sum_ {t \in \{1, 2 \}} \delta_ {t} (2 - \delta_ {t}) \frac {\mu_ {l _ {t}} ^ {2}}{l _ {t} !}, \tag {138}
$$

while, for  $l_{2}\neq l_{1} + 2$

$$
\begin{array}{l} F _ {2} (\delta) = \sum_ {t \in \{1, 2 \}} \left\{\frac {1}{(l _ {t} - 1) !} \Big [ (\mu_ {l _ {t}} + (l _ {t} - 1) \mu_ {l _ {t} - 2}) ^ {2} - ((1 - \delta_ {t}) \mu_ {l _ {t}} + (l _ {t} - 1) \mu_ {l _ {t} - 2}) ^ {2} \Big ] \right. \\ + \frac {1}{(l _ {t} + 1) !} \Big [ (\mu_ {l _ {t} + 2} + (l _ {t} + 1) \mu_ {l _ {t}}) ^ {2} - (\mu_ {l _ {t} + 2} + (1 - \delta_ {t}) (l _ {t} + 1) \mu_ {l _ {t}}) ^ {2} \Big ] \Big \}, \\ \end{array}
$$

while, for  $l_{2} = l_{1} + 2$

$$
\begin{array}{l} F _ {2} (\boldsymbol {\delta}) = \frac {1}{(l _ {1} - 1) !} \left[ \left(\mu_ {l _ {1}} + (l _ {1} - 1) \mu_ {l _ {1} - 2}\right) ^ {2} - \left((1 - \delta_ {1}) \mu_ {l _ {1}} + (l _ {1} - 1) \mu_ {l _ {1} - 2}\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {1}{\left(l _ {1} + 1\right) !} \left[ \left(\mu_ {l _ {1} + 2} + \left(l _ {1} + 1\right) \mu_ {l _ {1}}\right) ^ {2} - \left(\left(1 - \delta_ {2}\right) \mu_ {l _ {1} + 2} + \left(1 - \delta_ {1}\right) \left(l _ {1} + 1\right) \mu_ {l _ {1}}\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ + \frac {1}{(l _ {2} + 1) !} \Big [ (\mu_ {l _ {2} + 2} + (l _ {2} + 1) \mu_ {l _ {2}}) ^ {2} - (\mu_ {l _ {2} + 2} + (1 - \delta_ {2}) (l _ {2} + 1) \mu_ {l _ {2}}) ^ {2} \Big ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We have from Eq. (136),

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lambda_ {\min} (\bar {U} _ {i i} ^ {(q q)}) \\ = \min  \left\{\alpha^ {(q)}, \alpha^ {(q)} + \beta^ {(q)} d _ {q} (\tau_ {i} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \right\} \\ = \min \left\{\frac {1}{d _ {q} - 1} \mathrm {T r} (\bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i i} ^ {(q q)}) - \frac {1}{d _ {q} (d _ {q} - 1) (\tau_ {i} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2}} \langle \pmb {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i i} ^ {(q q)} \pmb {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} \rangle , \frac {1}{d _ {q} (\tau_ {i} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2}} \langle \pmb {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i i} ^ {(q q)} \pmb {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} \rangle \right\}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, using Eq. (137), we get

$$
\sup  _ {i \in [ N ]} \left| \frac {d _ {q _ {\xi}}}{r _ {q _ {\xi}} ^ {2}} \lambda_ {\min } \left(\bar {U} _ {i i} ^ {(q _ {\xi} q _ {\xi})}\right) - \min  \left\{F _ {1} (\boldsymbol {\delta}), F _ {2} (\boldsymbol {\delta}) \right\} \right| \xrightarrow {\mathbb {P}} 0. \tag {139}
$$

Following the same reasoning as in [GMMM19b, Proposition 5], we can verify that under Assumption 3.(b), we have  $\nabla F_1(\mathbf{0}), \nabla F_2(\mathbf{0}) \neq \mathbf{0}$  and  $\operatorname*{det}(\nabla F_1(\mathbf{0}), \nabla F_2(\mathbf{0})) \neq 0$ . We can therefore find  $\pmb{\delta} = (\delta_{1}, \delta_{2})$  such that  $F_1(\pmb{\delta}) > 0$ ,  $F_2(\pmb{\delta}) > 0$ . Furthermore,

$$
\sup  _ {i \in [ N ]} \left| \frac {d _ {q _ {\xi}}}{r _ {q _ {\xi}} ^ {2}} \lambda_ {\max } \left(\bar {U} _ {i i} ^ {(q _ {\xi} q _ {\xi})}\right) - \max  \left\{F _ {1} (\boldsymbol {\delta}), F _ {2} (\boldsymbol {\delta}) \right\} \right| \xrightarrow {\mathbb {P}} 0. \tag {140}
$$

Similarly, we get for  $q \neq q_{\xi}$  from Eqs. (126) and (127) with the expressions (128) (recalling that  $\{\tau_i^{(q)}\}_{i \in [N]}$  concentrates on 1),

$$
\sup  _ {i \in [ N ]} \left| r _ {q} ^ {- 2} \mathrm {T r} (\bar {U} _ {i i} ^ {(q q)}) - F _ {1} (\boldsymbol {\delta}) \right| \xrightarrow {\mathbb {P}} 0,
$$

$$
\sup  _ {i \in [ N ]} \left| r _ {q} ^ {- 2} \left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i i} ^ {(q q)} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle - F _ {1} (\delta) \right| \xrightarrow {\mathbb {P}} 0, \tag {141}
$$

$$
\sup  _ {i \in [ N ]} \left| (r _ {q} r _ {q ^ {\prime}}) ^ {- 1} \langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \bar {\boldsymbol {U}} _ {i i} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})} \rangle \right| \xrightarrow {\mathbb {P}} 0.
$$

We deduce that for  $q \neq q_{\xi}$  and  $q \neq q'$ ,

$$
\sup  _ {i \in [ N ]} \left| \frac {d _ {q}}{r _ {q} ^ {2}} \lambda_ {\min } (\bar {U} _ {i i} ^ {(q q)}) - F _ {1} (\boldsymbol {\delta}) \right| \xrightarrow {\mathbb {P}} 0,
$$

$$
\sup  _ {i \in [ N ]} \left| \frac {d _ {q}}{r _ {q} ^ {2}} \lambda_ {\max } (\bar {U} _ {i i} ^ {(q q)}) - F _ {1} (\boldsymbol {\delta}) \right| \xrightarrow {\mathbb {P}} 0,
$$

$$
\sup  _ {i \in [ N ]} \left| \frac {\left(d _ {q} d _ {q ^ {\prime}}\right) ^ {1 / 2}}{r _ {q} r _ {q ^ {\prime}}} \sigma_ {\max } \left(\bar {U} _ {i i} ^ {(q q ^ {\prime})}\right) \right| \xrightarrow {\mathbb {P}} 0,
$$

which finishes to prove properties (105) and (106).

# H Proof of Theorem 7.(b): upper bound for NT model

# H.1 Preliminaries

Lemma 15. Let  $\sigma$  be an activation function that satisfies Assumptions 3.(a) and 3.(c) for some level  $\gamma >0$ . Let  $\mathcal{Q} = \overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathrm{NT}}(\gamma)$  as defined in Eq. (49). Define for integer  $\pmb{k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$  and  $\pmb {\tau},\pmb{\tau}'\in \mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} A _ {(\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}), \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} = r _ {q} ^ {2} \cdot \left[ t _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q} - 1} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q} -} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime}\right) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q} -} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\prime}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k} _ {q -}) \right. \tag {142} \\ \left. + s _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q} + 1} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}\right) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau^ {\prime}} ^ {\prime}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}) \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

with  $\pmb{k}_{q + } = (k_{1},\dots ,k_{q} + 1,\dots ,k_{Q})$  and  $\pmb{k}_{q - } = (k_{1},\dots ,k_{q} - 1,\dots ,k_{Q})$ , and

$$
s _ {d, k} = \frac {k}{2 k + d - 2}, \qquad t _ {d, k} = \frac {k + d - 2}{2 k + d - 2},
$$

with the convention  $t_{d, - 1} = 0$

Then there exists constants  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  and  $C > 0$  such that for  $d$  large enough, we have for any  $\pmb{\tau},\pmb{\tau}'\in [1 - \varepsilon_0,1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$ ,

$$
\max  _ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q}} \frac {B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})}{A _ {(\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}), \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)}} \leq C d ^ {\gamma - \kappa_ {q}}.
$$

Proof of Lemma 15. From Assumptions 3.(a) and 3.(c) and Lemma 19, there exists  $c > 0$  and  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  such that for any  $\pmb{\tau}, \pmb{\tau}' \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$  and  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}$ ,

$$
\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\prime}) \geq c \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} d ^ {k _ {q} (\kappa_ {q} - \xi)}.
$$

Hence for  $k_q > 0$ , we get  $\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{\pmb{d}}(\sigma_{\pmb{d},\tau}^{\prime})\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{\pmb{d}}(\sigma_{\pmb{d},\tau^{\prime}}^{\prime})\geq cd^{-\gamma -\xi +\kappa_q}$ , and for  $k_q = 0$ , we get  $\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{\pmb{d}}(\sigma_{\pmb{d},\tau}^{\prime})\lambda_{\pmb{k}}^{\pmb{d}}(\sigma_{\pmb{d},\tau^{\prime}}^{\prime})\geq$ $cd^{-\gamma +\xi -\eta_q - \kappa_q}$ . Carefully injecting these bounds in Eq. (142) yields the lemma.

# H.2 Proof of Theorem 7.(b): outline

In this proof, we will consider  $Q$  sub-classes of functions corresponding to the NT model restricted to the  $q$ -th sphere:

$$
\mathcal {F} _ {\mathrm {N T} ^ {(q)}} (\boldsymbol {W}) \equiv \left\{f (\boldsymbol {x}) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} \langle \boldsymbol {a} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {w} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R): \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {d _ {q}}, i \in [ N ] \right\}.
$$

We define similarly the risk associated to this sub-model

$$
R _ {\mathrm {N T} ^ {(q)}} (f _ {d}, \pmb {W}) = \inf _ {f \in \mathcal {F} _ {\mathrm {N T} ^ {(q)}} (\pmb {W})} \mathbb {E} [ (f _ {d} (\pmb {x}) - f (\pmb {x})) ^ {2} ].
$$

and approximation subspace

$$
\begin{array}{l} \overline {{\mathcal {Q}}} _ {\mathrm {N T} ^ {(q)}} (\gamma) = \Bigl \{\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\ge 0} ^ {Q} \Big | k _ {q} > 0 \mathrm {a n d} \sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} k _ {q} (\xi - \kappa_ {q}) \le \gamma + (\xi - \kappa_ {q}) \Bigr \} \\ \cup \left\{\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q} \mid k _ {q} = 0 \text {a n d} \sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} k _ {q} \left(\xi - \kappa_ {q}\right) \leq \gamma - \left(\xi - \kappa_ {q} - \eta_ {q}\right) \right\}. \tag {143} \\ \end{array}
$$

Theorem 8. Let  $\{f_d \in L^2(\mathrm{PS}_\kappa^d, \mu_d^\kappa)\}_{d \geq 1}$  be a sequence of functions. Let  $\mathbf{W} = (\mathbf{w}_i)_{i \in [N]}$  with  $(\mathbf{w}_i)_{i \in [N]} \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{D-1})$  independently. Assume  $N \geq \omega_d(d^\gamma)$  for some positive constant  $\gamma > 0$ , and  $\sigma$  satisfy Assumptions 3.(a) and 3.(c) at level  $\gamma$ . Then for any  $\varepsilon > 0$ , the following holds with high probability:

$$
0 \leq R _ {\mathrm {N T} ^ {(q)}} \left(\mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d}, \boldsymbol {W}\right) \leq \varepsilon \| \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}, \tag {144}
$$

where  $\mathcal{Q} \equiv \overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathrm{NT}^{(q)}}(\gamma)$  is defined in Equation (143).

Remark 5. From the proof of Theorem 7.(a), we have a matching lower bound for  $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NT}^{(q)}}$

We recall

$$
\overline {{Q}} _ {\mathrm {N T}} (\gamma) = \left\{\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q} \Big | \sum_ {q = 1} ^ {Q} (\xi - \kappa_ {q}) k _ {q} \leq \gamma + \left(\xi - \min  _ {q \in S (\boldsymbol {k})} \kappa_ {q}\right) \right\}.
$$

Notice that

$$
\overline {{Q}} _ {\mathrm {N T}} (\gamma) = \bigcup_ {q \in Q} \overline {{Q}} _ {\mathrm {N T} ^ {(q)}} (\gamma)
$$

Denote  $q_{\pmb{k}} = \arg \min_{q\in S(\pmb{k})}\kappa_q$ , such that  $\pmb {k}\in \overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathrm{NT}^{(q_k)}}$  for any  $\pmb {k}\in \overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathrm{NT}}(\gamma)$ . Furthermore, notice that by definition for any  $f\in L^{2}(\mathrm{PS}_{\pmb{\kappa}}^{\pmb{d}},\mu_{\pmb{d}}^{\pmb{\kappa}})$  and  $q\in [Q]$ ,

$$
R _ {\mathrm {N T}} (f, \boldsymbol {W}) \leq R _ {\mathrm {N T} ^ {(q)}} (f, \boldsymbol {W}).
$$

Let us deduce Theorem 7.(b) from Theorem 8. Denote  $\mathcal{Q} = \overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathrm{NT}}(\gamma)$ . We divide the  $N$  neurons in  $|\mathcal{Q}|$  sections of size  $N' = N / |\mathcal{Q}|$ , i.e.  $\pmb{W} = (\pmb{W}_{\pmb{k}})_{\pmb{k}\in \mathcal{Q}}$  where  $\pmb{W}_{\pmb{k}}\in \mathbb{R}^{N^{\prime}\times d}$ . For any  $\varepsilon >0$ , we get from Theorem 8 that with high probability

$$
R _ {\mathrm {N T}} \left(\mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d}, \boldsymbol {W}\right) \leq \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q}} R _ {\mathrm {N T} ^ {(q _ {\boldsymbol {k}})}} \left(\mathsf {P} _ {\boldsymbol {k}} f, \boldsymbol {W} _ {\boldsymbol {k}}\right) \leq \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q}} \varepsilon \| \mathsf {P} _ {\boldsymbol {k}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} = \varepsilon \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

# H.3 Proof of Theorem 8

# H.3.1 Properties of the limiting kernel

Similarly to the proof of Theorem 6.(b), we construct a limiting kernel which is used as a proxy to upper bound the  $\mathrm{NT}^{(q)}$  risk.

We recall the definition of  $\mathrm{PS}^{\pmb{d}} = \prod_{q\in [Q]}\mathbb{S}^{d_q - 1}(\sqrt{d_q})$ . We introduce  $\mathcal{L} = L^{2}(\mathrm{PS}^{\pmb{d}}\to \mathbb{R},\mu_{\pmb{d}})$  and  $\mathcal{L}_{d_q} = L^2 (\mathrm{PS}^{\pmb{d}}\rightarrow \mathbb{R}^{d_q},\mu_{\pmb{d}})$ . For a given  $\pmb {\theta}\in \mathbb{S}^{D - 1}(\sqrt{D})$  and associated vector  $\pmb {\tau}\in \mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$ , recall the definition of  $\sigma_{\pmb {d},\pmb{\tau}}^{\prime}\in \mathcal{L}$ :

$$
\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime} \left(\left\{\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) = \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \tau^ {(q)} \cdot \left(r _ {q} / R\right) \cdot \left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}}\right)
$$

For any  $\pmb{\tau} \in \mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$ , define the operator  $\mathbb{T}_{\pmb{\tau}}: \mathcal{L} \to \mathcal{L}_{d_q}$ , such that for any  $g \in \mathcal{L}$

$$
\mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} g (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) = \frac {r _ {q}}{\sqrt {d _ {q}}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) g (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \right].
$$

The adjoint operator  $\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{*}:\mathcal{L}_{d_q}\to \mathcal{L}$  verifies for any  $h\in \mathcal{L}_{d_q}$

$$
\mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {*} h (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) = \frac {r _ {q}}{\sqrt {d _ {q}}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\mathsf {T}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \Big [ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) h (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \Big ].
$$

We define the operator  $\mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau},\pmb{\tau}'}: \mathcal{L}_{d_q} \to \mathcal{L}_{d_q}$  as  $\mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau},\pmb{\tau}'} \equiv \mathbb{T}_{\pmb{\tau}}\mathbb{T}_{\pmb{\tau}'}^*$ . For  $h \in \mathcal{L}_{d_q}$ , we can write

$$
\mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}} h (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}} [ \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) h (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) ],
$$

where

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {2}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) \\ = \frac {r _ {q} ^ {2}}{d _ {q}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\top} \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {1}} ^ {\prime} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {2}} ^ {\prime} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$
