Define  $\mathbb{H}_{\tau,\tau'}: \mathcal{L} \to \mathcal{L}$  as  $\mathbb{H}_{\tau,\tau'} \equiv \mathbb{T}_\tau^* \mathbb{T}_{\tau'}$ . For  $g \in \mathcal{L}$ , we can write

$$
\mathbb {H} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}} g (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {1}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {2}} \left[ \mathbb {H} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {2}) g (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {2}) \right]
$$

where

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {H} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {2}) \\ = \frac {r _ {q} ^ {2}}{d _ {q}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \Big [ \sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau}} ^ {\prime} \left(\{\langle \pmb {\bar {\theta}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\pmb {x}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \pmb {\tau} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\prime} \left(\{\langle \pmb {\bar {\theta}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\pmb {x}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \Big ] \langle \overline {{\pmb {x}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\pmb {x}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \rangle . \\ \end{array}
$$

We recall the decomposition of  $\sigma_{\pmb{d},\tau}^{\prime}$  in terms of tensor product of Gegenbauer polynomials:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma^{\prime} = \sum_{\boldsymbol {k}\in \mathbb{Z}^{Q}_{\geq 0}}\lambda^{d}_{\boldsymbol{k}}(\sigma^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{\tau}})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q^{d}_{\boldsymbol{k}}\Big(\sqrt{d_{1}}\overline{x}^{(1)}_{1},\ldots ,\sqrt{d_{Q}}\overline{x}^{(Q)}_{1}\Big), \\ \lambda_ {\pmb {k}} ^ {\pmb {d}} (\sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\pmb {x}}}} \Big [ \sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime} (\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}) Q _ {\pmb {k}} ^ {\pmb {d}} \Big (\sqrt {d _ {1}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \sqrt {d _ {Q}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)} \Big) \Big ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

Following the same computations as in Lemma 12, we get

$$
\mathbb {H} _ {\pmb {\tau}, \pmb {\tau} ^ {\prime}} (\overline {{\pmb {x}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\pmb {x}}} _ {2}) = \sum_ {\pmb {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\ge 0} ^ {Q}} A _ {(\pmb {\tau}, \pmb {\tau} ^ {\prime}), \pmb {k}} ^ {(q)} Q _ {\pmb {k}} ^ {\pmb {d}} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\pmb {x}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\pmb {x}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right),
$$

where

$$
\begin{array}{l} A _ {(\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}), \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)} = r _ {q} ^ {2} \cdot \left[ t _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q} - 1} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q} -} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime}\right) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q} -} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\prime}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k} _ {q -}) \right. \tag {145} \\ \left. + s _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q} + 1} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} ^ {\prime}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau^ {\prime}} ^ {\prime}) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k} _ {q +}) \right], \\ \end{array}
$$

with  $\pmb{k}_{q+} = (k_1, \dots, k_q + 1, \dots, k_Q)$  and  $\pmb{k}_{q-} = (k_1, \dots, k_q - 1, \dots, k_Q)$ , and convention  $t_{d_q, -1} = 0$ ,

$$
s _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q}} = \frac {k _ {q}}{2 k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 2}, \qquad t _ {d _ {q}, k _ {q}} = \frac {k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 2}{2 k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 2}.
$$

Recall that for  $\pmb{k} \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$  and  $\pmb{s} \in [B(\pmb{d}, \pmb{k})]$ ,  $Y_{\pmb{k},\pmb{s}}^{\pmb{d}} = \bigotimes_{q \in [Q]} Y_{k_q s_q}^{(d_q)}$  forms an orthogonal basis of  $\mathcal{L}$  and that

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {2}} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {2} \rangle \right\} _ {\boldsymbol {q} \in [ Q ]}\right) Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {2}) \right] = \frac {1}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {1}) \delta_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}}.
$$

We deduce that

$$
\mathbb{H}_{\boldsymbol{\tau},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{\prime}}Y_{\boldsymbol {k},\boldsymbol{s}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}) = \sum_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}A_{(\boldsymbol {\tau},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{\prime}),\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{(q)}\mathbb{E}_{\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2}}\Big[Q_{\boldsymbol{k}^{\prime}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\left(\left\{\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1},\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2}\rangle \right\}_{q\in [Q]}\right)Y_{\boldsymbol {k},\boldsymbol{s}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}_{2})\Big] = \frac{A_{(\boldsymbol{\tau},\boldsymbol{\tau}^{\prime}),\boldsymbol{k}}^{(q)}}{B(\boldsymbol{d},\boldsymbol{k})} Y_{\boldsymbol {k},\boldsymbol{s}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}_{1}).
$$

Consider  $\{\mathbb{T}_{\tau}Y_{\boldsymbol{k},\boldsymbol{s}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\}_{\boldsymbol{k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q},\boldsymbol{s}\in [B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})]}$ . We have:

$$
\langle \mathbb {T} _ {\tau} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}}, \mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {\prime}} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}} = \langle Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}}, \mathbb {H} _ {\tau , \tau^ {\prime}} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}} = \frac {A _ {(\tau , \tau^ {\prime}) , \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)}}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})} \delta_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {k} ^ {\prime}} \delta_ {\boldsymbol {s}, \boldsymbol {s} ^ {\prime}},
$$

$$
\mathbb {K} _ {\tau , \tau^ {\prime}} \mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {\prime \prime}} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} = \mathbb {T} _ {\tau} \mathbb {H} _ {\tau^ {\prime}, \tau^ {\prime \prime}} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} = \frac {A _ {(\tau^ {\prime} , \tau^ {\prime \prime}) , \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)}}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})} \mathbb {T} _ {\tau} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}}.
$$

Hence  $\{\mathbb{T}_{\tau^{\prime \prime}}Y_{k,s}^{(d)}\}$  forms an orthogonal basis that diagonalizes  $\mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau}^{\prime},\pmb{\tau}^{\prime \prime}}$  (notice that  $\mathbb{T}_{\tau}Y_{k,s}^{d}$  is parallel to  $\mathbb{T}_{\tau^{\prime}}Y_{k,s}^{d}$  for any  $\pmb {\tau},\pmb{\tau}^{\prime}\in \mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$ ). Let us consider the subspace  $\mathbb{T}_{\tau}(V_{\mathcal{Q}}^{d})$ , the image of  $V_{\mathcal{Q}}^{d}$  by the operator  $\mathbb{T}_{\tau}$ . From Assumptions 3.(a) and 3.(b) and Lemma 19, there exists  $\varepsilon_0\in (0,1)$  and  $d_0$  such that for any  $\pmb {\tau},\pmb{\tau}^{\prime}\in [1 - \varepsilon_{0},1 + \varepsilon_{0}]^{Q}$  and  $d\geq d_0$ , we have  $A_{(\tau ,\tau '),\pmb{k}}^{(q)} > 0$  for any  $\pmb {k}\in \mathcal{Q}$ , and therefore the inverse  $\mathbb{K}_{\tau ,\tau '}^{-1}|_{\mathbb{T}_{\tau}(V_{\mathcal{Q}}^{d})}$  (restricted to  $\mathbb{T}_{\tau}(V_{\mathcal{Q}}^{d})$ ) is well defined.

# H.3.2 Proof of Theorem 8

Let us assume that  $\{f_d\}$  is contained in  $\bigoplus_{\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}} V_{\pmb{k}}^{\pmb{d}}$ , i.e.  $\overline{f}_d = \mathsf{P}_{\mathcal{Q}} \overline{f}_d$ .

Consider

$$
\hat {f} (\pmb {x}; \pmb {\Theta}, \pmb {a}) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} \langle \pmb {a} _ {i}, \pmb {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \pmb {\theta} _ {i}, \pmb {x} \rangle / R).
$$

Define  $\pmb{\alpha}_{\pmb{\tau}}(\overline{\pmb{\theta}}) \equiv \mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau},\pmb{\tau}}^{-1}\mathbb{T}_{\pmb{\tau}}\overline{f}_d(\overline{\pmb{\theta}})$  and choose  $\pmb{a}_i^* = N^{-1}\pmb{\alpha}_{\pmb{\tau}_i}(\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i)$ , where we denoted  $\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i = (\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i^{(q)})_{q\in [Q]}$  with  $\overline{\pmb{\theta}}_i^{(q)} = \pmb{\theta}_i^{(q)} / \tau_i^{(q)} \in \mathbb{S}^{d_q - 1}(\sqrt{d_q})$  independent of  $\pmb{\tau}_i$ .

Fix  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  as prescribed in Lemma 15 and consider the expectation over  $\mathcal{P}_{\varepsilon_0}$  of the  $\mathrm{NT}^{(q)}$  risk (in particular,  $\pmb{a}^{*} = (\pmb{a}_{1}^{*},\dots,\pmb{a}_{N}^{*})\in \mathbb{R}^{Nd_{q}}$  are well defined):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \big [ R _ {\mathrm {N T} ^ {(q)}} (f _ {d}, \boldsymbol {\Theta}) \big ] = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \Big [ \inf _ {\boldsymbol {a} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {N d _ {q}}} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ (f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x}; \boldsymbol {\Theta}, \boldsymbol {a})) ^ {2} ] \Big ] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ (f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x}; \boldsymbol {\Theta}, \boldsymbol {a} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {\Theta}))) ^ {2} \right] \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We can expand the squared loss at  $\pmb{a}$  as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x})\right) ^ {2} \right] = \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} - 2 \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left\langle \boldsymbol {a} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] \tag {146} \\ + \sum_ {i, j = 1} ^ {N} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \langle \boldsymbol {a} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle \langle \boldsymbol {a} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

The second term of the expansion (146) around  $\pmb{a}^{*}$  verifies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \langle \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} ^ {*}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] \right] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \left[ \boldsymbol {\alpha} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) ^ {\top} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \overline {{f}} _ {d} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}) \right] \right] \right] \tag {147} \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \langle \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} \overline {{f}} _ {d}, \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}} \right] \\ = \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used that for each  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$ , we have  $\mathbb{T}_{\tau}^{*}\mathbb{K}_{\tau,\tau}^{-1}\mathbb{T}_{\tau} = \mathbf{I}|_{V_{\mathcal{Q}}^d}$ .

Let us consider the third term in the expansion (146) around  $\pmb{a}^{*}$ : the non-diagonal term verifies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \sum_ {i \neq j} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \big [ \langle \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} ^ {*}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle \langle \boldsymbol {a} _ {j} ^ {*}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle / R) \big ] \right] \\ = (1 - N ^ {- 1}) \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {2}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}} \left[ \boldsymbol {\alpha} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}) ^ {\top} E _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime} \left(\left\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \right. \right. \\ \times \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\prime} \left(\left\{\left. \left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} (\boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)}) ^ {\mathsf {T}} \right] \boldsymbol {\alpha} _ {\tau^ {2}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) \Bigg ] \\ = (1 - N ^ {- 1}) \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {2}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}} \left[ \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}) ^ {\top} \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {1}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} _ {2}) \right] \\ = (1 - N ^ {- 1}) \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {2}} \left[ \langle \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}} \overline {{f}} _ {d}, \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {1}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}, \boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} ^ {2}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} \rangle_ {L ^ {2}} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

For  $\pmb{k} \in \mathcal{Q}$  and  $\pmb{s} \in [B(\pmb{d}, \pmb{k})]$  and  $\pmb{\tau}^1, \pmb{\tau}^2 \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {1}} ^ {*} \mathbb {K} _ {\tau^ {1}, \tau^ {1}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {K} _ {\tau^ {1}, \tau^ {2}} \mathbb {K} _ {\tau^ {2}, \tau^ {2}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {2}} Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} = \left(\mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {1}} ^ {*} \mathbb {K} _ {\tau^ {1}, \tau^ {1}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {1}}\right) \cdot \left(\mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {2}} ^ {*} \mathbb {K} _ {\tau^ {2}, \tau^ {2}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\tau^ {2}}\right) \cdot Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} = Y _ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}}.
$$

Hence for any  $\pmb{\tau}^1, \pmb{\tau}^2 \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$ ,  $\mathbb{T}_{\pmb{\tau}^1}^*\mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau}^1, \pmb{\tau}^1}^{-1}\mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau}^1, \pmb{\tau}^2}\mathbb{K}_{\pmb{\tau}^2, \pmb{\tau}^2}^{-1}\mathbb{T}_{\pmb{\tau}^2} = \mathbf{I}|_{V_Q^d}$ . Hence

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \sum_ {i \neq j} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \langle \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} ^ {*}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle \langle \boldsymbol {a} _ {j} ^ {*}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \right] = (1 - N ^ {- 1}) \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \right. \tag {148}
$$

The diagonal term verifies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ N ]} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left\langle \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} ^ {*}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle^ {2} \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \right] \right. \\ = N ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}} \left[ \boldsymbol {\alpha} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) ^ {\top} \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \boldsymbol {\alpha} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \right] \\ \leq N ^ {- 1} \Big [ \max _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon_ {0}, 1 + \varepsilon_ {0} ] ^ {Q}} \| \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \Big ] \cdot \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} [ \| \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

We have, from Lemma 14,

$$
\mathbb {K} _ {\pmb {\tau}, \pmb {\tau}} (\overline {{\pmb {\theta}}}, \overline {{\pmb {\theta}}}) = \alpha^ {(q)} \mathbf {I} _ {d _ {q}} + \beta^ {(q)} \overline {{\pmb {\theta}}} ^ {(q)} (\overline {{\pmb {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2}
$$

where

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left[ \begin{array}{c} \alpha^ {(q)} \\ \beta^ {(q)} \end{array} \right] = [ d _ {q} (d _ {q} - 1) (\tau^ {(q)}) ^ {4} ] ^ {- 1} \left[ \begin{array}{c c} d _ {q} (\tau^ {(q)}) ^ {4} & - (\tau^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \\ - (\tau^ {(q)}) ^ {2} & 1 \end{array} \right] \\ \times \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \langle \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \rangle \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime} (\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \ldots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}) ^ {2} ] \\ \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ (\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {\prime} (\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \ldots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}) ^ {2} ] \end{array} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence from Lemma 17 and for  $\varepsilon_0$  small enough, there exists  $C > 0$  such that for  $d$  large enough

$$
\sup _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon_ {0}, 1 + \varepsilon_ {0} ] ^ {Q}} \| \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq C \frac {r _ {q} ^ {2}}{d _ {q}} = C d ^ {\kappa_ {q}}.
$$

Furthermore

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q}} \frac {B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})}{A _ {(\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}), \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)}} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {s} \in [ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ]} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}, \boldsymbol {s}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\overline {{f}} _ {d}) ^ {2} \\ \leq \left[ \max  _ {k \in \mathcal {Q}} \frac {B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k})}{A _ {(\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}), \boldsymbol {k}} ^ {(q)}} \right] \cdot \| \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

From Lemma 15, we get

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \| \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {T} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} \overline {{f}} _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} \right] \leq C d ^ {\gamma - \kappa_ {q}} \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

Hence,

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ N ]} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left\langle \boldsymbol {a} _ {i} ^ {*}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle^ {2} \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \sigma^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) \right] \right] \leq C \frac {d ^ {\gamma}}{N} \| \mathrm {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \tag {149}
$$

Combining Eq. (147), Eq. (148) and Eq. (149), we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\Theta} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(f _ {d} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x}; \boldsymbol {\Theta}, \boldsymbol {a} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {\Theta}))\right) ^ {2} \right] \right] \\ = \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} - 2 \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} + (1 - N ^ {- 1}) \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} + N ^ {- 1} \mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} \left[ \boldsymbol {\alpha} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) ^ {\top} \mathbb {K} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}, \tau} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \boldsymbol {\alpha} _ {\boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}}) \right]\left. \right. \\ \leq C \frac {d ^ {\gamma}}{N} \| \mathsf {P} _ {\mathcal {Q}} f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

By Markov's inequality, we get for any  $\varepsilon > 0$  and  $d$  large enough,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(R _ {\mathrm {N T} ^ {(q)}} \left(f _ {d}, \boldsymbol {\Theta}\right) > \varepsilon \cdot \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\left\{R _ {\mathrm {N T} ^ {(q)}} \left(f _ {d}, \boldsymbol {\Theta}\right) > \varepsilon \cdot \| f _ {d} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} \right\} \cap \mathcal {P} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}}\right) + \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {P} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {c}\right) \\ \leq C ^ {\prime} \frac {d ^ {\gamma}}{N} + \mathbb {P} \left(\mathcal {P} _ {\varepsilon_ {0}} ^ {c}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

The assumption that  $N = \omega_d(d^\gamma)$  and Lemma 8 conclude the proof.

# Proof of Theorem 4 in the main text

Step 1. Show that  $R_{\mathrm{NN},2N}(f_*) \leq \inf_{\pmb{W} \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times d}} R_{\mathrm{NT},N}(f_*, \pmb{W})$ .

Define the neural tangent model with  $N$  neurons by  $\hat{f}_{\mathrm{NT},N}(\pmb{x};\pmb{s};\pmb{W}) = \sum_{i=1}^{N}\langle \pmb{s}_i,\pmb{x}\rangle \sigma'(\langle \pmb{w}_i,\pmb{x}\rangle)$  and the neural networks with  $N$  neurons by  $\hat{f}_{\mathrm{NN},N}(\pmb{x};\pmb{W},\pmb{b}) = \sum_{i=1}^{N}b_i\sigma(\langle \pmb{w}_i,\pmb{x}\rangle)$ . For any  $\pmb{W} \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times d}$ ,  $\pmb{s} \in \mathbb{R}^N$ , and  $\varepsilon > 0$ , we define

$$
\hat {g} _ {N} (\pmb {x}; \pmb {W}, \pmb {s}, \varepsilon) \equiv \varepsilon^ {- 1} \Big (\hat {f} _ {\mathrm {N N}, N} (\pmb {x}; \pmb {W} + \varepsilon \pmb {s}, \mathbf {1}) - \hat {f} _ {\mathrm {N N}, N} (\pmb {x}; \pmb {W}, \mathbf {1}) \Big),
$$

$$
\mathcal {E} (\pmb {x}; \pmb {W}, \pmb {s}, \varepsilon) = \hat {g} _ {N} (\pmb {x}; \pmb {W}, \pmb {s}, \varepsilon) - \hat {f} _ {\mathrm {N T}, N} (\pmb {x}; \pmb {s}; \pmb {W}).
$$

Then by Taylor expansion, there exists  $(\tilde{\boldsymbol{w}}_i)_{i\in [N]}$  such that

$$
| \mathcal {E} (\boldsymbol {x}; \boldsymbol {W}, \boldsymbol {s}, \varepsilon) | = \frac {\varepsilon}{2} \Big | \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} \langle \boldsymbol {s} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle^ {2} \sigma^ {\prime \prime} (\langle \tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle) \Big |.
$$

By the boundedness assumption of  $\sup_{x\in \mathbb{R}}|\sigma ''(x)|$  , we have

$$
\lim _ {\varepsilon \rightarrow 0 +} \| \mathcal {E} (\cdot ; \boldsymbol {W}, \boldsymbol {s}, \varepsilon) \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} = 0,
$$

and hence

$$
\lim _ {\varepsilon \rightarrow 0 +} \| f _ {*} - \hat {g} _ {N} (\cdot ; \boldsymbol {W}, \boldsymbol {s}, \varepsilon) \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} = \| f _ {*} - \hat {f} _ {\mathrm {N T}, N} (\cdot ; \boldsymbol {s}; \boldsymbol {W}) \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

Note that  $\hat{g}_N$  can be regarded as a function in  $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}^{2N}$  and  $\hat{f}_{\mathrm{NT},N} \in \mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}^N(\pmb{W})$ , this implies that

$$
R _ {\mathrm {N N}, 2 N} \left(f _ {*}\right) \leq \inf  _ {\boldsymbol {W} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {N \times d}} R _ {\mathrm {N T}, N} \left(f _ {*}, \boldsymbol {W}\right). \tag {150}
$$

Step 2. Give upper bound of  $\inf_{\pmb{W} \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times d}} R_{\mathrm{NT}, N}(f_*, \pmb{W})$ . We take  $\overline{\pmb{W}} = (\bar{\pmb{w}}_i)_{i \leq N}$  with  $\bar{\pmb{w}}_i = \pmb{U} \bar{\pmb{v}}_i$ , where  $\bar{\pmb{v}}_i \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d_0 - 1}(r^{-1}))$ , and denote  $\overline{\pmb{V}} = (\bar{\pmb{v}}_i)_{i \leq N}$ . Then we have

$$
\mathcal {G} _ {\mathrm {N T}} ^ {N} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {V}}}) \equiv \left\{f (\boldsymbol {x}) = \bar {f} (\boldsymbol {U} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x}): \bar {f} (\boldsymbol {z}) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} \langle \bar {\boldsymbol {s}} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {z} \rangle \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \bar {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {z} \rangle), \bar {\boldsymbol {s}} _ {i} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {d _ {0}}, i \leq N \right\} \subseteq \mathcal {F} _ {\mathrm {N T}} ^ {N} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {W}}}).
$$

It is easy to see that, when  $f_{*}(\pmb {x}) = \varphi (\pmb{U}^{\top}\pmb {x})$  , we have

$$
\inf _ {\hat {f} \in \mathcal {G} _ {\mathrm {N T}} ^ {N} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {V}}})} \mathbb {E} [ (f _ {*} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x})) ^ {2} ] = \inf _ {\hat {f} \in \mathcal {F} _ {\mathrm {N T}} ^ {N} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {V}}})} \mathbb {E} [ (\varphi (\boldsymbol {z}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {z})) ^ {2} ],
$$

where  $\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NT}}^N (\overline{\boldsymbol{V}})$  is the class of neural tangent model on  $\mathbb{R}^{d_0}$

$$
\mathcal {F} _ {\mathrm {N T}} ^ {N} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {V}}}) = \left\{\bar {f} (\boldsymbol {z}) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {N} \langle \bar {\boldsymbol {s}} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {z} \rangle \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \bar {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {z} \rangle): \bar {\boldsymbol {s}} _ {i} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {d _ {0}}, i \leq N \right\}.
$$

Moreover, by Theorem 3 in the main text, when  $d_0^{\ell + \delta} \leq N \leq d_0^{\ell + 1 - \delta}$  for some  $\delta > 0$  independent of  $N, d$ , we have

$$
\inf _ {\hat {f} \in \mathcal {F} _ {\mathrm {N T}} ^ {N} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {V}}})} \mathbb {E} [ (\varphi (\boldsymbol {z}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {z})) ^ {2} ] = (1 + o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1)) \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {> \ell + 1} \varphi \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2} = (1 + o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1)) \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {> \ell + 1} f _ {*} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

As a consequence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \inf  _ {\boldsymbol {W} \in \mathbb {R} ^ {N \times d}} R _ {\mathrm {N T}, N} (f _ {*}, \boldsymbol {W}) \leq \inf  _ {\hat {f} \in \mathcal {F} _ {\mathrm {N T}} ^ {N} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {W}}})} \mathbb {E} [ (f _ {*} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x})) ^ {2} ] \leq \inf  _ {\hat {f} \in \mathcal {G} _ {\mathrm {N T}} ^ {N} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {V}}})} \mathbb {E} [ (f _ {*} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {x})) ^ {2} ] \\ = \inf  _ {\hat {f} \in \mathcal {F} _ {\mathrm {N T}} ^ {N} (\overline {{\boldsymbol {V}}})} \mathbb {E} [ (\varphi (\boldsymbol {z}) - \hat {f} (\boldsymbol {z})) ^ {2} ] = (1 + o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1)) \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {> \ell + 1} f _ {*} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Combining with Eq. (150) gives that, when  $d_0^{\ell + \delta} \leq N \leq d_0^{\ell + 1 - \delta}$ , we have

$$
R _ {\mathrm {N N}, N} (f _ {*}) \leq (1 + o _ {d} (1)) \cdot \| \mathsf {P} _ {> \ell + 1} f _ {*} \| _ {L ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

Step 3. Show that  $R_{\mathrm{NN},N}(f_{*})$  is independent of  $\kappa$ .

We let  $\tilde{r} = d^{\tilde{\kappa}/2}$  and  $\mathring{r} = d^{\mathring{\tilde{\kappa}}/2}$  for some  $\tilde{\kappa} \neq \mathring{\kappa}$ . Suppose we have  $\tilde{\pmb{x}} = \pmb{U}\tilde{\pmb{z}}_1 + \pmb{U}^\perp\pmb{z}_2$  and  $\mathring{\pmb{x}} = \pmb{U}\mathring{\pmb{z}}_1 + \pmb{U}^\perp\pmb{z}_2$ , where  $\tilde{\pmb{z}}_1 \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d_0 - 1}(\tilde{r}\sqrt{d}_0))$ ,  $\mathring{\pmb{z}}_1 \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d_0 - 1}(\mathring{r}\sqrt{d}_0))$ , and  $\pmb{z}_2 \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d - d_0 - 1}(\sqrt{d - d_0}))$ . Moreover, we let  $\tilde{f}_*(\tilde{\pmb{x}}) = \varphi(\pmb{U}^\top\tilde{\pmb{x}}/\tilde{r})$  and  $\mathring{f}_*(\mathring{\pmb{x}}) = \varphi(\pmb{U}^\top\mathring{\pmb{x}}/\mathring{r})$  for some function  $\varphi: \mathbb{R}^{d_0} \to \mathbb{R}$ .

Then, for any  $\tilde{\pmb{W}} = (\tilde{\pmb{w}}_i)_{i\leq N}\subseteq \mathbb{R}^d$  and  $\tilde{\pmb{b}} = (\tilde{b}_i)_{i\leq N}\subseteq \mathbb{R}$ , there exists  $(\tilde{\pmb{v}}_{1,i})_{i\leq N}\subseteq \mathbb{R}^{d_0}$  and  $(\tilde{\pmb{v}}_{2,i})_{i\leq N}\subseteq \mathbb{R}^{d - d_0}$  such that  $\tilde{\pmb{w}}_i = \pmb {U}\tilde{\pmb{v}}_{1,i} + \pmb {U}^\perp \tilde{\pmb{v}}_{2,i}$ . We define  $\mathring{\boldsymbol{v}}_{1,i} = \tilde{r}\cdot \tilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{1,i} / \mathring{r},\mathring{\boldsymbol{w}}_i = \mathbf{U}\mathring{\boldsymbol{v}}_{1,i} + \mathbf{U}^\perp \mathring{\boldsymbol{v}}_{2,i},$ $\mathring{\boldsymbol{W}} = (\mathring{\boldsymbol{w}}_i)_{i\leq N}$ , and  $\mathring{\boldsymbol{b}} = \tilde{\boldsymbol{b}}$ . Then we have

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\tilde {\boldsymbol {x}}} \left[ \left(\mathring {f} _ {*} (\mathring {\boldsymbol {x}}) - f _ {\mathrm {N N}, N} (\mathring {\boldsymbol {x}}; \mathring {\boldsymbol {W}}, \mathring {\boldsymbol {b}})\right) ^ {2} \right] = \mathbb {E} _ {\tilde {\boldsymbol {x}}} \left[ \left(\tilde {f} _ {*} (\tilde {\boldsymbol {x}}) - f _ {\mathrm {N N}, N} (\tilde {\boldsymbol {x}}; \tilde {\boldsymbol {W}}, \tilde {\boldsymbol {b}})\right) ^ {2} \right].
$$

On the other hand, for any  $\tilde{\boldsymbol{W}} = (\tilde{\boldsymbol{w}}_i)_{i\leq N}\subseteq \mathbb{R}^d$  and  $\tilde{\pmb{b}} = (\tilde{b}_i)_{i\leq N}\subseteq \mathbb{R}$ , we can find  $\tilde{\boldsymbol{W}} = (\tilde{\boldsymbol{w}}_i)_{i\leq N}\subseteq \mathbb{R}^d$  and  $\tilde{\pmb{b}} = (\tilde{b}_i)_{i\leq N}\subseteq \mathbb{R}$  such that the above equation holds. This proves that  $R_{\mathrm{NN},N}(f_{*})$  is independent of  $\kappa$ .

# J Convergence of the Gegenbauer coefficients

In this section, we prove a string of lemmas that are used to show convergence of the Gegenbauer coefficients.

# J.1 Technical lemmas

First recall that for  $q \in [Q]$  we denote  $\tau^{(q)} \equiv \| \pmb{\theta}^{(q)} \|_2 / \sqrt{d_q}$  where  $\pmb{\theta}^{(q)}$  are the  $d_q$  coordinates of  $\pmb{\theta} \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{D-1}(\sqrt{D}))$  associated to the  $q$ -th sphere of  $\mathrm{PS}^d$ . We show that  $\tau^{(q)}$  is  $(1/d_q)$ -sub-Gaussian.

Lemma 16. There exists constants  $c, C > 0$  such that for any  $\varepsilon > 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {P} (| \tau^ {(q)} - 1 | > \varepsilon) \le C \exp (- c d _ {q} \varepsilon^ {2}).
$$

Proof of Lemma 16. Let  $\mathbf{G} \sim \mathbb{N}(0, \mathbf{I}_D)$ . We consider the random vector  $\mathbf{U} \equiv \mathbf{G} / \| \mathbf{G}\|_2 \in \mathbb{R}^D$ . We have  $\mathbf{U} \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{D-1}(1))$ . We denote  $N_{d_q} = G_1^2 + \ldots + G_{d_q}^2$  and  $N_D = G_1^2 + \ldots + G_D^2$ . The random variable  $\tau^{(q)}$  has the same distribution as

$$
\tau^ {(q)} \equiv \| \pmb {\theta} ^ {(q)} \| _ {2} / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \overset {\mathrm {d}} {=} \frac {\sqrt {N _ {d _ {q}} / d _ {q}}}{\sqrt {N _ {D} / D}}.
$$

Hence,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \tau^ {(q)} - 1 \right| > \varepsilon\right) = \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \frac {\sqrt {N _ {d _ {q}} / d _ {q}}}{\sqrt {N _ {D} / D}} - 1 \right| > \varepsilon\right) \tag {151} \\ \leq \mathbb {P} \Big (\Big | \sqrt {N _ {d _ {q}} / d _ {q}} - 1 \Big | > \varepsilon / 2 \Big) + \mathbb {P} \Big (\Big | \sqrt {N _ {D} / D} - 1 \Big | > \varepsilon / (2 + 2 \varepsilon) \Big), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used the fact that

$$
\left| a - 1 \right| \leq \frac {\varepsilon}{2} \mathrm {a n d} \left| b - 1 \right| \leq \frac {\varepsilon}{2 + 2 \varepsilon} \Rightarrow \left| \frac {a}{b} - 1 \right| \leq \varepsilon .
$$

Let us first consider  $N_{d_q}$  with  $\varepsilon \in (0,2]$ . The  $G_i^2$  are sub-exponential random variables with

$$
\mathbb {E} \Big [ e ^ {\lambda (G _ {i} ^ {2} - 1)} \Big ] \leq e ^ {2 \lambda^ {2}}, \quad \forall | \lambda | <   1 / 4.
$$

From standard sub-exponential concentration inequality, we get

$$
\mathbb {P} \left(\left| N _ {d _ {q}} / d _ {q} - 1 \right| > \varepsilon\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- d _ {q} \varepsilon \min  (1, \varepsilon) / 8\right). \tag {152}
$$

Hence, for  $\varepsilon \in (0,2]$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {P} \Big (\left| \sqrt {N _ {d _ {q}} / d _ {q}} - 1 \right| > \varepsilon / 2 \Big) \leq \mathbb {P} \Big (\left| N _ {d _ {q}} / d _ {q} - 1 \right| > \varepsilon / 2 \Big) \leq 2 \exp \Big (- d _ {q} \varepsilon^ {2} / 3 2 \Big),
$$

while for  $\varepsilon > 2$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \sqrt {N _ {d _ {q}} / d _ {q}} - 1 \right| > \varepsilon / 2\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(N _ {d _ {q}} / d _ {q} > (\varepsilon / 2 + 1) ^ {2}\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(N _ {d _ {q}} / d _ {q} - 1 > \varepsilon^ {2} / 4\right) \\ \leq \exp \Big (- d _ {q} \varepsilon^ {2} / 3 2 \Big). \\ \end{array}
$$

In the case of  $N_{D}$ , applying (152) with  $\varepsilon /(2 + 2\varepsilon)\leq 1$  shows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \sqrt {N _ {D} / D} - 1 \right| > \varepsilon / (2 + 2 \varepsilon)\right) \leq \mathbb {P} \left(\left| N _ {D} / D - 1 \right| > \varepsilon / (2 + 2 \varepsilon)\right) \\ \leq 2 \exp \Big (- D \varepsilon^ {2} / (3 2 (1 + \varepsilon) ^ {2}) \Big). \\ \end{array}
$$

Combining the above bounds into (151) yields for  $\varepsilon \geq 0$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {P} \left(\left| \tau^ {(q)} - 1 \right| > \varepsilon\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- d _ {q} \varepsilon^ {2} / 3 2\right) + 2 \exp \left(- D \varepsilon^ {2} / \left(3 2 (1 + \varepsilon) ^ {2}\right)\right) \\ \leq 4 \exp \Big (- \varepsilon^ {2} \min \Big (d _ {q}, D / (1 + \varepsilon) ^ {2} \Big) / 3 2 \Big). \\ \end{array}
$$

Notice that  $|\tau^{(q)} - 1| \leq \sqrt{D / d_q} - 1$  and we only need to consider  $\varepsilon \in [0, \sqrt{D / d_q} - 1]$ . We conclude that for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {P} (| \tau^ {(q)} - 1 | > \varepsilon) \leq 4 \exp \Big (- d _ {q} \varepsilon^ {2} / 3 2 \Big).
$$

We consider an activation function  $\sigma: \mathbb{R} \to \mathbb{R}$ . Fix  $\pmb{\theta} \in \mathbb{S}^{D-1}(\sqrt{D})$  and recall that  $\pmb{x} = (\pmb{x}^{(1)}, \dots, \pmb{x}^{(Q)}) \in \mathrm{PS}_{\kappa}^{\pmb{d}}$ . We recall that  $\pmb{x} \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathrm{PS}_{\kappa}^{\pmb{d}}) = \mu_{\pmb{d}}^{\kappa}$  while  $\overline{\pmb{x}} \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathrm{PS}^{\pmb{d}}) = \mu_{\pmb{d}}$ . Therefore, for a given  $\overline{\pmb{\theta}}$ ,  $\{\langle \overline{\pmb{\theta}}^{(q)}, \overline{\pmb{x}}^{(q)} \rangle / \sqrt{d_q}\}_{q \in [Q]} \sim \tilde{\mu}_\pmb{d}^1$  as defined in Eq. (33). Therefore we reformulate  $\sigma(\langle \pmb{\theta}, \cdot \rangle / R)$  as a function  $\sigma_{\pmb{d},\tau}$  from  $\mathrm{ps}^{\pmb{d}}$  to  $\mathbb{R}$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {\theta}, \boldsymbol {x} \right\rangle / R\right) = \sigma \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \tau^ {(q)} \cdot \left(r _ {q} / R\right) \cdot \left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}}\right) \tag {153} \\ \equiv \sigma_ {\pmb {d}, \tau} \left(\{\langle \overline {{\pmb {\theta}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\pmb {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle / \sqrt {d _ {q}} \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

We will denote in the rest of this section  $\alpha_{q} = \tau^{(q)}r_{q} / R$  for  $q = 1,\ldots ,Q$ . Notice in particular that  $\alpha_{q}\propto d^{\eta_{q} + \kappa_{q} - \xi}$  where we recall that  $\xi = \max_{q\in [Q]}\{\eta_q + \kappa_q\}$ . Without loss of generality, we will assume that the (unique) maximum is attained on the first sphere, i.e.  $\xi = \eta_{1} + \kappa_{1}$  and  $\xi >\eta_q + \kappa_q$  for  $q\geq 2$ .

Lemma 17. Assume  $\sigma$  is an activation function with  $\sigma(u)^2 \leq c_0 \exp(c_1 u^2 / 2)$  almost surely, for some constants  $c_0 > 1$  and  $c_1 < 1$ . We consider the function  $\sigma_{d,\tau} : \mathrm{ps}^d \to \mathbb{R}$  associated to  $\sigma$ , as defined in Eq. (75).

Then

(a)  $\mathbb{E}_{G\sim \mathsf{N}(0,1)}[\sigma (G)^2 ] <   \infty$  
(b) Let  $\pmb{w}^{(q)}$  be unit vectors in  $\mathbb{R}^{d_q}$  for  $q = 1,\dots ,Q$ . There exists  $\varepsilon_0 = \varepsilon_0(c_1)$  and  $d_0 = d_0(c_1)$  such that, for  $\overline{\pmb{x}} = (\pmb{x}^{(1)},\dots ,\pmb{x}^{(Q)})\sim \mu_{\pmb{d}}^{\kappa}$ ,

$$
\sup  _ {d \geq d _ {0}} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon_ {0}, 1 + \varepsilon_ {0} ] ^ {Q}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\{\langle \boldsymbol {w} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) ^ {2} \right] <   \infty . \tag {154}
$$

(c) Let  $\pmb{w}^{(q)}$  be unit vectors in  $\mathbb{R}^{d_q}$  for  $q = 1,\dots ,Q$ . Fix integers  $\pmb{k} = (k_{1},\ldots ,k_{Q})\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$ . Then for any  $\delta >0$ , there exists constants  $\varepsilon_0 = \varepsilon_0(c_1,\delta)$  and  $d_0 = d_0(c_1,\delta)$ , and a coupling of  $G\sim \mathsf{N}(0,1)$  and  $\overline{\pmb{x}} = (\pmb{x}^{(1)},\dots,\pmb{x}^{(Q)})\sim \mu_{\pmb{d}}^{\pmb{\kappa}}$  such that for any  $d\geq d_0$  and  $\pmb {\tau}\in [1 - \varepsilon_0,1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, G} \left[ \left(\left[ \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(1 - \langle \boldsymbol {w} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle^ {2} / d _ {q}\right) ^ {k _ {q}} \right] \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\{\langle \boldsymbol {w} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) - \sigma (G)\right) ^ {2} \right] <   \delta . \tag {155}
$$

Proof of Lemma 17. Part  $(a)$  is straightforward.

For part (b), recall that the probability distribution of  $\langle \pmb{w}^{(q)},\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(q)}\rangle$  when  $\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(q)}\sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d_q - 1}(\sqrt{d_q}))$  is given by

$$
\tilde {\tau} _ {d _ {q} - 1} ^ {1} (\mathrm {d} x) = C _ {d _ {q}} \left(1 - \frac {x ^ {2}}{d _ {q}}\right) ^ {\frac {d _ {q} - 3}{2}} \mathbf {1} _ {x \in [ - \sqrt {d _ {q}}, \sqrt {d _ {q}} ]} \mathrm {d} x, \tag {156}
$$

$$
C _ {d _ {q}} = \frac {\Gamma (d _ {q} - 1)}{2 ^ {d _ {q} - 2} \sqrt {d _ {q}} \Gamma ((d _ {q} - 1) / 2) ^ {2}}. \tag {157}
$$

A simple calculation shows that  $C_n \to (2\pi)^{-1/2}$  as  $n \to \infty$ , and hence  $\sup_n C_n \leq \overline{C} < \infty$ . Therefore for  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon, 1 + \varepsilon]^Q$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\left\{\langle \boldsymbol {w} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \right\} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ = \int_ {\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left[ - \sqrt {d _ {q}}, \sqrt {d _ {q}} \right]} \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) ^ {2} \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(C _ {d _ {q}} \left(1 - \frac {(\bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2}}{d _ {q}}\right) ^ {\frac {d _ {q} - 3}{2}} d \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) \\ \leq \overline {{C}} ^ {Q} \int_ {\mathbb {R} ^ {Q}} c _ {0} \exp \left(c _ {1} \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) ^ {2} / 2\right) \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(\exp \left(- \frac {d _ {q} - 3}{2 d _ {q}} (\bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2}\right) \mathrm {d} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) \\ = c _ {0} \bar {C} ^ {Q} \int_ {\mathbb {R} ^ {Q}} \exp \left(- \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {1} ^ {\intercal} \boldsymbol {M} \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {1} / 2\right) \left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \mathrm {d} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where we denoted  $\overline{\pmb{x}}_1 = (\overline{x}_1^{(1)},\dots ,\overline{x}_1^{(Q)})$  and  $M\in \mathbb{R}^{Q\times Q}$  with

$$
M _ {q q} = \frac {d _ {q} - 3}{d _ {q}} - c _ {1} ^ {2} \alpha_ {q} ^ {2}, \quad M _ {q q ^ {\prime}} = - c _ {1} \alpha_ {q} \alpha_ {q ^ {\prime}}, \quad \text {f o r} q \neq q ^ {\prime} \in [ Q ].
$$

Recalling the definition of  $\alpha_{q} = \tau^{(q)}r_{q} / R$ , with  $r_q = d^{(\eta_q + \kappa_q) / 2}$  and  $R = d^{\xi /2}(1 + o_d(1))$ . Hence for any  $\varepsilon >0$ , uniformly on  $\pmb {\tau}\in [1 - \varepsilon ,1 + \varepsilon ]^Q$ , we have  $\alpha_{q}\rightarrow 0$  for  $q\geq 2$  and  $\lim \sup_{d\to \infty}|\alpha_1 - 1|\leq \varepsilon$ . Hence if we choose  $\varepsilon_0 < c_1^{-1} - 1$ , there exists  $c > 0$  such that for  $d$  sufficiently large  $\pmb {M}\succeq c\mathbf{I}_Q$  and for any  $\pmb {\tau}\in [1 - \varepsilon_0,1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} \left(\{\langle \boldsymbol {w} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq c _ {0} \overline {{C}} ^ {Q} \int_ {\mathbb {R} ^ {Q}} \exp \left(- c \| \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {1} \| _ {2} ^ {2} / 2\right) \left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \mathrm {d} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) <   \infty .
$$

Finally, for part (c), without loss of generality we will take  $\pmb{w}^{(q)} = \pmb{e}_1^{(q)}$  so that  $\langle \pmb{w}^{(q)},\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(q)}\rangle = \overline{x}_1^{(q)}$ . From part (b), there exists  $\varepsilon >0$  and  $d_0$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sup  _ {d \geq d _ {0}} \sup  _ {\tau \in [ 1 - \varepsilon , 1 + \varepsilon ]} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, G} \left[ \left[ \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(1 - \langle \boldsymbol {w} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle^ {2} / d _ {q}\right) ^ {2 k _ {q}} \right] \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} \left(\{\langle \boldsymbol {w} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \sup _ {d \geq d _ {0}} \sup _ {\tau \in [ 1 - \varepsilon , 1 + \varepsilon ]} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \Big [ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} \left(\{\langle \boldsymbol {w} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}\right) ^ {2} \Big ] <   \infty . \\ \end{array}
$$

Consider  $\pmb{G} \sim \mathsf{N}(0, \mathbf{I}_Q)$  and an arbitrary coupling between  $\overline{\pmb{x}}$  and  $\pmb{G}$ . For any  $M > 0$  we can choose  $\sigma_M$  bounded continuous so that for any  $d$  and  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon, 1 + \varepsilon]^Q$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \boldsymbol {G}} \left[ \left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(1 - (\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} / d _ {q}\right) ^ {k _ {q}} \cdot \sigma \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) - \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(1 - G _ {q} ^ {2} / d _ {q}\right) ^ {k _ {q}} \cdot \sigma \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} G _ {q}\right)\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \boldsymbol {G}} \left[ \left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(1 - (\bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2} / d _ {q}\right) ^ {k _ {q}} \cdot \sigma_ {M} \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) - \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(1 - G _ {q} ^ {2} / d _ {q}\right) ^ {k _ {q}} \cdot \sigma_ {M} \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} G _ {q}\right)\right) ^ {2} \right] + \frac {1}{M}. \\ \end{array}
$$

It is therefore sufficient to prove the claim for  $\sigma_{M}$ . Letting  $\pmb{\xi}_{q} \sim \mathsf{N}(0, \mathbf{I}_{d_{q}-1})$  independently for each  $q \in [Q]$  and independent of  $\pmb{G}$ , we construct the coupling via

$$
\bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)} = \frac {G _ {q} \sqrt {d _ {q}}}{\sqrt {G _ {q} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {q} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}}, \quad \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} _ {- 1} ^ {(q)} = \frac {\boldsymbol {\xi} _ {q} \sqrt {d _ {q}}}{\sqrt {G _ {q} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {\xi} _ {q} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}}, \quad q \in [ Q ], \tag {159}
$$

where we set  $\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(q)} = (\overline{x}_1^{(q)},\overline{x}_{-1}^{(q)})$  for each  $q\in [Q]$ . We thus have  $(\overline{x}_1^{(q)},\overline{x}_{-1}^{(q)})\to G$  almost surely, hence the limit superior of Eq. (158) is by weak convergence bounded by  $1 / M$  for any arbitrary  $M$ . Furthermore, noticing that  $\alpha_{q}\rightarrow 0$  uniformly on  $\pmb {\tau}\in [1 - \varepsilon ,1 + \varepsilon ]^Q$  for  $q\geq 2$ , we have by bounded convergence

$$
\lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon , 1 + \varepsilon ] ^ {Q}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, \boldsymbol {G}} \left[\left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(1 - G _ {q} ^ {2} / d _ {q}\right) ^ {k _ {q}} \cdot \sigma \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} G _ {q}\right) - \sigma \left(\alpha_ {1} G _ {1}\right)\right) ^ {2} \right] = 0. \tag {160}
$$

We further have  $\lim_{(d,\tau^{(1)})\to (\infty ,1)}\alpha_1 = 1$ . Hence, by bounded convergence,

$$
\lim  _ {(d, \tau^ {(1)}) \rightarrow (\infty , 1)} \mathbb {E} _ {G _ {1}} \left[\left(\sigma \left(\alpha_ {1} G _ {1}\right) - \sigma (G _ {1})\right) ^ {2} \right] = 0. \tag {161}
$$

Combining Eq. (158) with the coupling (159) and Eqs (160) and (161) yields the result.

Consider the expansion of  $\sigma_{d,\tau}$  in terms of tensor product of Gegenbauer polynomials. We have

$$
\sigma (\langle \boldsymbol {\theta},\boldsymbol {x}\rangle /R) = \sum_{\boldsymbol {k}\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}}\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol{\tau}})B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})Q_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}\left(\{\langle \overline{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{(q)},\overline{\boldsymbol{x}}^{(q)}\rangle \}_{q\in [Q]}\right),
$$

where

$$
\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} \left(\bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sqrt {d _ {1}} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \sqrt {d _ {Q}} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) \right].
$$

with the expectation taken over  $\overline{\pmb{x}} = (\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(1)},\dots ,\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(Q)})\sim \mu_{\pmb{d}}\equiv \mathrm{Unif}(\mathrm{PS}^{\pmb{d}})$ . We will need the following lemma, which is direct consequence of Rodrigues formula, to get the scaling of the Gegenbauer coefficients of  $\sigma_{\pmb {d},\tau}$

Lemma 18. Let  $\pmb{k} = (k_{1},\dots,k_{Q})\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$  and denote  $|\pmb {k}| = k_{1} + \ldots +k_{Q}$ . Assume that the activation function  $\sigma$  is  $|\pmb {k}|$ -times weakly differentiable and denote  $\sigma^{(|\pmb {k}|)}$  its  $|\pmb {k}|$ -weak derivative. Let  $\alpha_{q} = \tau^{(q)}r_{q} / R$  for  $q = 1,\dots ,Q$ . Then

$$
\lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}) = \left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} ^ {k _ {q}}\right) \cdot R (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) \cdot \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(1 - \frac {(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2}}{d _ {q}}\right) ^ {k _ {q}}\right) \cdot \sigma^ {(| \boldsymbol {k} |)} \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) \right], \tag {162}
$$

where  $\overline{\pmb{x}}\sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathrm{PS}^d)$  and

$$
R (\pmb {d}, \pmb {k}) = \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \frac {d _ {q} ^ {k _ {q} / 2} \Gamma ((d _ {q} - 1) / 2)}{2 ^ {k _ {q}} \Gamma (k _ {q} + (d _ {q} - 1) / 2)}.
$$

Furthermore,

$$
\lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) R (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ^ {2} = \frac {1}{\boldsymbol {k} !}, \tag {163}
$$

where  $k! = k_1! \dots k_Q!$ .

Proof of Lemma 18. We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lambda_ {k} ^ {d} \left(\sigma_ {d, \tau}\right) \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} \left(\sqrt {d _ {1}} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \sqrt {d _ {Q}} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) \right] \\ = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(Q - 1)}} \left[ \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(Q)}} \left[ \sigma \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q - 1 ]} \alpha_ {q} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)} + \alpha_ {Q} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) Q _ {k _ {Q}} ^ {(d _ {Q})} \left(\sqrt {d _ {Q}} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) \right] \prod_ {q \in [ Q - 1 ]} Q _ {k _ {q}} ^ {(d _ {q})} \left(\sqrt {d _ {q}} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) \right], \tag {164} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used the definition (34) of tensor product of Gegenbauer polynomials.

Consider the integration with respect to  $\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(Q)}$ . Denote for ease of notations  $u = \alpha_{1}\overline{x}_{1}^{(1)} + \ldots + \alpha_{Q - 1}\overline{x}_{1}^{(Q - 1)}$ . We use the Rodrigues formula for the Gegenbauer polynomials (see Eq. (24)):

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(Q)} \sim \mathrm {U n i f} (\mathbb {S} ^ {d _ {Q} - 1} (\sqrt {d _ {Q}}))} \left[ \sigma \left(u + \alpha_ {Q} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) Q _ {k _ {Q}} ^ {(d _ {Q})} \left(\sqrt {d _ {Q}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) \right] \\ = \frac {\omega_ {d _ {Q} - 2}}{\omega_ {d _ {Q} - 1}} \int_ {[ - 1, 1 ]} \sigma (\alpha_ {Q} \sqrt {d _ {Q}} t + u) Q _ {k _ {Q}} ^ {(d _ {Q})} (d _ {Q} t) (1 - t ^ {2}) ^ {(d _ {Q} - 3) / 2} \mathrm {d} t \\ = (- 1 / 2) ^ {k _ {Q}} \frac {\Gamma ((d _ {Q} - 1) / 2)}{\Gamma (k _ {Q} + (d _ {Q} - 1) / 2)} \cdot \frac {\omega_ {d _ {Q} - 2}}{\omega_ {d _ {Q} - 1}} \int_ {[ - 1, 1 ]} \sigma (\alpha_ {Q} \sqrt {d _ {Q}} t + u) (\frac {\mathrm {d}}{\mathrm {d} t}) ^ {k _ {Q}} (1 - t ^ {2}) ^ {k _ {Q} + (d _ {Q} - 3) / 2} \mathrm {d} t \\ = \alpha_ {Q} ^ {k _ {Q}} 2 ^ {- k _ {Q}} d _ {Q} ^ {k _ {Q} / 2} \frac {\Gamma ((d _ {Q} - 1) / 2)}{\Gamma (k _ {Q} + (d _ {Q} - 1) / 2)} \cdot \frac {\omega_ {d _ {Q} - 2}}{\omega_ {d _ {Q} - 1}} \int_ {[ - 1, 1 ]} (1 - t ^ {2}) ^ {k _ {Q}} \sigma^ {(k _ {Q})} (\alpha_ {Q} \sqrt {d _ {Q}} t + u) (1 - t ^ {2}) ^ {(d _ {Q} - 3) / 2} \mathrm {d} t \\ = \alpha_ {Q} ^ {k _ {Q}} \frac {d _ {Q} ^ {k _ {Q} / 2} \Gamma ((d _ {Q} - 1) / 2)}{2 ^ {k _ {Q}} \Gamma (k _ {Q} + (d _ {Q} - 1) / 2)} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(Q)} \sim \operatorname {U n i f} \left(\mathbb {S} ^ {d _ {Q} - 1} (\sqrt {d _ {Q}})\right)} \left[ \left(1 - \left(\bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) ^ {2} / d _ {Q}\right) ^ {k _ {Q}} \sigma^ {(k _ {Q})} \left(\alpha_ {Q} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(Q)} + u\right) \right]. \tag {165} \\ \end{array}
$$

Iterating Eq. (165) over  $q \in [Q]$  and Eq. (164) yield the desired formula (162).

Furthermore, for each  $q \in [Q]$ ,

$$
k _ {q}! B (d _ {q}, k _ {q}) = (2 k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 2) \prod_ {j = 0} ^ {k _ {q} - 2} (j + d _ {q} - 1),
$$

$$
\frac {\Gamma ((d _ {q} - 1) / 2)}{2 ^ {k _ {q}} \Gamma (k _ {q} + (d _ {q} - 1) / 2)} = \prod_ {j = 0} ^ {k _ {q} - 1} \frac {1}{2 j + d _ {q} - 1}.
$$

Combining these two equations yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} k _ {q}! B (d _ {q}, k _ {q}) \frac {d _ {q} ^ {k _ {q}} \Gamma ((d _ {q} - 1) / 2) ^ {2}}{2 ^ {2 k _ {q}} \Gamma (k _ {q} + (d _ {q} - 1) / 2) ^ {2}} \\ = \frac {2 k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 2}{2 k _ {q} + d _ {q} - 3} \cdot \left(\prod_ {j = 0} ^ {k _ {q} - 2} \frac {j + d _ {q} - 1}{2 j + d _ {q} - 1}\right) \cdot \left(\prod_ {j = 0} ^ {k _ {q} - 1} \frac {d _ {q}}{2 j + d _ {q} - 1}\right), \tag {166} \\ \end{array}
$$

which converges to 1 when  $d_q \to \infty$ . We deduce that

$$
\lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} B (d, k) R (d, k) ^ {2} = \frac {1}{k !}.
$$

![](images/d9b3d099ec10d2b375a527fa6852a9ae1ef587990b3e35d55a405c05beff2194.jpg)

# J.2 Proof of convergence in probability of the Gegenbauer coefficients

Lemma 19. Let  $\pmb{k} = (k_{1},\dots ,k_{Q})\in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$  and denote  $|\pmb {k}| = k_{1} + \ldots +k_{Q}$ . Assume that the activation function  $\sigma$  is  $|\pmb {k}|$ -times weakly differentiable and denote  $\sigma^{(|\pmb{k}|)}$  its  $|\pmb {k}|$ -weak derivative. Assume furthermore that there exist constants  $c_{0} > 0$  and  $c_{1} < 1$  such that  $\sigma^{(|\pmb{k}|)}(u)^2\leq c_0\exp (c_1u^2 /2)$  almost surely.

Then for any  $\delta > 0$ , there exists  $\varepsilon_0 \in (0,1)$  and  $d_0$  such that for any  $d \geq d_0$  and  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$ ,

$$
\Bigg|\left(\prod_{q\in [Q]}d^{(\xi -\eta_{q} - \kappa_{q})k_{q}}\right)B(\boldsymbol {d},\boldsymbol {k})\lambda_{\boldsymbol{k}}^{\boldsymbol{d}}(\sigma_{\boldsymbol{d},\boldsymbol{\tau}})^{2} - \frac{\mu_{|\boldsymbol{k}|}(\sigma)^{2}}{\boldsymbol{k}!}\Bigg|\leq \delta .
$$

Proof of Lemma 19. From Lemma 18, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} d ^ {(\xi - \eta_ {q} - \kappa_ {q}) k _ {q}}\right) B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau}) ^ {2} \\ = \left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} ^ {2 k _ {q}} d ^ {\left(\xi - \eta_ {q} - \kappa_ {q}\right) k _ {q}}\right) \cdot \left[ B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) R (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ^ {2} \right] \tag {167} \\ \times \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(1 - \frac {(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2}}{d _ {q}}\right) ^ {k _ {q}} \cdot \sigma^ {(| \boldsymbol {k} |)} \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) \right] ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Recall  $\alpha_{q} = \tau^{(q)}r_{q} / R$  with  $r_q = d^{(\kappa_q + \eta_q) / 2}$  and  $R = d^{\xi /2}(1 + o_d(1))$ . Hence, we have

$$
\lim  _ {(d, \tau) \rightarrow (\infty , \mathbf {1})} \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} ^ {2 k _ {q}} d ^ {\left(\xi - \eta_ {q} - \kappa_ {q}\right) k _ {q}} = 1. \tag {168}
$$

Furthermore, from Lemma 18, we have

$$
\lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) R (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ^ {2} = \frac {1}{\boldsymbol {k} !}. \tag {169}
$$

We can apply Lemma 17 to the activation function  $\sigma^{(|\pmb{k}|)}$ . In particular part (c) of the lemma implies that there exists  $\varepsilon_0 \in (0,1)$  such that for  $d$  sufficiently large, we have for any  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$ ,

$$
\left| E _ {\overline {{x}}} \left[ \left(\prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \left(1 - \frac {(\bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)}) ^ {2}}{d _ {q}}\right) ^ {k _ {q}}\right) \cdot \sigma^ {(| \boldsymbol {k} |)} \left(\sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \alpha_ {q} \bar {x} _ {1} ^ {(q)}\right) \right] - \mathbb {E} _ {G} [ \sigma^ {(| \boldsymbol {k} |)} (G) ] \right| \leq \delta / 2. \tag {170}
$$

From Eq. (28), we have  $\mathbb{E}_G[\sigma^{(|\pmb{k}|)}(G)] = \mu_{|\pmb{k}|}(\sigma)$ . Combining Eqs. (168) and (170) into Eq. (167) yields the result.

Lemma 20. Let  $k$  be a non-negative integer and denote  $\pmb{k} = (k,0,\dots,0) \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$ , where we recall that without loss of generality we choose  $q = 1$  as the unique arg  $\max_{q \in [Q]} \{\eta_q + \kappa_q\}$ . Assume that the activation function  $\sigma$  verifies  $\sigma(u)^2 \leq c_0 \exp(c_1 u^2 / 2)$  almost surely for some constants  $c_0 > 0$  and  $c_1 < 1$ .

Then for any  $\delta > 0$ , there exists  $\varepsilon_0 = \varepsilon_0(c_1, \delta)$  and  $d_0 = d_0(c_1, \delta)$  such that for any  $d \geq d_0$  and  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$ ,

$$
\left| B (d _ {1}, k) \lambda_ {k} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}) ^ {2} - \frac {\mu_ {k} (\sigma) ^ {2}}{k !} \right| \leq \delta .
$$

Proof of Lemma 20. Recall the correspondence (29) between Gegenbauer and Hermite polynomials. Note for any monomial  $m_l(x) = x^k$ , we can apply Lemma 17.(c) to  $m_l(\overline{x}_1^{(q_\xi)})\sigma$  and find a coupling such that for any  $\eta > 0$ , there exists  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  and

$$
\lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {\tau} \in [ 1 - \varepsilon_ {0}, 1 + \varepsilon_ {0} ] ^ {Q}} \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}, G} \left[\left(m _ {k} \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(q _ {\xi})}\right) \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} \left(\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}\right) - m _ {k} (G) \sigma (G)\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq \eta . \tag {171}
$$

We have

$$
[ B (d _ {1}, k) k! ] ^ {1 / 2} \lambda_ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}}) = \mathbb {E} _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} [ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {\tau}} (\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}) Q _ {k} ^ {(d _ {1})} (\sqrt {d _ {1}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}) [ B (d _ {1}, k) k! ] ^ {1 / 2} ].
$$

Using the asymptotic correspondence between Gegenbauer polynomials and Hermite polynomials (29)

$$
\lim _ {d \to \infty} \mathrm {C o e f f} \{Q _ {k} ^ {(d)} (\sqrt {d} x) B (d, k) ^ {1 / 2} \} = \mathrm {C o e f f} \left\{\frac {1}{(k !) ^ {1 / 2}} \mathrm {H e} _ {k} (x) \right\},
$$

and Eq. (171), we get for any  $\delta > 0$ , there exists  $\varepsilon_0 > 0$  such that for  $d$  sufficiently large, we have for any  $\pmb{\tau} \in [1 - \varepsilon_0, 1 + \varepsilon_0]^Q$ ,

$$
\left| E _ {\overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}}} \left[ \sigma_ {\boldsymbol {d}, \tau} (\overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(Q)}) Q _ {k} ^ {(d _ {1})} (\sqrt {d _ {1}} \overline {{x}} _ {1} ^ {(1)}) [ B (d _ {1}, k) k! ] ^ {1 / 2} \right] - \mathbb {E} _ {G} [ \sigma (G) \mathrm {H e} _ {k} (G) ] \right| \leq \delta ,
$$

which concludes the proof.

# K Bound on the operator norm of Gegenbauer polynomials

Proposition 5 (Bound on the Gram matrix). Let  $\pmb{k} \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{Q}$  and denote  $\gamma = \sum_{q \in [Q]} \eta_q k_q$ . Let  $n \leq d^\gamma / e^{A_d \sqrt{\log d}}$  for any  $A_d \to \infty$ . Let  $(\overline{\pmb{x}}_i)_{i \in [n]}$  with  $\overline{\pmb{x}}_i = (\{\overline{\pmb{x}}_i^{(q)}\}_{q \in [Q]}) \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathrm{PS}^d)$  independently, and  $Q_{k_q}^{(d_q)}$  be the  $k_q$ 'th Gegenbauer polynomial with domain  $[-d_q, d_q]$ . Consider the random matrix  $\pmb{W} = (\pmb{W}_{ij})_{i,j \in [n]} \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times n}$ , with

$$
\boldsymbol {W} _ {i j} = Q _ {\boldsymbol {k}} ^ {\boldsymbol {d}} (\{\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \rangle \} _ {q \in [ Q ]}) = \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} Q _ {k _ {q}} ^ {(d _ {q})} (\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \rangle).
$$

Then we have

$$
\lim  _ {d, n \to \infty} \mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {W} - \mathbf {I} _ {n} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ] = 0.
$$

Corollary 1 (Uniform bound on the Gram matrix). Let  $n \leq d^{\gamma} / e^{A_d\sqrt{\log d}}$  for some  $\gamma > 0$  and any  $A_{d} \to \infty$ . Let  $(\overline{\pmb{x}}_i)_{i \in [N]}$  with  $\overline{\pmb{x}}_i = (\{\overline{\pmb{x}}_i^{(q)}\}_{q \in [Q]}) \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathrm{PS}^d)$  independently. Consider for any  $\pmb{k} \in \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^Q$ , the random matrix  $\pmb{W_k} = ((\pmb{W_k})_{ij})_{i,j \in [n]} \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times n}$  as defined in Proposition 5. Denote:

$$
\mathcal {Q} = \left\{\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathbb {Z} _ {\geq 0} ^ {Q} \Big | \sum_ {q \in [ Q ]} \eta_ {q} k _ {q} <   \gamma \right\}.
$$

Then we have

$$
\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {Q} ^ {c}} \mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {W} _ {\boldsymbol {k}} - \boldsymbol {I} _ {n} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ] = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1).
$$

Proof of Corollary 1. For each  $q \in [Q]$ , we consider  $\pmb{\Delta}^{(q)} = \pmb{W}_k^{(q)} - \mathbf{I}_n$  where  $\pmb{W}_k^{(q)} = ((\pmb{W}_k^{(q)})_{ij})_{i,j \in [n]}$  with

$$
\left(\boldsymbol {W} _ {k} ^ {(q)}\right) _ {i j} = Q _ {k} ^ {(d _ {q})} \left(\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {i} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} _ {j} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle\right).
$$

Then, defining  $\gamma_q \equiv \gamma / \eta_q$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \Big [ \sup  _ {k \geq 2 \gamma_ {q} + 3} \| \boldsymbol {W} _ {k} ^ {(q)} - \mathbf {I} _ {n} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \Big ] \leq \mathbb {E} \Big [ \sum_ {k \geq 2 \gamma_ {q} + 3} \| \boldsymbol {W} _ {k} ^ {(q)} - \mathbf {I} _ {n} \| _ {F} ^ {2} \Big ] \\ = n (n - 1) \sum_ {k \geq 2 \gamma_ {q} + 3} \mathbb {E} \left[ Q _ {k} ^ {(d _ {q})} \left(\left\langle \overline {{\boldsymbol {x}}} ^ {(q)}, \overline {{\boldsymbol {y}}} ^ {(q)} \right\rangle\right) ^ {2} \right] = n (n - 1) \sum_ {k \geq 2 \gamma_ {q} + 3} B \left(d _ {q}, k\right) ^ {- 1}. \\ \end{array}
$$

For  $d$  sufficiently large, there exists  $C > 0$  such that for any  $p \geq m \equiv \lceil 2\gamma_q + 3 \rceil$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {B (d _ {q} , m)}{B (d _ {q} , p)} = \prod_ {k = m} ^ {p - 1} \frac {(2 k + d _ {q} - 2)}{(2 k + d _ {q})} \cdot \frac {(k + 1)}{(k + d _ {q} - 2)} \leq \prod_ {k = m} ^ {p - 1} \frac {1}{1 + (d _ {q} - 3) / (k + 1)} \\ \leq \prod_ {k = m} ^ {p - 1} e ^ {- \frac {m + 1}{d _ {q} - 2 + m} \cdot \frac {d _ {q} - 2}{k + 1}} \leq \frac {C}{p ^ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Hence, there exists constant  $C'$ , such that for large  $d$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {k \geq 2 \gamma_ {q} + 3} B (d _ {q}, k) ^ {- 1} \leq C ^ {\prime} \cdot B (d _ {q}, m) ^ {- 1}.
$$

Recalling that  $B(d_q, m) = \Theta_d(d^{\eta_q m}) = \omega_d(d^{2\gamma})$ , and  $n = o_d(d^\gamma)$ , we deduce

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \sup  _ {k \geq 2 \gamma_ {q} + 3} \| \boldsymbol {W} _ {k} ^ {(q)} - \mathbf {I} _ {n} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \right] = o _ {d} (1). \tag {172}
$$

Let us now consider  $\pmb{\Delta} = \pmb{W}_{\pmb{k}} - \mathbf{I}_{n}$ . We will denote  $\pmb{\Delta}^{(q)} = \pmb{W}_{k_q}^{(q)} - \mathbf{I}_n$ . Then it is easy to check (recall the diagonal elements of  $\pmb{W}_{k_q}^{(d_q)}$  are equal to one) that for any  $q \in [Q]$

$$
\boldsymbol {\Delta} = \left(\bigodot_ {q ^ {\prime} \neq q} \boldsymbol {W} _ {k _ {q ^ {\prime}}} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}\right) \odot \boldsymbol {\Delta} ^ {(q)}
$$

where  $\mathbf{A} \odot \mathbf{B}$  denotes the Hadamard product, or entrywise product,  $(\mathbf{A} \odot \mathbf{B})_{i,j \in [n]} = (A_{ij}B_{ij})_{i,j \in [n]}$ . We recall the following inequality on the operator norm of Hadamard product of two matrices, with  $\mathbf{A}$  positive definite:

$$
\| \boldsymbol {A} \odot \boldsymbol {B} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left(\max _ {i j} \boldsymbol {A} _ {i j}\right) \| \boldsymbol {B} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

Hence, in particular

$$
\| \boldsymbol {\Delta} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \Big (\prod_ {q ^ {\prime} \neq q} \max  _ {i j} [ (\boldsymbol {W} _ {k _ {q ^ {\prime}}} ^ {(q ^ {\prime})}) _ {i j} ] \Big) \| \boldsymbol {\Delta} ^ {(q)} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}
$$

Consider  $\mathcal{I} = [0,2\gamma_1 + 3[\times \ldots \times [0,2\gamma_Q + 3[\cap \mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^Q]$ . Then, from Eq. (172), we get directly

$$
\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {I} ^ {c}} \| \boldsymbol {W} _ {\boldsymbol {k}} - \boldsymbol {I} _ {n} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1). \tag {173}
$$

Furthermore,  $\mathcal{I} \cap \mathcal{Q}$  is finite and from Proposition 5, we directly get

$$
\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {k} \in \mathcal {I} \cap \mathcal {Q}} \| \boldsymbol {W} _ {\boldsymbol {k}} - \boldsymbol {I} _ {n} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (1). \tag {174}
$$

Combining bounds (173) and (174) yields the result.
