# K.1 Proof of Proposition 5

The proof follows closely the proof of the uniform case presented in [GMMM19b]. For completeness, we copy here the relevant lemmas.

Step 1. Bounding operator norm by moments.

Denote  $\pmb{\Delta} = \pmb{W} - \mathbf{I}_n$ . We define for each  $q \in [Q]$ ,  $\pmb{W}_{k_q}^{(d_q)} = (Q_{k_q}^{(d_q)}(\langle \overline{\pmb{x}}_i^{(q)}, \overline{\pmb{x}}_j^{(q)} \rangle))_{ij \in [n]}$  and  $\pmb{\Delta}^{(q)} = \pmb{W}_{k_q}^{(d_q)} - \mathbf{I}_n$ . Then it is easy to check (recall the diagonal elements of  $\pmb{W}_{k_q}^{(d_q)}$  are equal to one)

$$
\boldsymbol {\Delta} = \boldsymbol {\Delta} ^ {(1)} \odot \dots \odot \boldsymbol {\Delta} ^ {(Q)},
$$

where  $\mathbf{A} \odot \mathbf{B}$  denotes the Hadamard product, or entrywise product,  $(\mathbf{A} \odot \mathbf{B})_{i,j \in [n]} = (A_{ij}B_{ij})_{i,j \in [n]}$ . For any sequence of integers  $p = p(d)$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {\Delta} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ] \leq \mathbb {E} [ \operatorname {T r} \left(\boldsymbol {\Delta} ^ {2 p}\right) ^ {1 / (2 p)} ] \leq \mathbb {E} [ \operatorname {T r} \left(\boldsymbol {\Delta} ^ {2 p}\right) ] ^ {1 / (2 p)} \tag {175}
$$

To prove the proposition, it suffices to show that for any sequence  $A_{d}\to \infty$  , we have

$$
\lim  _ {d, n \rightarrow \infty , n = O _ {d} \left(d ^ {\gamma} e ^ {- A _ {d} \sqrt {\log d}}\right)} \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} \left(\boldsymbol {\Delta} ^ {2 p}\right)\right] ^ {1 / (2 p)} = 0. \tag {176}
$$

In the following, we calculate  $\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Tr}(\pmb{\Delta}^{2p})]$ . We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} \left(\boldsymbol {\Delta} ^ {2 p}\right) \right] = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {i} = \left(i _ {1}, \dots , i _ {2 p}\right) \in [ n ] ^ {2 p}} \mathbb {E} \left[ \Delta_ {i _ {1} i _ {2}} \Delta_ {i _ {2} i _ {3}} \dots \Delta_ {i _ {2 p} i _ {1}} \right] \\ = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {i} = (i _ {1}, \dots , i _ {2 p}) \in [ n ] ^ {2 p}} \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} \mathbb {E} \left[ \Delta_ {i _ {1} i _ {2}} ^ {(q)} \Delta_ {i _ {2} i _ {3}} ^ {(q)} \dots \Delta_ {i _ {2 p} i _ {1}} ^ {(q)} \right], \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used that  $\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(q)}$  and  $\overline{\pmb{x}}^{(q^{\prime})}$  are independent for  $q\neq q^{\prime}$ .

We will denote for any  $\pmb{i} = (i_1,\dots ,i_k)\in [n]^k$ , define for each  $q\in [Q]$

$$
M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} ^ {(q)} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \mathbb {E} [ \Delta_ {i _ {1} i _ {2}} ^ {(q)} \dots \Delta_ {i _ {k} i _ {1}} ^ {(q)} ] & k \geq 2, \\ 1 & k = 1. \end{array} \right.
$$

Similarly, we define  $M_{i}$  associated to  $\pmb{\Delta}$ ,

$$
M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} = \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} ^ {(q)}.
$$

To calculate these quantities, we will apply repeatedly the following identity, which is an immediate consequence of Eq. (21). For any  $i_1, i_2, i_3$  distinct, we have

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {i _ {2}}} [ \Delta_ {i _ {1} i _ {2}} ^ {(q)} \Delta_ {i _ {2} i _ {3}} ^ {(q)} ] = \frac {1}{B (d _ {q} , k _ {q})} \Delta_ {i _ {1} i _ {3}} ^ {(q)}.
$$

Throughout the proof, we will denote by  $C, C', C''$  constants that may depend on  $k$  but not on  $p, d, n$ . The value of these constants is allowed to change from line to line.

# Step 2. The induced graph and equivalence of index sequences.

For any index sequence  $\pmb{i} = (i_1, i_2, \dots, i_{2p}) \in [n]^{2p}$ , we defined an undirected multigraph  $G_i = (V_i, E_i)$  associated to index sequence  $\pmb{i}$ . The vertex set  $V_i$  is the set of distinct elements in  $i_1, \dots, i_{2p}$ . The edge set  $E_i$  is formed as follows: for any  $j \in [2p]$  we add an edge between  $i_j$  and  $i_{j+1}$  (with convention  $2p + 1 \equiv 1$ ). Notice that this could be a self-edge, or a repeated edge:  $G_i = (V_i, E_i)$  will be -in general- a multigraph. We denote  $v(\pmb{i}) = |V_i|$  to be the number of vertices of  $G_i$ , and  $e(\pmb{i}) = |E_i|$  to be the number of edges (counting multiplicities). In particular,  $e(\pmb{i}) = k$  for  $\pmb{i} \in [n]^k$ . We define

$$
\mathcal {T} _ {\star} (p) = \left\{\boldsymbol {i} \in [ n ] ^ {2 p}: G _ {\boldsymbol {i}} \text {d o e s n o t h a v e s e l f e d g e} \right\}.
$$

For any two index sequences  $\pmb{i}_1, \pmb{i}_2$ , we say they are equivalent  $\pmb{i}_1 \asymp \pmb{i}_2$ , if the two graphs  $G_{i_1}$  and  $G_{i_2}$  are isomorphic, i.e. there exists an edge-preserving bijection of their vertices (ignoring vertex labels). We denote the equivalent class of  $\pmb{i}$  to be

$$
\mathcal {C} (\boldsymbol {i}) = \{\boldsymbol {j}: \boldsymbol {j} \asymp \boldsymbol {i} \}.
$$

We define the quotient set  $\mathcal{Q}(p)$  by

$$
\mathcal {Q} (p) = \left\{\mathcal {C} (\boldsymbol {i}): \boldsymbol {i} \in [ n ] ^ {2 p} \right\}.
$$

The following Lemma was proved in [GMMM19b, Proposition 3]

Lemma 21. The following properties hold for all sufficiently large  $n$  and  $d$ :

(a) For any equivalent index sequences  $\pmb{i} = (i_1, \dots, i_{2p}) \asymp \pmb{j} = (j_1, \dots, j_{2p})$ , we have  $M_{\pmb{i}}^{(q)} = M_{\pmb{j}}^{(q)}$ .  
(b) For any index sequence  $\pmb {i}\in [n]^{2p}\setminus \mathcal{T}_{\star}(p)$ , we have  $M_{\pmb{i}} = 0$  
(c) For any index sequence  $\pmb{i} \in \mathcal{T}_{\star}(p)$ , the degree of any vertex in  $G_{i}$  must be even.  
(d) The number of equivalent classes  $|\mathcal{Q}(p)| \leq (2p)^{2p}$ .  
(e) Recall that  $v(\pmb{i}) = |V_{\pmb{i}}|$  denotes the number of distinct elements in  $\pmb{i}$ . Then, for any  $\pmb{i} \in [n]^{2p}$ , the number of elements in the corresponding equivalence class satisfies  $|\mathcal{C}(\pmb{i})| \leq v(\pmb{i})^{v(\pmb{i})} \cdot n^{v(\pmb{i})} \leq p^p n^{v(\pmb{i})}$ .

In view of property (a) in the last lemma, given an equivalence class  $\mathcal{C} = \mathcal{C}(\pmb{i})$ , we will write  $M_{\mathcal{C}} = M_{\pmb{i}}$  for the corresponding value.

# Step 3. The skeletonization process.

For multi-graph  $G$ , we say that one of its vertices is redundant, if it has degree 2. For any index sequence  $\pmb{i} \in \mathcal{T}_{\star}(p) \subset [n]^{2p}$  (i.e. such that  $G_{i}$  does not have self-edges), we denote by  $r(\pmb{i}) \in \mathbb{N}_+$  to be the redundancy of  $\pmb{i}$ , and by  $\mathrm{sk}(\pmb{i})$  to be the skeleton of  $\pmb{i}$ , both defined by the following skeletonization process. Let  $\pmb{i}_0 = \pmb{i} \in [n]^{2p}$ . For any integer  $s \geq 0$ , if  $G_{\pmb{i}_s}$  has no redundant vertices then stop and set  $\mathrm{sk}(\pmb{i}) = \pmb{i}_s$ . Otherwise, select a redundant vertex  $\pmb{i}_s(\ell)$  arbitrarily (the  $\ell$ -th element of  $\pmb{i}_s$ ). If  $\pmb{i}_s(\ell - 1) \neq \pmb{i}_s(\ell + 1)$ , then remove  $\pmb{i}_s(\ell)$  from the graph (and from the sequence), together with its adjacent edges, and connect  $\pmb{i}_s(\ell - 1)$  and  $\pmb{i}_s(\ell + 1)$  with an edge, and denote  $\pmb{i}_{s + 1}$  to be the resulting index sequence, i.e.,  $\pmb{i}_{s + 1} = (\pmb{i}_s(1), \dots, \pmb{i}_s(\ell - 1), \pmb{i}_s(\ell + 2), \dots, \pmb{i}_s(\mathrm{end}))$ . If  $\pmb{i}_s(\ell - 1) = \pmb{i}_s(\ell + 1)$ , then remove  $\pmb{i}_s(\ell)$  from the graph (and from the sequence), together with its adjacent edges, and denote  $\pmb{i}_{s + 1}$  to be the resulting index sequence, i.e.,  $\pmb{i}_{s + 1} = (\pmb{i}_s(1), \dots, \pmb{i}_s(\ell - 1), \pmb{i}_s(\ell + 1), \pmb{i}_s(\ell + 2), \dots, \pmb{i}_s(\mathrm{end}))$ . (Here  $\ell + 1$ , and  $\ell - 1$  have to be interpreted modulo  $|\pmb{i}_s|$ , the length of  $\pmb{i}_s$ .) The redundancy of  $\pmb{i}$ , denoted by  $r(\pmb{i})$ , is the number of vertices removed during the skeletonization process.

It is easy to see that the outcome of this process is independent of the order in which we select vertices.

Lemma 22. For the above skeletonization process, the following properties hold

(a) If  $\mathbf{i} \asymp \mathbf{j} \in [n]^p$ , then  $\operatorname{sk}(\mathbf{i}) \asymp \operatorname{sk}(\mathbf{j})$ . That is, the skeletons of equivalent index sequences are equivalent.  
(b) For any  $\pmb{i} = (i_1, \dots, i_k) \in [n]^k$ , and  $q \in [Q]$ , we have

$$
M _ {\pmb {i}} ^ {(q)} = \frac {M _ {\mathrm {s k} (\pmb {i})} ^ {(q)}}{B (d _ {q} , k _ {q}) ^ {r (\pmb {i})}}.
$$

(c) For any  $\pmb{i} \in \mathcal{T}_{\star}(p) \subset [n]^{2p}$ , its skeleton is either formed by a single element, or an index sequence whose graph has the property that every vertex has degree greater or equal to 4.

Given an index sequence  $\pmb{i} \in \mathcal{T}_{\star}(p) \subset [n]^{2p}$ , we say  $\pmb{i}$  is of type 1, if  $\mathrm{sk}(\pmb{i})$  contains only one index. We say  $\pmb{i}$  is of type 2 if  $\mathrm{sk}(\pmb{i})$  is not empty (so that by Lemma 22,  $G_{\mathrm{sk}(\pmb{i})}$  can only contain vertices with degree greater or equal to 4). Denote the class of type 1 index sequence (respectively type 2 index sequence) by  $\mathcal{T}_1(p)$  (respectively  $\mathcal{T}_2(p)$ ). We also denote by  $\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_a(p)$ ,  $a \in \{1,2\}$  the set of equivalence classes of sequences in  $\mathcal{T}_a(p)$ . This definition makes sense since the equivalence class of the skeleton of a sequence only depends on the equivalence class of the sequence itself.

# Step 4. Type 1 index sequences.

Recall that  $v(\pmb{i})$  is the number of vertices in  $G_{\pmb{i}}$ , and  $e(\pmb{i})$  is the number of edges in  $G_{\pmb{i}}$  (which coincides with the length of  $\pmb{i}$ ). We consider  $\pmb{i} \in \mathcal{T}_1(p)$ . Since for  $\pmb{i} \in \mathcal{T}_1(p)$ , every edge of  $G_{\pmb{i}}$  must be at most a double edge. Indeed, if  $(u_1, u_2)$  had multiplicity larger than 2 in  $G_{\pmb{i}}$ , neither  $u_1$  nor  $u_2$  could be deleted during the skeletonization process, contradicting the assumption that  $\mathrm{sk}(\pmb{i})$  contains a single vertex. Therefore, we must have  $\min_{\pmb{i} \in \mathcal{T}_1} v(\pmb{i}) = p + 1$ . According to Lemma 22.(b), for every  $\pmb{i} \in \mathcal{T}_1(p)$ , we have

$$
M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} = \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} ^ {(q)} = \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} 1 / B (d _ {q}, k _ {q}) ^ {v (\boldsymbol {i}) - 1} = \frac {1}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k}) ^ {v (\boldsymbol {i}) - 1}}.
$$

Note by Lemma 21.(e), the number of elements in the equivalence class of  $\pmb{i}$  is  $|\mathcal{C}(\pmb {i})|\leq p^p\cdot n^{v(\pmb {i})}$ . Hence we get

$$
\max  _ {\boldsymbol {i} \in \mathcal {T} _ {1} (p)} \left[ | \mathcal {C} (\boldsymbol {i}) | | M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} | \right] \leq \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {i} \in \mathcal {T} _ {1} (p)} \left[ p ^ {p} n ^ {v (\boldsymbol {i})} / B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ^ {v (\boldsymbol {i}) - 1} \right] = p ^ {p} n ^ {p + 1} / B (\boldsymbol {d}, \boldsymbol {k}) ^ {p}. \tag {177}
$$

Therefore, denoting  $K = \sum_{q\in [Q]}\eta_qk_q$

$$
\sum_ {\boldsymbol {i} \in \mathcal {T} _ {1} (p)} M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} = \sum_ {\mathcal {C} \in \widetilde {\mathcal {T}} _ {1} (p)} | \mathcal {C} | | M _ {\mathcal {C}} | \tag {178}
$$

$$
\leq \left| \mathcal {Q} (p) \right| p ^ {p} \frac {n ^ {p + 1}}{B (\boldsymbol {d} , \boldsymbol {k}) ^ {p}} \leq (C p) ^ {3 p} n ^ {p + 1} d ^ {- K p}. \tag {179}
$$

where in the last step we used Lemma 21 and the fact that for  $q \in [Q]$ ,  $B(d_q, k_q) \geq C_0 d_q^{k_q}$  for some  $C_0 > 0$ .

# Step 5. Type 2 index sequences.

We have the following simple lemma bounding  $M_{\pmb{i}}$ , copied from [GMMM19b, Proposition 3]. This bound is useful when  $\pmb{i}$  is a skeleton.

Lemma 23. For any  $q \in [Q]$ , there exists constants  $C$  and  $d_0$  depending uniquely on  $k_q$  such that, for any  $d \geq d_0(k_q)$ , and any index sequence  $i \in [n]^m$  with  $2 \leq m \leq d_q / (4k_q)$ , we have

$$
\left| M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} ^ {(q)} \right| \leq \left(C m ^ {k _ {q}} \cdot d _ {q} ^ {- k _ {q}}\right) ^ {m / 2}.
$$

Suppose  $\pmb{i} \in \mathcal{T}_2(p)$ , and denote  $v(\pmb{i})$  to be the number of vertices in  $G_{\pmb{i}}$ . We have, for a sequence

$p = o_d(d)$  , and each  $q\in [Q]$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} ^ {(q)} \right| \stackrel {(1)} {=} \frac {\left| M _ {\mathrm {s k} (\boldsymbol {i})} ^ {(q)} \right|}{B \left(d _ {q} , k _ {q}\right) ^ {r (\boldsymbol {i})}} \\ \stackrel {(2)} {\leq} \left(\frac {C e (\operatorname {s k} (\boldsymbol {i}))}{d _ {q}}\right) ^ {k _ {q} \cdot e (\operatorname {s k} (\boldsymbol {i})) / 2} (C ^ {\prime} d _ {q}) ^ {- r (\boldsymbol {i}) k _ {q}} \\ \stackrel {(3)} {\leq} \left(\frac {C p}{d _ {q}}\right) ^ {k _ {q} \cdot e (\operatorname {s k} (i)) / 2} \left(C ^ {\prime} d _ {q}\right) ^ {- r (i) k _ {q}} \\ \stackrel {(4)} {\leq} \left(\frac {C p}{d _ {q}}\right) ^ {k _ {q} \cdot v (\operatorname {s k} (i))} \left(C ^ {\prime} d _ {q}\right) ^ {- r (i) k _ {q}} \\ \stackrel {(5)} {\leq} C ^ {v (\boldsymbol {i})} p ^ {k _ {q} \cdot v (\operatorname {s k} (\boldsymbol {i}))} d _ {q} ^ {- (v (\operatorname {s k} (\boldsymbol {i})) + r (\boldsymbol {i})) \cdot k _ {q}} \\ \stackrel {(6)} {\leq} (C p) ^ {k _ {q} \cdot v (\boldsymbol {i})} d _ {q} ^ {- v (\boldsymbol {i}) k _ {q}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Here (1) holds by Lemma 22.(b); (2) by Lemma 23, and the fact that  $\mathrm{sk}(\pmb{i})\in [n]^{e(\mathrm{sk}(\pmb{i}))}$ , together by  $B(d_q,k_q)\geq C_0d_q^{k_q}$ ; (3) because  $e(\mathrm{sk}(\pmb {i}))\leq 2p$ ; (4) by Lemma 22.(c), implying that for  $\pmb {i}\in \mathcal{T}_2(p)$ , each vertex of  $G_{\mathrm{sk}(\pmb{i})}$  has degree greater or equal to 4, so that  $v(\mathrm{sk}(\pmb {i}))\leq e(\mathrm{sk}(\pmb {i})) / 2$  (notice that for  $d\geq d_0(k_q)$  we can assume  $C_p / d_q < 1$ ). Finally, (5) follows since  $r(\pmb {i}),v(\mathrm{sk}(\pmb {i}))\leq v(\pmb {i})$ , and (6) the definition of  $r(i)$  implying  $r(\pmb {i}) = v(\pmb {i}) - v(\mathrm{sk}(\pmb {i}))$ .

Hence we get

$$
\left| M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} \right| \leq \prod_ {q \in [ Q ]} (C p) ^ {k _ {q} \cdot v (\boldsymbol {i})} d _ {q} ^ {- v (\boldsymbol {i}) k _ {q}}
$$

Note by Lemma 21.(e), the number of elements in equivalent class  $|\mathcal{C}(\boldsymbol{i})| \leq p^{v(\boldsymbol{i})} \cdot n^{v(\boldsymbol{i})}$ . Since  $v(\boldsymbol{i})$  depends only on the equivalence class of  $\boldsymbol{i}$ , we will write, with a slight abuse of notation  $v(\boldsymbol{i}) = v(\mathcal{C}(\boldsymbol{i}))$ . Notice that the number of equivalence classes with  $v(\mathcal{C}) = v$  is upper bounded by the number multi-graphs with  $v$  vertices and  $2p$  edges, which is at most  $v^{4p}$ . Denoting  $\alpha = \max_{q \in [Q]} \{1 / \eta_q\}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {i} \in \mathcal {T} _ {2} (p)} M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} \leq \sum_ {\mathcal {C} \in \widetilde {\mathcal {T}} _ {2} (p)} | \mathcal {C} | | M _ {\mathcal {C}} | (180) \\ \leq \sum_ {\mathcal {C} \in \tilde {\mathcal {T}} _ {2} (p)} \left(C p ^ {\alpha}\right) ^ {(K + 1) v (\mathcal {C})} \left(\frac {n}{d ^ {K}}\right) ^ {v (\mathcal {C})} (181) \\ \leq \sum_ {v = 2} ^ {2 p} v ^ {4 p} \left(\frac {\operatorname {C n p} ^ {\alpha (K + 1)}}{d ^ {K}}\right) ^ {v}. (182) \\ \end{array}
$$

Define  $\varepsilon = Cnp^{\alpha (K + 1)} / d^K$ . We will assume hereafter that  $p$  is selected such that

$$
2 p \leq - \log \left(\frac {C n p ^ {\alpha (K + 1)}}{d ^ {K}}\right). \tag {183}
$$

By calculus and condition (183), the function  $F(v) = v^{4p}\varepsilon^v$  is maximized over  $v\in [2,2p]$  at  $v = 2$ , whence

$$
\sum_ {i \in \mathcal {T} _ {2} (p)} M _ {i} \leq 2 p F (2) \leq C ^ {p} \left(\frac {n}{d ^ {K}}\right) ^ {2}. \tag {184}
$$

# Step 6. Concluding the proof.

Using Eqs. (179) and (184), we have, for any  $p = o_d(d)$  satisfying Eq. (183), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} \left[ \operatorname {T r} \left(\boldsymbol {\Delta} ^ {2 p}\right) \right] = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {i} = \left(i _ {1}, \dots , i _ {2 p}\right) \in [ N ] ^ {2 p}} M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} = \sum_ {\boldsymbol {i} \in \mathcal {T} _ {1} (p)} M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} + \sum_ {\boldsymbol {i} \in \mathcal {T} _ {2} (p)} M _ {\boldsymbol {i}} (185) \\ \leq (C p) ^ {3 p} \frac {n ^ {p + 1}}{d ^ {K p}} + C ^ {p} \left(\frac {n}{d ^ {K}}\right) ^ {2}. (186) \\ \end{array}
$$

Form Eq. (175), we obtain

$$
\mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {\Delta} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ] \leq C \left\{p ^ {3 / 2} n ^ {1 / (2 p)} \sqrt {\frac {n}{d ^ {K}}} + \left(\frac {n}{d ^ {K}}\right) ^ {1 / p} \right\}. \tag {187}
$$

Finally setting  $n = d^{K}e^{-2A\sqrt{\log d}}$  and  $p = (K / A)\sqrt{\log d}$ , this yields

$$
\mathbb {E} \left[ \| \boldsymbol {\Delta} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \right] \leq C \left\{e ^ {- \frac {A}{4} \sqrt {\log d}} + e ^ {- 2 A ^ {2} / K} \right\}. \tag {188}
$$

Therefore, as long as  $A \to \infty$ , we have  $\mathbb{E}[\|\pmb{\Delta}\|_{\mathrm{op}}] \to 0$ . It is immediate to check that the above choice of  $p$  satisfies the required conditions  $p = o_d(d)$  and Eq. (183) for all  $d$  large enough.

# L Technical lemmas

We put here one technical lemma that is used in the proof of Theorem 7.(a).

Lemma 24. Let  $\pmb{D} = (D^{qq'})_{q,q' \in [Q]} \in \mathbb{R}^{DN \times DN}$  be a symmetric  $Q$  by  $Q$  block matrix with  $D^{qq'} \in \mathbb{R}^{d_qN \times d_{q'}N}$ . Denote  $\pmb{B} = \pmb{D}^{-1}$ . Assume that  $\pmb{D}$  satisfies the following properties:

1. For any  $q \in [Q]$ , there exists  $c_{q}, C_{q} > 0$  such that we have with high probability

$$
0 <   \frac {r _ {q} ^ {2}}{d _ {q}} c _ {q} = d ^ {\kappa_ {q}} c _ {q} \leq \lambda_ {\min} (\pmb {D} ^ {q q}) \leq \lambda_ {\max} (\pmb {D} ^ {q q}) \leq \frac {r _ {q} ^ {2}}{d _ {q}} C _ {q} = d ^ {\kappa_ {q}} C _ {q} <   \infty ,
$$

as  $d\to \infty$

2. For any  $q \neq q' \in [Q]$ , we have  $\sigma_{\max}(\pmb{D}^{qq'}) = o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(r_q r_{q'} / \sqrt{d_q d_{q'}}) = o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d^{(\kappa_q + \kappa_{q'}) / 2})$ .

Then for any  $q \neq q' \in [Q]$ , we have

$$
\| \boldsymbol {B} ^ {q q} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = O _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\frac {d _ {q}}{r _ {q} ^ {2}}\right) = O _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (d ^ {- \kappa_ {q}}), \quad \| \boldsymbol {B} ^ {q q ^ {\prime}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\frac {\sqrt {d _ {q} d _ {q ^ {\prime}}}}{r _ {q} r _ {q ^ {\prime}}}\right) = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (d ^ {- (\kappa_ {q} + \kappa_ {q ^ {\prime}}) / 2}). \tag {189}
$$

Proof of Lemma 24. Let us show the result recursively on the integer  $Q$ . Note that the case  $Q = 1$  is direct.

Consider  $\pmb{D} = (\pmb{D}^{qq'})_{q,q' \in [Q]}$ . Denote  $\tilde{D} = D - d_Q$ ,  $\pmb{A} = (\pmb{D}^{qq'})_{q,q' \in [Q-1]}$  in  $\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{D}N \times \tilde{D}N}$  and  $\pmb{C} = [(D^{1Q})^\top, \dots, (D^{(Q-1)Q})^\top]^\top \in \mathbb{R}^{d_Q N \times \tilde{D}N}$  such that

$$
\boldsymbol {D} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {A} & \boldsymbol {C} \\ \boldsymbol {C} ^ {\intercal} & \boldsymbol {D} ^ {Q Q} \end{array} \right].
$$

Assume that  $\mathbf{A}^{-1}$  verifies Eq. (189). Denote

$$
\boldsymbol {B} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \boldsymbol {R} & \boldsymbol {T} \\ \boldsymbol {T} ^ {\intercal} & \boldsymbol {B} ^ {Q Q} \end{array} \right].
$$

From the two by two blockmatrix inversion, we have:

$$
\boldsymbol {B} ^ {Q Q} = \left(\boldsymbol {D} ^ {Q Q} - \boldsymbol {C} ^ {\intercal} \boldsymbol {A} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {C}\right) ^ {- 1},
$$

$$
\boldsymbol {T} = - \boldsymbol {A} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {C} \boldsymbol {B} ^ {Q Q}.
$$

We have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {C} ^ {\mathsf {T}} \boldsymbol {A} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {C} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \sum_ {q, q ^ {\prime} \in [ Q - 1 ]} \left\| (\boldsymbol {D} ^ {q Q}) ^ {\mathsf {T}} (\boldsymbol {A} ^ {- 1}) _ {q q ^ {\prime}} \boldsymbol {D} ^ {q ^ {\prime} Q} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ = \sum_ {q, q ^ {\prime} \in [ Q - 1 ]} o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\frac {r _ {q} r _ {Q}}{\sqrt {d _ {q} d _ {Q}}}\right) \cdot O _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\frac {\sqrt {d _ {q} d _ {q ^ {\prime}}}}{r _ {q} r _ {q ^ {\prime}}}\right) \cdot o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\frac {r _ {q ^ {\prime}} r _ {Q}}{\sqrt {d _ {q ^ {\prime}} d _ {Q}}}\right) \\ = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} (r _ {Q} ^ {2} / d _ {Q}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used in the second line the properties on  $\pmb{D}$  and our assumption on  $\pmb{A}^{-1}$ . Hence  $\pmb{D}^{QQ} - \pmb{C}^{\mathsf{T}}\pmb{A}^{-1}\pmb{C} \preceq (r_q^2 / d_q)(c_q - o_{d,\mathbb{P}}(1))\mathbf{I}$  and  $\| \pmb{B}^{QQ}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = O_{d,\mathbb{P}}(d_q / r_q^2)$ .

Furthermore, for  $q < Q$ ,

$$
\boldsymbol {B} ^ {q Q} = - \sum_ {q ^ {\prime} \in [ Q - 1 ]} (\boldsymbol {A} ^ {- 1}) _ {q q ^ {\prime}} \boldsymbol {C} _ {q ^ {\prime}} \boldsymbol {B} ^ {Q Q}
$$

Hence

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {B} ^ {q Q} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \sum_ {q ^ {\prime} \in [ Q - 1 ]} \left\| (\boldsymbol {A} ^ {- 1}) _ {q q ^ {\prime}} \boldsymbol {D} ^ {q ^ {\prime} Q} \boldsymbol {B} ^ {Q Q} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ = \sum_ {q, q ^ {\prime} \in [ Q - 1 ]} O _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\frac {\sqrt {d _ {q} d _ {q ^ {\prime}}}}{r _ {q} r _ {q ^ {\prime}}}\right) \cdot o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\frac {r _ {q} r _ {Q}}{\sqrt {d _ {q ^ {\prime}} d _ {Q}}}\right) \cdot O _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\frac {d _ {Q}}{r _ {Q} ^ {2}}\right) = o _ {d, \mathbb {P}} \left(\frac {\sqrt {d _ {q} d _ {Q}}}{r _ {q} r _ {Q}}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

which finishes the proof.

# L.1 Useful lemmas from [GMMM19b]

For completeness, we reproduce in this section lemmas proven in [GMMM19b].

Lemma 25. The number  $B(d, k)$  of independent degree- $k$  spherical harmonics on  $\mathbb{S}^{d-1}$  is non-decreasing in  $k$  for any fixed  $d \geq 2$ .

Lemma 26. For any fixed  $k$ , let  $Q_k^{(d)}(x)$  be the  $k$ -th Gegenbauer polynomial. We expand

$$
Q _ {k} ^ {(d)} (x) = \sum_ {s = 0} ^ {k} p _ {k, s} ^ {(d)} x ^ {s}.
$$

Then we have

$$
p _ {k, s} ^ {(d)} = O _ {d} (d ^ {- k / 2 - s / 2}).
$$

Lemma 27. Let  $N = o_d(d^{\ell +1})$  for a fixed integer  $\ell$ . Let  $(\pmb{w}_i)_{i\in [N]}\sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d - 1})$  independently. Then as  $d\to \infty$ , we have

$$
\max _ {i \neq j} | \langle \pmb {w} _ {i}, \pmb {w} _ {j} \rangle | = O _ {d, \mathbb {P}} ((\log d) ^ {k / 2} d ^ {- k / 2}).
$$

Proposition 6 (Bound on the Gram matrix). Let  $N \leq d^{k} / e^{A_{d}\sqrt{\log d}}$  for a fixed integer  $k$  and any  $A_{d} \to \infty$ . Let  $(\pmb{\theta}_{i})_{i \in [N]} \sim \mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d - 1}(\sqrt{d}))$  independently, and  $Q_{k}^{(d)}$  be the  $k$ th Gegenbauer polynomial with domain  $[-d,d]$ . Consider the random matrix  $\pmb{W} = (\pmb{W}_{ij})_{i,j \in [N]} \in \mathbb{R}^{N \times N}$ , with  $\pmb{W}_{ij} = Q_{k}^{(d)}(\langle \pmb{\theta}_{i}, \pmb{\theta}_{j} \rangle)$ . Then we have

$$
\lim  _ {d, N \to \infty} \mathbb {E} [ \| \boldsymbol {W} - \mathbf {I} _ {d} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ] = 0.
$$
